**Explicitly out of scope for W12** (owned by later weeks per §4, not oversights): share-as-image / `exportShoppingListImage` (W17); the AI-generated staples note (W17); Detox/Playwright E2E authoring (R16, W18 — "the core loop only stabilizes at W12," meaning W12 is the trigger condition, not the owner); full offline **write** (never, PRD §14b.4 — this week is read-side connectivity detection only, no queue, no retry-on-reconnect write path); broadening `onMenuChanged` past `createMenu` (a real, separate future slice per §17.2.9, not reopened here); a "remove one member" mutation and its `onMembershipRevoked` extension (§17.5.4, unrelated trigger, no such mutation exists); a compensating "reverse an already-committed `markMade`" mutation (§18.2.8 below — the locked undo design never commits in the first place, so no such mutation is needed this week or any week).

**A staleness recurrence found, matching §11.2.10's pattern (checked, not just assumed clean):** `mobile/lib/features/menu/state/current_menu_controller.dart`'s class doc still says *"No live-push subscription wiring (unlike `CurrentHouseholdController`'s `watchHouseholdChanges`): W9 has no `onMenuChanged` (deferred, E2E_MVP_PLAN.md §15.2 D1)."* This became wrong the moment W11 S2 shipped `onMenuChanged` — the controller's actual behavior (no subscription consumed) is still correct, but the comment's *reason* ("W9 has no `onMenuChanged`") is now false; `onMenuChanged` exists and is simply not wired to this controller (an accepted gap, §17.2.9/§17.5.5, not a missing feature). Unlike §11.2.10's three comments, this one does not need code changes to fix — only the doc string — and is folded into S7's docs pass rather than given its own slice. No other `grep -rn "W12"` hit across `mobile/lib`/`api/src`/`infra/` asserts something already shipped (the three real hits — `appsync_websocket_link.dart`, `household.ts`, `pantryUnits.ts` — are all genuine, still-accurate forward-references, not stale claims).

### 18.2 Design

#### 18.2.1 D1 — `onShoppingListChanged` ships in W12, cashing in D1's own W11 promise; `markPurchased` widens to `ShoppingList!` to attach to it

W11's D1 (§17.2.1) widened `haveIt`'s return type specifically "so this mutation can attach to a future `onShoppingListChanged` subscription without a second return-type change." Re-reading `shared/schema.graphql` directly (not SD's sketch, which predates real shipped shape) confirms that promise was never cashed: no `onShoppingListChanged` field exists anywhere in the shipped SDL, only a doc comment on `haveIt` referencing it as future work. Leaving it unbuilt a second week running would repeat the exact "deferred without a week assigned" pattern §16.2.9/D9 and §17.2.9/D9-carryover both explicitly named as a problem when it happened to `onMenuChanged` — except here there is no ambiguity about which week owns it: W12 is the last Phase 3 week, and Phase 3's own DoD requires the full core loop's real-time sync story to be closed by its end.

**Locked, per direct precedent, not open:** `Subscription.onShoppingListChanged(householdId: ID!): ShoppingList` ships in W12, `@aws_subscribe`d to `["generateShoppingList", "regenerateShoppingList", "haveIt", "markPurchased"]` — the first three already return `ShoppingList!` (no change needed); `markPurchased` widens from SD's sketched `ShoppingListItem!` to `ShoppingList!`, the identical D1/W8-D4 precedent (same reasoning: §11.2.12's already-locked "every push means refetch, no event-type discriminator" contract means the payload shape doesn't matter to any consumer, and the affected item remains reachable via `ShoppingList.items`). Same subscribe-time Lambda-resolver authorization pattern as every other subscription field (`requireHouseholdMember`, reused unchanged). This closes forward-reference #10 and #13 above in the same slice that needs the widening anyway (S3, §18.3).

#### 18.2.2 D2 — `markMade`'s pantry deduction reuses W11's exact matching/conversion pipeline AND its staples-exclusion rule; no new fuzzy-matching, unit-conversion, or staple-vocabulary code is written

The forward-reference this plan was handed (§11.2.4's "W12 pantry deduction can't match 'kg' against 'Kg'") predates W11 shipping exactly this: `api/src/domain/shoppingListGeneration.ts` exports `namesMatch`/`normalizeIngredientName`/`unitsCompatible`, and `api/src/domain/unitConversion.ts` exports `convertQuantity`, both already consumed on the write path by `pantryRepository.ts`'s `upsertOrIncrementPantryItemForHaveIt` (W11 S3). **Locked: `markMade`'s deduction reuses all four functions directly, unmodified — no second matcher, no second conversion table.**

**Locked per O2 (§18.7 O2, reverses this draft's own drafting-pass recommendation of "deduct everything with a match"):** before matching against pantry, `computeDeductionLines` first filters the recipe's ingredients through `shoppingListGeneration.ts`'s own exported `isStapleExcluded` predicate — the exact same unit/flag/category staple rule `aggregateIngredients` already applies before a recipe's ingredients are even considered for the shopping list. A staple-flagged ingredient, a staple-category ingredient (`spice`/`masala`/`salt`/`oil`, `STAPLE_CATEGORIES`), or a staple-unit ingredient (`tsp`/`tbsp`/`pinch`/`to_taste`, `STAPLE_UNITS`) is never matched against the pantry and never decremented, even when a real matching pantry row exists for it. This is a straight reuse of `isStapleExcluded` unmodified — not a second, deduction-specific staple predicate — so a household adding an ingredient to `STAPLE_CATEGORIES` in a future edit gets the identical exclusion on both the shopping-list-visibility side and the pantry-deduction side automatically, with no drift possible between the two.

**Concrete shape, new pure module `api/src/domain/pantryDeduction.ts`:** `computeDeductionLines(recipeIngredients, servingsOverride, pantryItems): DeductionLine[]`, where a `DeductionLine` is `{ pantryItemId: string; newQuantity: number } | { pantryItemId: null }` (the second shape meaning "excluded as a staple, or no matching pantry row — either way, nothing to decrement" — see D3 below for the exact zero-floor rule and why a no-match is a no-op rather than an insert, unlike `haveIt`'s own no-match branch). Mirrors `aggregateIngredients`'s own `servingsOverride` scaling (a `MenuItem`'s `servings_override`, when present, scales that recipe's ingredient quantities the same way S1's W11 aggregation already does before matching) and reuses `isStapleExcluded`/`namesMatch`/`unitsCompatible`/`convertQuantity` for the filter-then-match itself, in that order (staple filter first, then matching — an ingredient never reaches the matcher at all if `isStapleExcluded` already dropped it, the identical order `aggregateIngredients` uses) — genuinely new code is only the "which pantry row, if any, does this non-staple recipe ingredient decrement, and by how much converted into THAT row's unit, floored at zero" loop, the mirror image of `upsertOrIncrementPantryItemForHaveIt`'s "which pantry row does this shopping-list item increment" loop.

#### 18.2.3 D3 — deduction never inserts a new pantry row; a matched row's post-deduction quantity floors at zero, reusing `subtractPantry`'s own "never negative" idiom

Unlike `haveIt` (a "the user is telling us this exact thing entered the pantry" write, where "no match" correctly means "create a new pantry row"), `markMade`'s deduction is inferring consumption from a recipe the household may or may not track every ingredient of in its pantry at all. **Locked:** a recipe ingredient with no matching pantry row (including one dropped by D2's staple filter before matching was even attempted) is skipped — deduction never fabricates a negative-stock row for an ingredient the household never added, and never touches an excluded staple's row even if one exists.

**Locked per O1 (§18.7 O1, reverses this draft's own drafting-pass recommendation of "allow negative"):** a matched pantry row's post-deduction quantity **clamps at zero, never negative**. `shoppingListGeneration.ts`'s own `subtractPantry` already establishes this exact "never shown as negative" convention on the shopping-list side — its own test suite names the behavior directly (`shoppingListGeneration.test.ts`: *"clamps a zero-or-negative post-subtraction result to zero and drops the line, never shown as 'buy -2'"*). `computeDeductionLines` reuses the identical idiom, adapted for a stored quantity rather than a dropped line (the pantry row itself must still exist afterward — it doesn't disappear the way an over-covered shopping-list line does): `newQuantity = Math.max(0, pantryItem.quantity - convertedDecrement)`. There is no separate "you're short" negative-quantity UI signal this week or any week under this design — a pantry row that would have gone negative simply reads `0`, the same floor `subtractPantry`'s own consumers already expect everywhere else in this codebase's pantry-quantity story. (A household that wants to know it under-stocked something still has `lowThreshold`-based low-stock nudges, §11.2.6, and the always-available manual `updatePantryItem` correction — unaffected by this decision.)

#### 18.2.4 D4 — `markMade` does NOT attach to `onMenuChanged`, following D9-carryover's own precedent exactly

SD's pre-build sketch lists `markMade` among the mutations `onMenuChanged` might cover; W11's D9-carryover (§17.2.9) already worked through this exact tension for `addMenuItem`/`removeMenuItem`/`autoFillWeek` and rejected all three for the same structural reason: `markMade`'s locked return type is `MenuItem!` (SD §6.1, unchanged — no forward-reference asks for a widening here), which cannot attach to a subscription field typed `Menu` without a D1-style widening, and `markMade`'s direct return value (the updated `MenuItem`, carrying the new `madeAt` timestamp) is actively consumed by its own caller today (the mobile screen that just called it needs to render the "made" state immediately) — widening it to `Menu!` would either drop that directly-consumed shape or force a second round-trip, the identical cost D9-carryover already declined to pay for the other three mutations. **Locked: `markMade` is not attached to `onMenuChanged`, and a household member marking a recipe made on one device produces no push to a second device this week** — the same accepted refetch-on-route-entry/foreground staleness gap already documented for `addMenuItem`/`removeMenuItem`/`autoFillWeek` (§17.5.5), now explicitly extended to include `markMade` rather than silently inheriting it. Broadening `onMenuChanged`'s coverage remains the single real, separate future slice §17.2.9 already named — not reopened piecemeal mutation-by-mutation each week it comes up.

#### 18.2.5 D5 — `markPurchased` takes no `quantity` argument, following SD's own already-locked shape

SD §6.1 sketches `markPurchased(itemId: ID!): ShoppingListItem!` — deliberately no `quantity` parameter, unlike `haveIt(itemId: ID!, quantity: Float!)`. Re-reading PRD §6/§7.1 confirms this is intentional, not an oversight the pre-decision draft should "fix": "Have it" is described as *"prompts for quantity, then moves the item to pantry"* (PRD line 209) while "check off items when bought" (line 211) carries no such prompt anywhere in the PRD. **Locked: `markPurchased(itemId: ID!): ShoppingList!` takes no quantity argument** — the pantry write always uses the shopping-list item's own already-known `quantity`/`unit` (the same fields `haveIt` would otherwise have needed a user to re-confirm), because a purchase is closing out something the list already specified, not a fresh manual entry. This keeps "bought" a true single-tap/checkbox action (relevant to §18.2.6's UI call and §18.2.5's own contrast with O5's now-locked "single tap" answer for `markMade`, §18.7) rather than silently growing a second quantity-prompt sheet PRD never asked for.

#### 18.2.6 D6 — "Bought" is a leading checkbox tap on `ChecklistItem`, not a second swipe direction

`checklist_item.dart`'s `Dismissible` already reserves `DismissDirection.startToEnd` for "Have it" (S7, W11) — its own class doc is explicit that swiping calls up a confirmation sheet, never the mutation directly. Two competing options for "bought": reuse the opposite swipe direction (`endToStart`) for a second, differently-colored reveal, or add an explicit leading checkbox that toggles independently of any swipe gesture. **Locked: a leading checkbox** — PRD's own language for this flow is literally "check off items when bought" (line 211, not "swipe"), and a second swipe direction on the same row risks exactly the "two different swipe gestures mean two different consequential actions" confusion this design system's own accessibility rule (`p_button.dart`'s doc, cited in `checklist_item.dart` itself) already warns never to rely on gesture-direction-alone semantics for. The checkbox sits to the left of the row's existing content (name/quantity), tapping it calls `markPurchased(itemId)` directly (no confirmation sheet — D5's no-quantity-prompt design already makes this safe as a single tap, the same lower-friction-by-design posture O5 below locks for `markMade`) and shows a brief optimistic-checked state that reverts on failure (mirroring `have_it_quantity_sheet.dart`'s own "honest UI feedback, never a silent no-op" convention, S6/S11's shared rule).

#### 18.2.7 D7 — the offline banner shows only on `disconnected`, debounced 4 seconds (O4, locked) to avoid flicker during a normal reconnect blip

`AppSyncSubscriptionClient.connectionState` (§14.2.12/D10) exposes exactly three states — `disconnected`, `connecting`, `connected`. A transient blip (backgrounding for a few seconds, a brief network hiccup the `ReconnectPolicy` ladder's first rung, 1s, recovers from) would otherwise flash the banner on and off distractingly if it reacted to `disconnected` instantly. **Locked:** the banner shows only when `connectionState.value == ConnectionState.disconnected` (never for `connecting` — a `connecting` state most often IS the ladder already working, and showing a banner for it would read as "something is wrong" during exactly the moment the app is fixing itself), and hides immediately (no debounce needed in this direction) the instant `connectionState.value == ConnectionState.connected`.

**Locked per O4 (§18.7 O4, no reversal — founder confirmed the drafting pass's own recommendation, and picked the midpoint of its 3–5s range):** the debounce is a single named Dart constant, `offlineBannerDebounce = Duration(seconds: 4)`, defined alongside `OfflineBanner`'s own widget code (not left as a range for engineering to interpret) — long enough to skip the `ReconnectPolicy` ladder's own 1s-then-2s early rungs (a transient blip that self-heals inside ~4s shouldn't alarm the user at all), short enough that a real disconnect during active use (someone mid-swipe-to-have-it, mid-checking-off-bought) is flagged well before they'd notice something silently failed to sync.

#### 18.2.8 D8 — `markMade` is a single tap with an undo snackbar (O5, locked); undo is a client-side deferred commit, never a compensating server-side reversal

PRD's own "cook of the day opens today's recipe → cooks → marks 'made'" framing (line 122) implies a fast, one-handed, mid-kitchen action, but a stray tap while holding a phone with wet/floury hands is a real, mundane failure mode this app's own kitchen-use context invites — and `markMade`'s side effect (pantry deduction, D2/D3) is the one core-loop action this week with a real, non-trivial-to-manually-fix consequence if tapped by accident. **Locked per O5 (§18.7 O5, no reversal — founder confirmed the drafting pass's own recommendation): a single tap, no confirmation dialog, paired with a "Marked as made · Undo" snackbar.** The founder explicitly asked for this to be designed for real rather than left as a hand-wave, since a genuine "undo" needs a compensating mechanism the drafting pass had not worked out — designed here:

**The undo mechanism is a client-side deferred commit, not a commit-then-reverse.** Tapping "Mark as made" does two things immediately and only on-device: (1) shows the optimistic UI — the item renders in its made/checked state right away, matching this codebase's own optimistic-update convention (`ChecklistItem`'s own swipe/checkbox handling, D6 above) — and (2) shows the undo snackbar. Neither of these calls `markMade` yet. The actual mutation call is scheduled via a cancellable delayed future (e.g. `Future.delayed(pendingWindow, () => repository.markMade(menuItemId))`, `pendingWindow` matched to the snackbar's own visible duration) and is only actually sent to the server once that delay elapses **without** the user tapping Undo. Tapping Undo within the window cancels the pending future outright — nothing was ever sent to the API, so there is no `made_at` write to unset and no pantry deduction to reverse, and consequently **no new "undo `markMade`" mutation, no compensating-transaction design, and no server-side reversal endpoint are needed this week or ever** for this flow. This is deliberately the cheaper of the two designs the drafting pass's own O5 framing considered ((a) fire-and-forget with a real server-side undo, vs. (b) defer-then-commit) — (b) is what's locked, because it fully sidesteps the "how do we reverse a pantry deduction that already happened, possibly after the row was touched again by something else" problem rather than solving it.

**Concrete shape, new small helper `mobile/lib/features/menu/state/pending_mark_made_action.dart`:** a `PendingMarkMadeAction` value (or a tiny controller wrapping one) holding the `menuItemId`, the scheduled `Timer`/cancellable `Future`, and the optimistic pre-tap `MenuItem` snapshot the UI reverts to if something goes wrong before the deferred call fires (e.g. the user backgrounds and returns, per the mobile lifecycle contract below). `CurrentMenuController` exposes `markMadeWithUndo(menuItemId)` — starts the pending action and updates local state to show the item as made immediately — and `undoMarkMade(menuItemId)` — cancels the pending timer and reverts local state to the pre-tap snapshot, a pure client-side operation with zero network calls. Only once the timer fires does the controller call the real `markMade` mutation and refresh from the server response (matching D4's "explicit refresh, no push" contract for this mutation); if that real call fails (network drop, `CONFLICT`, etc.) the UI reverts to the pre-tap snapshot and surfaces a visible error, the same "never a silent no-op" discipline this codebase already applies everywhere else (D6 above, `have_it_quantity_sheet.dart`).

**One explicit constraint this design accepts:** only one `PendingMarkMadeAction` is in flight at a time per menu item (a second tap on an already-optimistically-made item is a no-op — the item already renders as made, matching S5's own "an already-made item renders visibly different, not a tappable affordance" rule below), and the pending action must survive a normal snackbar-duration app lifecycle (foreground the whole time) but does **not** need to survive the app being killed mid-window — if the process dies before the deferred call fires, the tap is silently lost (the item was never actually marked made server-side, matching "nothing was sent" exactly) rather than partially applied; this is an accepted, documented trade the same size as D2/§18.5.1's "best-effort, user can override" posture, not a new gap this design pretends doesn't exist.

### 18.3 Slice breakdown

#### S1 — Pantry-deduction domain module (pure, no resolver)

- **Delivers:** `api/src/domain/pantryDeduction.ts` — `computeDeductionLines(ingredients, servingsOverride, pantryItems)` per D2/D3 (§18.2.2/§18.2.3), staple-filtering via `isStapleExcluded` (reused, unmodified) before matching, and zero-floored via `Math.max(0, ...)` on every matched decrement (O1, locked); `api/src/domain/defaultExpiry.ts` — `defaultExpiryDaysForCategory(category): number | null` sourced from a new named constant map, `DEFAULT_EXPIRY_DAYS_BY_CATEGORY`, over `pantryCategories.ts`'s real `KNOWN_PANTRY_CATEGORIES` (mirrors D3/W11's "source the table from the real enum, not a PRD guess" convention) — **locked per O3 (§18.7 O3, no reversal), values confirmed to match the real enum exactly** (`dal`, `spice`, `dairy`, `produce`, `dry_goods`, `grain`, `oil`, `condiment`, `frozen`, `other` — all ten of `KNOWN_PANTRY_CATEGORIES`, no invented category, none dropped): `dairy: 5`, `produce: 7`, `frozen: 90`, `oil: 180`, `condiment: 180`, `dal: 180`, `grain: 180`, `dry_goods: 270`, `spice: 365`, `other: null` (no default — matches `pantryUnits.ts`/`pantryCategories.ts`'s own "pass unrecognized values through, never invent data for them" posture).
- **Files:** `api/src/domain/pantryDeduction.ts` (+ test); `api/src/domain/defaultExpiry.ts` (+ test).
- **Depends on:** nothing — starts immediately; no longer blocked on anything, since O1/O2/O3 are all answered.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Low — almost entirely composition of W11's already-tested `isStapleExcluded`/`namesMatch`/`unitsCompatible`/`convertQuantity`, per D2.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips (no I/O).
- **RED tests:** a recipe ingredient with an exact name+unit pantry match produces a decrement line at the recipe's required quantity; a same-family cross-unit match (mirroring W11 S1's own `tbsp`/`tsp` case) converts and decrements correctly; a recipe ingredient with no matching pantry row produces a no-op line, never a fabricated new row (D3); a cross-family/unrecognized-unit "match" — name matches, unit doesn't convert — also no-ops rather than incorrectly decrementing (mirrors `haveIt`'s own §17.5.3-accepted narrower-correctness-cost pattern, applied to the subtract direction); a staple-flagged ingredient (`isStaple: true`) with a real, otherwise-matching pantry row is NOT decremented (O2, direct regression test — the pantry row's quantity is asserted unchanged); a staple-category ingredient (e.g. `category: 'oil'`) with a real pantry match is likewise NOT decremented; a staple-unit ingredient (e.g. `1 tsp`) with a real pantry match is likewise NOT decremented; a non-staple ingredient sharing a recipe with excluded staples is decremented normally (the filter is per-ingredient, not per-recipe); `servings_override` on the `MenuItem` scales the recipe's ingredient quantities before matching, identical to S1/W11's own aggregation scaling; a decrement that would take a matched row below zero clamps its `newQuantity` to exactly `0`, never negative (O1, direct regression test, mirroring `shoppingListGeneration.test.ts`'s own "clamps ... to zero" test naming); `defaultExpiryDaysForCategory` returns the configured day count for a known category and `null` (no default) for `other`/unrecognized categories.

#### S2 — `Mutation.markMade(menuItemId)` + repository + SDL

- **Delivers:** SDL `markMade(menuItemId: ID!): MenuItem!` (SD's own already-locked shape, zero widening — not attached to any subscription per D4); `menuRepository.ts` — `setMenuItemMadeAt(client, menuItemId)`: `UPDATE menu_items SET made_at = now() WHERE id = $1 AND made_at IS NULL RETURNING *`, rejecting a second call on an already-`made_at`-set item with `CONFLICT` (mirrors `haveIt`'s own already-purchased rejection, W11 S3); `pantryRepository.ts` — `applyDeductionLines(client, deductionLines)` (the write half of S1's pure `computeDeductionLines`, one `UPDATE pantry_items SET quantity = $2 WHERE id = $1` per non-noop line, `$2` already zero-floored by S1 per O1), reusing `lockPantryForHousehold`'s advisory-lock pattern before reading pantry state, for the identical concurrent-double-decrement race `haveIt`'s own lock already closes; `markMade.ts` resolver: `requireHouseholdMember` (via the menu item's own menu → household, matching `addMenuItem`'s id-only pattern) → fetch the `MenuItem`'s recipe + ingredients + household pantry → S1's pure `computeDeductionLines` (staple-filtered per O2, zero-floored per O1) → `setMenuItemMadeAt` + `applyDeductionLines` in one transaction (a forced failure on either half leaves neither committed, the same atomicity `haveIt`'s own transaction guarantees).
- **Files:** `shared/schema.graphql`; `api/src/repositories/menuRepository.ts`; `api/src/repositories/pantryRepository.ts`; `api/src/resolvers/markMade.ts`; `api/src/mappers/menu.ts`; `infra/stacks/api-stack.ts`.
- **Depends on:** S1.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / Medium — the transaction shape is precedented (`haveIt`), but this is the first resolver to touch both `menu_items` and `pantry_items` in one write.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `database-reviewer` → `security-reviewer` → `code-reviewer` → `doc-updater`.
- **RED tests:** marking a menu item made sets `made_at` and returns the updated `MenuItem`; a second `markMade` on the same item is rejected `CONFLICT`, matching `haveIt`'s own double-purchase rejection shape; deduction correctly decrements a matched pantry row (exact and cross-unit cases, end-to-end against Testcontainers, not just S1's pure-function tests); a staple ingredient with a real matching pantry row is confirmed NOT decremented end-to-end (O2, direct regression, not just S1's pure-function coverage); an unmatched (or staple-excluded) ingredient leaves the pantry untouched and does not create a row; a decrement that would go negative lands at exactly `0` end-to-end, not a negative value, verified against Testcontainers (O1, direct regression); the `made_at` write and every pantry decrement are atomic — a forced mid-transaction failure leaves neither side changed; `servings_override` scales the deduction correctly end-to-end; non-member denied identically to `addMenuItem`'s pattern; explicit `null` on `menuItemId` rejected; **this is the live-reachable path W10 S7's own gap 1 (§16.9) has been waiting on** — a follow-up `Query.menu` after `markMade` now shows `made_at` set through the real API for the first time, closing W10's own carried-forward verification obligation (owned by S7 below, not re-tested here, but this slice is what makes S7 possible).

#### S3 — `Mutation.markPurchased(itemId)` + `Subscription.onShoppingListChanged` + repository

- **Delivers:** SDL `markPurchased(itemId: ID!): ShoppingList!` (D5's no-quantity-argument shape, D1's widened return type); `Subscription.onShoppingListChanged(householdId: ID!): ShoppingList` attached to `["generateShoppingList", "regenerateShoppingList", "haveIt", "markPurchased"]` (D1, §18.2.1) with its own subscribe-time `requireHouseholdMember` authorizer, identical shape to every other subscription field; `shoppingListRepository.ts` — `markItemPurchased(client, itemId)`: `UPDATE shopping_list_items SET purchased = true, moved_to_pantry = true, purchased_by = $caller, purchased_at = now() WHERE id = $1 AND purchased = false RETURNING *` (rejects a second call `CONFLICT`, identical shape to `haveIt`'s own already-purchased guard); `pantryRepository.ts` — reuses `upsertOrIncrementPantryItemForHaveIt` **unchanged for the match/increment path**, but a NEW `insertFreshPantryItemForBought` variant (or a parameterized `defaultExpiryDays` argument threaded through the existing "no match, insert fresh row" branch) sets `expiryDate` from S1's `defaultExpiryDaysForCategory(item.category)` (O3's locked table) instead of hard-coding `null` the way `haveIt`'s own fresh-row insert does (PRD's own "bought items ... with expiry defaults" requirement, absent from "Have it," per D5's framing of the two flows as genuinely different); `markPurchased.ts` resolver: `requireHouseholdMember` (via the item's list, matching `haveIt`'s id-only pattern) → the shopping-list item's own `quantity`/`unit`/`category`/`name` (no separate argument, D5) → the matching/insert-with-default-expiry pantry write → `markItemPurchased` → return the parent `ShoppingList!`. `markPurchased` is unaffected by O1/O2 (those are `markMade`-deduction-only decisions) — its own pantry write stays the plain match/increment-or-insert path D5/D6 already lock.
- **Files:** `shared/schema.graphql`; `api/src/repositories/shoppingListRepository.ts`; `api/src/repositories/pantryRepository.ts`; `api/src/resolvers/markPurchased.ts`; `api/src/resolvers/onShoppingListChanged.ts`; `api/src/mappers/shoppingList.ts`; `infra/stacks/api-stack.ts`.
- **Depends on:** S1 (default-expiry function).
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.5 hrs / Medium — the mutation itself is a close mirror of `haveIt`, but the shared pantry-insert helper now needs a real branch point (default expiry vs. none) it didn't have before, and the new subscription field's mutation-list needs `haveIt`/`generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` all re-verified compatible (they already are — no code change to them, but S9-equivalent verification, folded into S7 here, must confirm all four fire correctly, not just the new one).
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `database-reviewer` → `security-reviewer` → `code-reviewer` → `doc-updater`.
- **RED tests:** marking an item purchased with no existing pantry match creates a fresh row at the default expiry for its category (and `null` for an uncategorized/`other` item, per S1/O3); marking an item purchased matching an existing pantry row increments it and does NOT overwrite that row's existing expiry (the increment path is untouched, per this slice's own "match/increment path unchanged" design); a second `markPurchased` on the same item is rejected `CONFLICT`; the shopping-list item flips `purchased`/`movedToPantry` atomically with the pantry write, forced-failure-tested identically to `haveIt`; the mutation takes no `quantity` argument and always uses the item's own listed quantity (a direct regression test for D5, not left implicit); `onShoppingListChanged` fires on all four attached mutations (`generateShoppingList`, `regenerateShoppingList`, `haveIt`, `markPurchased`) and denies a non-member identically to every other subscribe-time authorizer; non-member denied on `markPurchased` itself identically to `haveIt`'s pattern; explicit `null` rejected where applicable.

#### S4 — Mobile: `MenuRepository.markMade` + `ShoppingListRepository.markPurchased` plumbing

- **Delivers:** `MenuRepository.markMade(menuItemId)` (interface, Ferry impl, fake) mirroring `addMenuItem`'s shape; `CurrentMenuController.markMade(menuItemId)` — the RAW mutation wrapper S5's `markMadeWithUndo` (§18.2.8) calls once its deferred window elapses, refreshing on success (matching the "explicit refresh, no push" contract D4 locks for this mutation this week); `ShoppingListRepository.markPurchased(itemId)` mirroring `haveIt`'s shape (minus the quantity argument, D5); `CurrentShoppingListController.markPurchased(itemId)` + new live-subscription wiring to `onShoppingListChanged` (mirrors `pantry_controller.dart`'s `_changeSubscription` pattern exactly — a `StreamSubscription<void>?` cancelled in `ref.onDispose`, subscribed/unsubscribed on foreground/background the same way `PantryController`'s own S8-era wiring already does, not a new pattern); `.graphql` operation files + codegen for both mutations and the new subscription; the mobile `schema.graphql` re-sync.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/menu/data/menu_repository.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/menu/state/current_menu_controller.dart` (+ its stale-comment fix per §18.1); `mobile/lib/features/shopping_list/data/shopping_list_repository.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/shopping_list/state/current_shopping_list_controller.dart`; new `.graphql` operation files + codegen.
- **Depends on:** S2, S3.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Low.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips.
- **RED tests:** `markMade` refreshes `CurrentMenuController` state on success (the item's `madeAt` visible after refresh, no push expected per D4); a rejection surfaces as a typed `AppError`; `markPurchased` refreshes `CurrentShoppingListController` state (item drops out of `toBuy`, the same getter `haveIt` already relies on, per its own doc — zero new filtering logic needed); a push on `onShoppingListChanged` triggers a refetch (the same "every push means refetch" convention every other subscription-backed controller already follows); the fake repositories round-trip every state the S5/S6 UI tests below need.

#### S5 — "Mark as made" (wireframe, Flow 6, screen 40/49)

- **Delivers:** a "Mark as made" affordance on `today_screen.dart`'s per-item card (today's agenda is the natural surface — a recipe scheduled for today is exactly what gets cooked and marked, not the 7-day grid); **locked per O5 (§18.7 O5): a single tap, no confirm dialog**, driving `CurrentMenuController.markMadeWithUndo(menuItemId)` (§18.2.8's `PendingMarkMadeAction` design) rather than calling `markMade` directly — the tap shows the optimistic made-state immediately and surfaces a "Marked as made · Undo" `SnackBar`; tapping Undo within the snackbar's window calls `undoMarkMade(menuItemId)`, reverting the optimistic state with zero network calls (nothing was ever sent, per D8); letting the window elapse fires the real `markMade` mutation via S4's `CurrentMenuController.markMade`; an already-made item (whether from a previous real `markMade` or the current tap's own optimistic state) renders visibly different (a checkmark/muted state) rather than still offering the affordance, since a second tap would only hit S2's own `CONFLICT` rejection uselessly, or double-schedule a second pending action needlessly.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/today_screen.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/menu/state/pending_mark_made_action.dart` (new, §18.2.8's small pending-action helper) — no `mark_made_confirm_dialog.dart` is built, since O5 locked "no confirmation dialog."
- **Depends on:** S4.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / Medium — larger than the draft's own "small either way" estimate, since O5's locked answer is the deferred-commit/undo design (§18.2.8), not the cheaper no-undo fallback the draft flagged as its own escape hatch.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips.
- **RED tests:** tapping the affordance immediately shows the item in its made state and shows the undo snackbar, WITHOUT calling `markMade` yet (a direct assertion the repository fake was NOT invoked synchronously — the core regression test for D8's "deferred, not fire-and-forget" design); tapping Undo within the window cancels the pending action, reverts the item to its pre-tap state, and confirms `markMade` is never called at all for that tap; letting the window elapse without tapping Undo calls `markMade` with the correct `menuItemId` exactly once; an already-`madeAt`-set item (server-confirmed) renders the made state, not a tappable affordance; a second tap while a pending action is already in flight for the same item is a no-op, not a second scheduled call; a failed `markMade` (once the deferred call actually fires) reverts the item's displayed state to the pre-tap snapshot with a visible error, never a silent no-op (this codebase's standing convention).

#### S6 — "Bought syncs to pantry" (wireframe, Flow 10, screen 41/49)

- **Delivers:** D6's leading-checkbox affordance on `ChecklistItem`, enabled on the same "persistent Shopping List screen only" condition `menuId != null` already gates the Have-it swipe on (a freshly generated List preview is not an "act on it now" surface, matching S7/W11's own reasoning verbatim); tapping calls `markPurchased(itemId)` directly (D5 — no sheet, no deferred/undo mechanism — O5's undo design is locked for `markMade` only, not `markPurchased`, which stays the plain "single tap, immediate commit" shape D5/D6 already lock); an optimistic-checked visual state that reverts on failure.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/shopping_list/presentation/checklist_item.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/shopping_list/presentation/shopping_list_screen.dart` (if any list-level "N bought" summary is added — optional, not required by any wireframe named in scope).
- **Depends on:** S4.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Medium — touches the same widget S7/W11 already made careful about not double-removing rows via `Dismissible`; the checkbox must not race the swipe gesture on the same row.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips.
- **RED tests:** tapping the checkbox calls `markPurchased` with the correct `itemId`, never `haveIt`; the checkbox and the swipe-to-have-it gesture on the same row don't double-fire when triggered in quick succession (an in-flight guard mirroring `ChecklistItem._isProcessing`'s own existing pattern, extended to cover both triggers on one row); a successful `markPurchased` removes the row via the controller's own state rebuild (the same "never let the widget remove itself" discipline `_handleSwipe`'s own doc already states, applied to the checkbox path); a failed `markPurchased` reverts the optimistic checked state and shows a visible error; the checkbox is absent (not merely disabled) on the List preview screen, matching the swipe gesture's own existing `menuId == null` gate.

#### S7 — Offline banner + live-AWS verification + W10 S7 carryover + weekly doc pass

- **Delivers:** a new `OfflineBanner` widget bound to `subscriptionClientProvider`'s `connectionState` (D7, §18.2.7), using the locked `offlineBannerDebounce = Duration(seconds: 4)` constant (O4), mounted once in `mobile/lib/app/app.dart`'s `_RoutedApp` via `MaterialApp.router(builder: ...)` so every screen gets it without per-screen wiring; shows only on `disconnected`, debounced 4s, hides immediately on `connected`; direct-Lambda-invoke live verification of `markMade` (deduction against real dev Aurora, including the same-family cross-unit case, the staple-exclusion case per O2, and the zero-floor case per O1) and `markPurchased` (default-expiry insert per O3's locked table, increment-path expiry untouched, `onShoppingListChanged` firing) against real dev Aurora/AppSync, throwaway households deleted afterward (RUNBOOK.md §2 method, per every prior week's own precedent); **closes W10 S7's own carried-forward gap 1 (§16.9/§16.7 D4)** — `overwrite: true` preserving a `made_at IS NOT NULL` row, now verifiable live for the first time since `markMade` exists and can set the column through the real API rather than only via Testcontainers-direct SQL; the stale `current_menu_controller.dart` comment fix (§18.1); §4.2's mandatory weekly pass — actual-vs-planned hours into §4's W12 row, a decisions-versus-shipped audit of §18.7's LOCKED table.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/shared/ui/components/offline_banner.dart` (+ test); `mobile/lib/app/app.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/menu/state/current_menu_controller.dart` (comment only); `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md` (§4 row, a W12-result subsection); `docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md` (§6.1 re-sync — `markMade`/`markPurchased`/`onShoppingListChanged` all move from "sketched" to "shipped," same annotation style W11's S9 used).
- **Depends on:** all slices.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / Low-Medium, non-optional.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer` (banner); `doc-updater` (verification write-up + doc sync).
- **RED tests (banner):** the banner is invisible while `connectionState.value == connected`; the banner is invisible immediately after `disconnected` begins, until 4 seconds elapse (O4, exact value, not a range); the banner is visible once the 4-second debounce elapses while still `disconnected`; the banner is invisible again immediately on the next `connected` value, with no trailing debounce in that direction; the banner is invisible during `connecting` (never shown for that state, per D7); mounted once at the app root, verified present regardless of which tab/route is active (a direct test against `_RoutedApp`, not inferred).

**Planned total: ~13.5 hrs** against §4's nominal ~10-12 — up from the draft's own ~12.5 hr floor because O5's locked undo design (§18.2.8) adds real, non-trivial scope to S5 that the draft's own cheaper fallback ("single tap, no undo at all") would not have needed; O1/O2/O3/O4 added no scope beyond what the draft had already estimated for (O1/O2 changed S1's own logic and RED-test list, not its size; O3 simply supplied numbers the module's shape already awaited; O4 supplied one integer). No infrastructure spike is scheduled this week (R3's AppSync soak remains open per W11 S4's own write-up, but re-attempting it is not W12's job — it is blocked on real Cognito-authenticated test accounts, an unrelated prerequisite, not a W12 deliverable).

### 18.4 Sequencing

```
S1 (pantry-deduction + default-expiry domain modules, pure)
  │
  ├───────────────────────────────┐
  ▼                                 ▼
S2 (markMade + repository)     S3 (markPurchased + onShoppingListChanged + repository)
  │                                 │
  └────────────┬────────────────────┘
               ▼
        S4 (mobile plumbing: both repositories, both controllers,
            onShoppingListChanged subscription wiring)
               │
     ┌─────────┴─────────┐
     ▼                     ▼
S5 (Mark as made      S6 (Bought checkbox
    screen + undo)        on ChecklistItem)
     └─────────┬─────────┘
               ▼
   S7 (offline banner + live-AWS verification +
       W10 S7 carryover + weekly doc pass)
```

S2 and S3 are fully independent of each other (different tables, different resolvers, no shared file except `pantryRepository.ts` itself — S2 adds `applyDeductionLines`, S3 extends the existing `haveIt`-shared insert helper; both are additive, not conflicting edits, but land as two real slices rather than one to keep each's own agent pipeline and RED-test list focused, the same granularity W11's S2/S3 split modeled). The offline banner (part of S7) has no dependency on S1–S6 at all beyond `subscriptionClientProvider` already existing since W8 — it could in principle start on day one in parallel, the way W11's S8 did for `onMembershipRevoked`, but is kept inside S7 here rather than split into its own slice because it shares no meaningful risk or file surface with the live-verification work S7 also does. If the banner's own small scope proves worth isolating, splitting it out costs nothing at that point.

### 18.5 Risks

#### 18.5.1 `markMade`'s deduction is inference, not a user-confirmed quantity — a wrong deduction is silent and compounds over repeated cooks

Unlike `haveIt`/`markPurchased` (both writing a quantity the user or the shopping list already specified), `markMade` infers "how much of each pantry ingredient did this cooking session actually use" purely from the recipe's own ingredient list. PRD's own "best-effort; user can override" framing (line 159) accepts this is imprecise by design, but a systematically-wrong deduction (e.g., a recipe whose ingredient quantities were entered generously) would silently under- or over-report pantry stock every time it's cooked, with no single bad event to notice — and O1's now-locked zero-floor (§18.2.3) means a chronic under-count never even surfaces as a visible negative number the way the draft's own rejected "allow negative" recommendation would have. Mitigation: this is an accepted PRD-level trade, not a bug this week can fix — the "user can override" escape hatch is `updatePantryItem`, already shipped since W5, and is the intended correction path; this week's own job is only to make the automatic half correct against the matching rules that already exist (D2), not to add a review step. O5's locked undo window (§18.2.8) mitigates the narrower, different risk of an *accidental* `markMade` tap, not this broader "the deduction itself was numerically wrong" risk — the two are separate concerns and this design does not conflate them.

#### 18.5.2 The new pantry-insert branch point in `markPurchased` (default expiry vs. `haveIt`'s `null`) is the first time this codebase's "no match, insert fresh row" pantry helper needs caller-specific behavior

`upsertOrIncrementPantryItemForHaveIt`'s own doc (W11) frames its fresh-row insert as a single, simple fallback. S3 adds a second caller with different insert semantics (O3's locked default-expiry table instead of `null`) for the exact same match logic — a real risk of the shared helper accreting caller-specific branches that make it harder to reason about which caller gets what, the same class of concern this codebase's own `PantryItemPatch`/`PantryItemInput` split (§11.2.7-ish precedent: separate input types rather than one type serving two different call shapes) already exists to avoid. Mitigation: S3's design keeps the match/increment path completely unchanged and shared, and threads only the fresh-row insert's `expiryDate` as an explicit parameter (not a caller-identity flag the function branches on internally) — the same "pass the value, don't pass who's asking" discipline that keeps the function callable correctly by a third future caller without another internal branch.

#### 18.5.3 The offline banner can only observe the AppSync WebSocket's own connection state, not the device's actual network reachability

`connectionState` reflects whether the subscription transport is connected — a device could be online but AppSync-unreachable (rare) or the reverse could never actually happen (no network at all always means `disconnected` eventually, once the ladder's own connect attempt fails), so this is a reasonable proxy, not a true network monitor (`connectivity_plus` or similar is not in this codebase and is out of scope to add this week). Mitigation: PRD's own offline scope is "read-cache only" (line 606) — this banner's job is to tell the user "you may be seeing stale data," which `connectionState` answers correctly for the cases that matter (a real disconnect), not to diagnose network conditions precisely. O4's locked 4-second debounce (§18.2.7) is a UX-feel choice against this same proxy, not a fix for its own known imprecision.

#### 18.5.4 `onShoppingListChanged` now covers four mutations at once, on its first day of existing — a wider first-ship surface than every prior subscription field's own first week

Every other subscription field in this schema shipped attached to either one mutation (`onMembershipRevoked`, `onMenuChanged`) or grew its coverage incrementally across weeks (`onPantryChanged`: 3 from W5, unchanged since; `onHouseholdChanged`: 3 from W8). `onShoppingListChanged` ships day one already covering `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList`/`haveIt` (three mutations that have existed, untested against this subscription, since W11) plus `markPurchased` (new this week) — a wider blast radius for a first-ship bug than this codebase's own precedent favors. Mitigation: S3's RED tests explicitly assert all four attached mutations fire the subscription (not just the new one), and S7's live verification checks the same, rather than assuming the three already-shipped mutations "just work" because their return types already satisfied the type system before the field existed.

#### 18.5.5 O5's deferred-commit undo design introduces a new client-side pending-state class this codebase has not had before

Every other optimistic-update pattern in this codebase (D6's checkbox, the Have-it swipe sheet) commits to the server immediately and reverts on failure — a two-state dance (optimistic, then confirmed-or-reverted). D8's design adds a genuine third state (pending-uncommitted, cancellable) that must be reasoned about across app lifecycle events (backgrounding, navigating away from `today_screen.dart` mid-window) the existing two-state pattern never had to consider. Mitigation: §18.2.8 explicitly scopes this to "must survive foreground lifecycle, does not need to survive process death" — a deliberately narrow, stated contract rather than an unstated assumption — and S5's own RED tests exercise the pending-then-elapsed and pending-then-cancelled paths directly rather than assuming the existing optimistic-update test patterns cover a case they were never written against.

### 18.6 W12 exit criteria

- [ ] `markMade` correctly deducts pantry stock for exact-match and same-family-cross-unit cases, skips every staple-flagged/staple-category/staple-unit ingredient even when a real pantry match exists (O2), never fabricates a new pantry row for an unmatched ingredient, floors a matched row's post-deduction quantity at exactly zero rather than going negative (O1), and applies `servings_override` scaling before matching (S1, S2)
- [ ] A second `markMade` call on an already-made menu item is rejected `CONFLICT`, matching `haveIt`'s own double-purchase guard shape (S2)
- [ ] `markMade`'s `made_at` write and every pantry deduction it triggers are atomic — a forced failure leaves neither side changed (S2, S7)
- [ ] W10 S7's carried-forward gap — `overwrite: true` preserving a `made_at IS NOT NULL` row — is verified live against real dev Aurora for the first time, closing §16.9 gap 1 (S7)
- [ ] `markPurchased` takes no `quantity` argument (D5) and its return type satisfies `ShoppingList!`, attaching cleanly to the new `onShoppingListChanged` (S3, S7)
- [ ] A fresh pantry row created via `markPurchased` carries the category's locked default expiry (O3's table); the match/increment path's existing expiry is never overwritten (S3)
- [ ] `onShoppingListChanged` fires correctly on all four attached mutations (`generateShoppingList`, `regenerateShoppingList`, `haveIt`, `markPurchased`), verified live, not assumed from the three already-shipped mutations' pre-existing return types (S3, S7)
- [ ] No path calls `markPurchased` from `ChecklistItem`'s Have-it swipe gesture or vice versa — the two triggers on one row never double-fire (S6)
- [ ] The offline banner shows only while genuinely `disconnected` past its locked 4-second debounce (O4), never during `connecting`, and hides immediately on reconnect (S7)
- [ ] Tapping "Mark as made" never calls `markMade` synchronously — the mutation only fires once the undo window elapses uninterrupted; tapping Undo within the window cancels it with zero network calls and no server-side reversal (O5/D8, S5)
- [ ] Every nullable/new argument tested with an explicit `null` (§11.5.5's standing convention) — this week's exposure is `markMade`'s `menuItemId` and `markPurchased`'s `itemId`
- [ ] Non-member access denied identically across `markMade`/`markPurchased`/`onShoppingListChanged` (S2, S3)
- [ ] The `current_menu_controller.dart` stale "W9 has no `onMenuChanged`" comment is corrected (§18.1, S7)
- [ ] §4's W12 row has actual hours and §18.7's locked decisions are audited against what shipped (S7)

### 18.7 W12 locked decisions

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| **D1** | Does `onShoppingListChanged` ship in W12, cashing in D1/W11's own promise, and does `markPurchased` need to widen to attach? | **Ships in W12.** `Subscription.onShoppingListChanged(householdId: ID!): ShoppingList` attached to `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList`/`haveIt`/`markPurchased`; `markPurchased` widens `ShoppingListItem! → ShoppingList!`, same precedent as W11 D1/W8 D4. |
| **D2** | Does `markMade`'s deduction need new fuzzy-matching or unit-conversion code, or does it reuse W11's? | **Full reuse** — `namesMatch`/`unitsCompatible`/`convertQuantity`/`isStapleExcluded`, all unmodified, no second matcher or table. |
| **D3** | Does deduction insert a new pantry row for an unmatched ingredient? | **No** — an unmatched (or staple-excluded) ingredient is always a no-op; deduction never fabricates a new row. |
| **D4** | Does `markMade` attach to `onMenuChanged`? | **No**, same structural reasoning as W11's D9-carryover — `MenuItem!`'s directly-consumed return shape isn't worth widening this week. Accepted staleness gap, closed on refetch. |
| **D5** | Does `markPurchased` take a `quantity` argument? | **No** — always uses the shopping-list item's own listed `quantity`/`unit`, per SD's already-locked sketch and PRD's own "no prompt" framing for "bought." |
| **D6** | Swipe or checkbox for "bought"? | **Leading checkbox** — PRD's own "check off" language, and to avoid a second, ambiguous swipe-direction meaning on the same row `Dismissible` already uses for Have-it. |
| **D7** | Which `connectionState` values show the offline banner? | **`disconnected` only** (debounced — see O4); never `connecting`; hides immediately on `connected`. |
| **D8** | (New this week, born from O5 below.) How does `markMade`'s undo actually work, mechanically? | **Client-side deferred commit** — the mutation call itself is delayed behind a cancellable timer matched to the undo snackbar's own duration; Undo cancels the timer (nothing was ever sent to the server); letting it elapse fires the real `markMade`. No compensating/reversal mutation exists or is needed. |
| **O1** | Does a matched pantry row's post-deduction quantity clamp at zero, or go negative? | **Clamp at zero** — **reverses this draft's own drafting-pass recommendation** (which was "allow negative, as an honest 'you're short' signal"). Matches `subtractPantry`'s own "never negative" convention on the shopping-list side, reusing the identical `Math.max(0, ...)` idiom. No "you're short" negative-quantity UI signal is built. |
| **O2** | Are staple-flagged/staple-category/staple-unit ingredients deducted from the pantry, or skipped? | **Skipped** — **reverses this draft's own drafting-pass recommendation** (which was "deduct everything with a match"). `computeDeductionLines` applies `isStapleExcluded` (reused, unmodified) before matching, exactly mirroring shopping-list generation's own visibility rule rather than treating deduction as a separate concern. |
| **O3** | What are the actual default expiry day-counts per pantry category for `markPurchased`'s fresh-row insert? | **Category-specific table, exactly as the drafting pass recommended** (no reversal), confirmed to match `pantryCategories.ts`'s real `KNOWN_PANTRY_CATEGORIES` enum exactly: `dairy: 5`, `produce: 7`, `frozen: 90`, `oil: 180`, `condiment: 180`, `dal: 180`, `grain: 180`, `dry_goods: 270`, `spice: 365`, `other: null`. |
| **O4** | How long must `connectionState` stay `disconnected` before the offline banner renders? | **4 seconds**, exactly, as a named `offlineBannerDebounce = Duration(seconds: 4)` constant — the founder-picked midpoint of the drafting pass's own recommended 3–5s range (no reversal), locked as a concrete number rather than left as a range. |
| **O5** | Does `markMade` require confirmation, or is it a single tap? | **Single tap, no confirmation dialog, paired with an undo snackbar** — as the drafting pass recommended (no reversal), but with the "undo" mechanism the draft had explicitly left undesigned now locked as D8's client-side deferred commit (§18.2.8), not the draft's own "fallback to no undo at all" escape hatch. |

**Decision density: 13 locked** (D1–D8, 8 by direct precedent/structural necessity plus D8 born from designing O5's answer; O1–O5, 5 founder calls) — higher than W11's 9, because this week's founder-facing decisions (O1–O5) needed real design work once answered, not just a yes/no. **Two of the five founder decisions (O1, O2) reverse this plan's own drafting-pass recommendation** — both toward the simpler, more conservative behavior (clamp instead of surfacing negative stock; skip staples instead of decrementing them), matching a founder preference (also visible in O5's insistence on a real undo design rather than the draft's cheaper no-undo fallback) for correctness and safety over the drafting pass's own inclination toward "more information surfaced, even if messier." O3, O4, and O5's *core* answer (single-tap) all confirmed the drafting pass's own recommendation without reversal — the two reversals are isolated to O1/O2, not a wholesale rejection of this plan's reasoning.

### 18.8 S7 result — offline banner, real-AWS verification, W10 S7 carryover close-out, and W12 close-out (founder-authorized)

**Deploy at time of this pass:** `Parimaan-dev-Data`/`Parimaan-dev-Api` carry all W12 resolver Lambdas, confirmed via `aws lambda list-functions --region ap-south-1`: `MarkMadeFn8BB9D9D3`, `MarkPurchasedFnB1FC41D5`, `OnShoppingListChangedFn9995066B`, alongside every W11-and-earlier resolver. `AWS_PROFILE=parimaan-dev aws sts get-caller-identity` confirmed account `917246556431`/`ap-south-1` before anything else, per this pass's own authorization.

**Offline banner (Part 1, mobile).** `OfflineBanner` (`mobile/lib/shared/ui/components/offline_banner.dart`) binds to `AppSyncSubscriptionClient.connectionState`, debounced by the locked `offlineBannerDebounce = Duration(seconds: 4)` (O4), mounted once at the app root in `mobile/lib/app/app.dart`'s `_RoutedApp` via `MaterialApp.router(builder: ...)`. TDD (RED then GREEN), then `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`, both run in the foreground per this slice's own instruction. `flutter-reviewer`'s first pass found two real, blocking issues, both fixed and re-verified before merge:

- **CRITICAL (fixed):** `connectionState` defaults to `disconnected` and only leaves it once a real `subscribe()` call happens (`reconnectNow()` itself no-ops with zero active subscriptions) — so a screen that never opens a subscription (the sign-in screen; any authenticated screen before the first subscription-backed controller mounts) would otherwise show "you're offline" a flat 4 seconds after cold boot, with nothing actually wrong. Fixed with a `_hasEverAttemptedConnection` latch: the debounce/show logic only treats `disconnected` as a real disconnect once `connectionState` has been observed leaving `disconnected` at least once. Covered by two new tests (never-attempted stays hidden past the debounce window; attempted-then-disconnected shows correctly).
- **HIGH (fixed):** wrapping routed content in `Column`/`Expanded` alongside the banner's own `SafeArea` caused every downstream screen's own `SafeArea` to double-pad for the same top inset while the banner was visible. Fixed with `MediaQuery.removePadding(removeTop: true, ...)` around the child subtree whenever the banner is shown, with a new geometry-asserting regression test (`MediaQuery.paddingOf(context).top` reads `0` while visible, the ambient value while hidden).

Both fixes re-reviewed and approved by `flutter-reviewer` (second pass) and `code-reviewer` (one pass, three LOW/MEDIUM nits — a comment overstating a dead-code path, a missing `ref.onDispose` in a test helper, an imprecise test name — all fixed). Final state: 14 widget tests (9 in `offline_banner_test.dart`, plus a new `widget_test.dart` case proving `OfflineBanner` mounts exactly once at the app root across both the signed-out and signed-in initial routes), `flutter analyze --fatal-infos` clean, `dart format` applied to touched files only. The stale `current_menu_controller.dart` comment named in §18.1 had already been corrected by the time S7 started (fixed in an earlier slice's own commit) — verified, not re-fixed.

**Method for live verification (Part 2):** identical to RUNBOOK.md §2 / W11 S9's own precedent (§17.9) — direct Lambda invoke with an AppSync-resolver-shaped `{ arguments, identity }` event and a synthetic Cognito identity (fresh UUID `sub`/`username`, `claims.email` a `<uuid>@example.test`), driven from a throwaway Node script (kept in the session scratchpad, not committed). One throwaway household was built end-to-end through real resolvers only — `createHousehold` → `updateHouseholdSettings` → `createRecipe` ×3 → `createMenu` → `addMenuItem` ×3 → `addPantryItem` ×7 — and fully deleted at the end of the pass. A second synthetic identity (never added to the household) was used for every non-member-denial check. No pre-existing household, user, recipe, or menu was read or modified.

**Synthetic identities used (recorded explicitly, per RUNBOOK.md §2's own W11 S9 process-gap fix):** owner `sub` `abbaa64b-829b-4ce1-b8d4-7e2a63e8ad86`; outsider `sub` `35959bb0-bd25-4f36-88eb-f66dbeb83e73`; household id `b882c9cb-67a2-4db3-b089-25eedbb4be17` (deleted, confirmed below). Two earlier attempts against fresh synthetic identities failed on request-shape issues before any state-changing call ran (a missing `accompaniment` key in `mealStructure`'s required three-field shape, and a missing top-level `householdId` argument on `createRecipe`) — neither reached `markMade`/`markPurchased`, and each throwaway household from those attempts was still cleanly deleted via its own recorded identity before moving on.

**Seed shape, chosen to exercise D2/D3/O1/O2/O3 all at once:** Recipe R1 (`carb`, servings 2): `basmati rice` 200g/grain (exact-match target); `milk` 0.5l/dairy (same-family cross-unit target, pantry seeded in ml); `turmeric` 1 tsp (staple-unit exclusion); `special masala mix` 5g, `isStaple: true` (staple-flag exclusion); `chili powder` 50g/spice (staple-category exclusion); `atta` 1000g/grain (zero-floor target, pantry seeded well below need); `unmatched veggie` 300g/produce (no pantry row, D3 no-fabricated-row target); `buttermilk` 200ml/dairy (no pantry row, O3 fresh-insert target). Recipe R2 (`sabzi_dal`, servings 2): `quinoa` 100g/grain, placed with `servingsOverride: 4` (scale ×2). Recipe R3 (`accompaniment`, servings 1, no ingredients): placed but deliberately never marked made, purely to exercise `autoFillWeek(overwrite: true)`'s removal half.

**Verified live — every check below is a real invoke against real dev Aurora/AppSync, not a simulation (51/51 checks passed on the final run):**

1. **`markMade` correctly deducts pantry stock for an exact-match case** (`basmati rice` 1000g − 200g = **800g**, exact) **and a same-family cross-unit case** (`milk` 2000ml − 500ml[=0.5l] = **1500ml**, exact) — both from one real `markMade` call.
2. **O2 (locked, reversed): staple-flagged/staple-category/staple-unit ingredients are NEVER decremented by `markMade`, verified directly and explicitly, against real pantry matches.** `special masala mix` (`isStaple: true`) stayed at **500g** unchanged; `chili powder` (`category: spice`) stayed at **200g**; `turmeric` (`1 tsp`) stayed at **100g** — all three had a real, otherwise-matching pantry row and none moved.
3. **O1 (locked, reversed): a deduction that would take a matched row below zero clamps at exactly zero, never negative, verified directly with a real over-deduction case.** `atta` (pantry 100g, recipe need 1000g) landed at exactly **0**, not `-900`.
4. **`markMade` never fabricates a new pantry row for an unmatched ingredient.** `unmatched veggie` and `buttermilk` (both with no pre-existing pantry row) were absent from the pantry read immediately after `markMade` — pantry row count stayed at 7 (the seeded set), not 9.
5. **A second `markMade` call on an already-made menu item is rejected, exact message `"This menu item has already been marked as made."`** — direct-invoke output never carries the client-safe `errorType` AppSync's own resolver-response mapping would apply (only the Lambda runtime's generic wrapper plus the message), so this pass asserts on the exact message text, matching RUNBOOK.md §2's own documented convention.
6. **`servingsOverride` scaling applied before matching, verified end-to-end.** `quinoa` (recipe base 100g at `servings: 2`, placed with `servingsOverride: 4`) decremented pantry by **200g** (100g × 2, the correct `servingsOverride/servings` ratio), landing at 1000g − 200g = **800g**.
7. **The `made_at` write and every pantry deduction it triggers are atomic** — confirmed structurally (one `withUserTransaction` call wraps both `applyDeductionLines` and `setMenuItemMadeAt` in `markMade.ts`, unchanged since S2's own Testcontainers forced-failure test) rather than re-forced live; no live tooling exists to inject a mid-transaction failure against a real Lambda without an unacceptable code change, the same class of limitation every prior week's live pass has accepted for atomicity checks.
8. **W10 S7's own carried-forward gap (§16.9 gap 1 / §16.7 D4) is CLOSED — `overwrite: true` preserving a `made_at IS NOT NULL` row, now verified live for the first time.** `menuItem1` and `menuItem2` were both marked made via real `markMade` calls; `menuItem3` (Recipe R3, `accompaniment`) was deliberately left unmade. `autoFillWeek(menuId, overwrite: true, items: [])` was then called against the same menu: the follow-up `Query.menu` showed **both** made items (`menuItem1`, `menuItem2`) present, byte-identical ids, untouched — and `menuItem3` **removed**, exactly as D4's locked "never deletes a `madeAt`-set item regardless of `overwrite`" contract promises. Both the preservation half and the removal half were exercised in the same call, not assumed from one another.
9. **`markPurchased` takes no `quantity` argument and always uses the item's own listed quantity** — the mutation's own SDL shape (`markPurchased(itemId: ID!): ShoppingList!`) has no such argument to pass, and every live call in this pass supplied only `itemId`, confirming D5 holds at the wire level, not just in the schema text.
10. **A fresh pantry row created via `markPurchased` (no existing match) carries the correct category-specific default expiry (O3's locked table), spot-checked on two categories: `dairy: 5` and `produce: 7`.** `buttermilk` (dairy, purchased 2026-09-04) landed with `expiryDate: "2026-09-09"` (exactly +5 days); `unmatched veggie` (produce) landed with `expiryDate: "2026-09-11"` (exactly +7 days). **O3's `spice: 365` entry is STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE live through this path, named explicitly rather than silently substituted:** `recipe_ingredients.category: "spice"` is always staple-excluded (`isStapleExcluded`'s `STAPLE_CATEGORIES` branch) before a `ShoppingListItem` can ever exist for it, so `generateShoppingList` can never produce a live spice-category shopping-list line for `markPurchased` to act on — this is a real, permanent structural gap (not fixable by re-seeding), covered only by `defaultExpiry.test.ts`'s existing pure-function unit test. `dairy`/`produce` were substituted deliberately, not as an unexplained swap.
11. **Marking an item purchased that DOES match an existing pantry row increments it and does NOT overwrite that row's existing expiry.** `atta`'s pantry row (zero-floored to 0 by check 3 above, pre-seeded with `expiryDate: "2026-06-01"`) was incremented by `markPurchased` from **0 → 1000g**, with `expiryDate` reading **exactly `"2026-06-01"`** afterward — unchanged, not overwritten with grain's own 180-day default.
12. **A second `markPurchased` on the same item is rejected, exact message `"This item has already been marked as purchased."`** — same direct-invoke message-assertion convention as check 5.
13. **`onShoppingListChanged` fires correctly on all four attached mutations, verified live at the SDL/resolver-authorization level.** `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList`/`haveIt` were already confirmed compatible by W11 S9 (§17.9 finding 5, 9); `markPurchased` (new this week) was exercised directly in checks 9–12 above, all returning the widened `ShoppingList!` shape the subscription attaches to. The subscribe-time authorizer itself (`onShoppingListChanged.ts`, reusing `requireHouseholdMember` unchanged) was invoked directly: a real member received AppSync's standard `null` (pure-gate convention), a non-member received the exact `"You are not a member of this household."` denial. **Opening a real authenticated WebSocket connection to observe the actual push remains unreachable this pass** — the same Google-only-Cognito blocker every prior week's S9/S4 pass has already documented (§17.8, §17.9) — named again, not re-attempted, per this pass's own instruction not to re-attempt a known-blocked check.
14. **Non-member access denied identically across `markMade`/`markPurchased`/`onShoppingListChanged`.** All three returned the exact same `"You are not a member of this household."` message for the outsider identity — same message, same shape, no mutation leaking a different signal.
15. **Explicit `null` on nullable/new arguments doesn't crash `markMade`/`markPurchased`.** `markMade(menuItemId: null)` → `"Invalid input: expected string, received null"`; `markPurchased(itemId: null)` → the identical shape. Both a clean `ValidationError`-class rejection, not a 500 or an unhandled exception.
16. **Cleanup — complete and confirmed.** The one household this pass's final, successful run created (`b882c9cb-67a2-4db3-b089-25eedbb4be17`) was fully deleted via `deleteHousehold`, and a follow-up `Query.household` confirmed the standard no-existence-oracle denial (`"You are not a member of this household."`). The two earlier, request-shape-only failed attempts (see synthetic-identities note above) were also each cleaned up via their own recorded identity before this pass moved to the next attempt — no throwaway household from any attempt this pass made was left behind. Nothing from this pass remains in dev Aurora.

**No CRITICAL or HIGH findings against the API, and no NEW regression.** Every `markMade`/`markPurchased`/`onShoppingListChanged` behavior checked matched its locked design exactly on the first fully-correct seed; the two earlier failed attempts were both request-shape mistakes in this pass's own throwaway script (a missing required `mealStructure.accompaniment` key, a missing `createRecipe` `householdId` argument), not product defects — neither reached a `markMade`/`markPurchased` call.

- [~] `markMade`'s `made_at` write and every pantry deduction it triggers are atomic — **structurally confirmed** (one `withUserTransaction` call wraps both writes), **not re-forced live** this pass (no live tooling exists to inject a mid-transaction failure against a real deployed Lambda without an unacceptable code change); S2's own Testcontainers forced-failure test remains the direct proof
- [x] **W10 S7's carried-forward gap — `overwrite: true` preserving a `made_at IS NOT NULL` row — is verified live against real dev Aurora for the first time, closing §16.9 gap 1** (check 8) — both the preservation half (two made items survived) and the removal half (one unmade item was removed) exercised in the same call
- [x] `markPurchased` takes no `quantity` argument (D5) and its return type satisfies `ShoppingList!`, attaching cleanly to `onShoppingListChanged` — **verified live** (checks 9, 13)

- [~] `onShoppingListChanged` fires correctly on all four attached mutations — **verified live at the SDL/resolver-authorization level** (check 13); the actual WebSocket push to a live connection remains unreachable, the same named Cognito-auth blocker every prior week's S9/S4 pass has already documented, not re-attempted per this pass's own instruction
- [~] No path calls `markPurchased` from `ChecklistItem`'s Have-it swipe gesture or vice versa (S6) — **not independently re-verified live this pass** (a mobile-UI-only concern, outside direct-Lambda-invoke reach), covered by S6's own widget-test suite
- [x] The offline banner shows only while genuinely `disconnected` past its locked 4-second debounce (O4), never during `connecting`, and hides immediately on reconnect — **verified** by 9 widget tests, including the never-attempted-connection false-positive fix and the never-shown-during-connecting case
- [~] Tapping "Mark as made" never calls `markMade` synchronously; tapping Undo cancels with zero network calls (O5/D8) — **not independently re-verified live this pass** (mobile-UI-only), covered by S5's own `fake_async`-based test suite (already merged, `5473f59`)
- [x] Every nullable/new argument tested with an explicit `null` — `markMade.menuItemId`, `markPurchased.itemId` — **verified live** (check 15)
- [x] Non-member access denied identically across `markMade`/`markPurchased`/`onShoppingListChanged` — **verified live** (check 14), identical message across all three
- [x] The `current_menu_controller.dart` stale "W9 has no `onMenuChanged`" comment is corrected — **already fixed in an earlier W12 slice's own commit**, confirmed by direct read, not re-fixed
- [x] §4's W12 row has actual hours (table above) and §18.7's locked decisions are audited against what shipped (audit table above)

**Net: 9 of 13 boxes fully closed live by this pass, 4 correctly left as partial** (three mobile-UI-only concerns genuinely outside a direct-Lambda-invoke pass's reach, each already covered by its own slice's merged test suite, not silently assumed; one — the WebSocket-push half of `onShoppingListChanged` and atomicity's live-forced-failure half — the same class of human-credential-gated/tooling-gated gap named, not faked, in every prior week's own S9/S4 pass).

#### End of Month 3 milestone — is the full core loop genuinely closed?

Per §4's own gate language for W12 ("**End of Month 3 milestone:** full core loop; founder household using daily") and §4's own End-of-Month-3 criteria row: **(a) full core loop working (plan → auto-fill → generate list → have-it/mark-purchased → pantry updates) — YES, closed end-to-end by this pass.** Every link in that chain now has a real, merged, live-verified resolver: `autoFillWeek`/`addMenuItem` (W9–W10) plan the menu; `generateShoppingList` (W11) produces the list; `haveIt` (W11) and `markPurchased` (W12, this week) both move a shopping-list item into the pantry; `markMade` (W12, this week) deducts pantry stock when a planned recipe is actually cooked. This pass is the first time the *entire* loop — not just its individual links — has been exercised together in one live seed: create → settings → recipes → menu → plan two items → seed pantry → cook one (`markMade`, deducting real stock, correctly skipping staples, correctly zero-flooring) → generate a shopping list reflecting the post-cook pantry state → buy the remaining items (`markPurchased`, correctly defaulting expiry / correctly preserving an existing row's expiry) — against real dev Aurora, not Testcontainers.

**(b) Founder household uses it for one full week — NOT this pass's to close.** This is a real-world usage criterion, not a code-verification one; nothing in an agent-run direct-Lambda-invoke pass can satisfy it. Named as still open, per this document's own standing discipline of never closing a box on faith.

**(c) 42 wireframe screens shipped — consistent with §18.1's own count** (Mark as made, Bought syncs to pantry, Offline banner all shipped this week, landing at 42/49 per §18.1's arithmetic) — not independently re-counted by this pass; §18.1's own accounting stands.

**(d) RDS Proxy + subscription spikes passed — RDS Proxy: yes** (R6, closed W11 S4/§17.8, 88 concurrent invokes, zero failures). **Subscription soak (R3): still open**, unchanged since §17.8/§17.9 — blocked on real Google-authenticated Cognito test credentials, a founder-owned prerequisite this pass cannot manufacture, named again rather than silently re-closed.

**Overall: the core loop itself (a) is genuinely, verifiably closed end-to-end against real infrastructure. The Month 3 gate's other three criteria are a mix of already-closed (RDS Proxy), a real-world usage milestone outside any code pass's reach (b), and one long-standing, explicitly-named, credential-gated blocker (R3, part of d) — none of which this pass can close, and none of which this pass claims to have closed.**

**This closes W12 end to end** — all seven slices merged, the offline banner shipped and reviewed (two real `flutter-reviewer` findings fixed), the full `markMade`/`markPurchased`/`onShoppingListChanged` surface verified live including both locked-reversal behaviors (O1, O2) directly, W10 S7's own carried-forward gap finally closed live, one decision-vs-shipped audit clean across all thirteen decisions, and every exit-criteria box either closed live or named open with its exact blocker, none closed on faith.
