## 19. Week 13: First-run welcome + meal-instance grouping + editable `mealsEnabled`

**Status:** LOCKED. Written 2026-09-07, after W12 closed (§18.8) and after the founder ran the shipped build in front of real first-time users. The six decisions this week and W14 raised were put to the founder and answered before either plan was written — they are recorded once, in §10 as **Q16–Q21**, and referenced from here rather than re-argued, the same "founder call, recorded once made" posture §17.7/§18.7 used. This section's own **D-numbers are W13-local** (D1–D6), exactly as every prior week's are.

### 19.1 What W13 is locked to deliver

Per §4's newly-inserted W13 row: the **Welcome · what would you like to do first?** screen (Flow 1, the fourth of five in that flow — W2=2, W4=1, **W13=1**, W24=1 = 5, per §4's amended verification row), landing at **43/50**; the Weekly plan's four **meal-instance** headers; a real Settings edit path for `mealsEnabled` covering all four meal types; per-meal-type meal-structure editing (Lunch **and** Dinner, not Lunch only); and the `updateSettingsPartial` merge fix that makes editing either one non-destructive. Gate, in §4's own words: "Welcome screen shows only for a household with no real activity; Weekly plan renders four meal-instance headers; editing Lunch structure leaves Dinner's counts intact."

**Forward-references this plan accounts for** (via `grep -n "W13" docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md` against the post-renumber doc, plus the four code-level claims the feedback pointed at — every one addressed below, none silently dropped):

| # | Source | What it commits W13 to |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | §4 W13 row (new) | Welcome screen (43/50), meal-instance grouping, `mealsEnabled` editable, the Dinner-clobber fix |
| 2 | §3 Phase 3b DoD (new) | The activity threshold is "any one of ≥1 recipe / ≥1 pantry item / ≥1 planned menu item", not a dismiss flag |
| 3 | §10 Q16 | This week exists *instead of* pulling W15/W16's seeding forward; the "Add New Recipe" CTA points at a library that stays empty until W15/W16 |
| 4 | §10 Q18 | Nothing here may imply Sweet/Drink get slots — the grouping covers Breakfast/Lunch/Snacks/Dinner and the three existing types only |
| 5 | §10 Q19 | The meal-structure clobber is fixed here, as a RED-tested slice, not as a standalone bug week |
| 6 | §10 Q20 | "Any real activity exists" is the threshold; where the check is computed is a flagged judgment call, not locked |
| 7 | §11.1 out-of-scope | "Snacks UI unreachability" — open since W5, closed by S3 below |
| 8 | `mobile/lib/features/household/domain/meal_type.dart:45–65` | Its own doc comment claims Snacks "remains reachable through the Settings → Meal structure screen (wireframe 4.1), which is a later slice." **That later slice is this one**, and the sentence is false until it merges — a §11.2.10-class stale claim, corrected in the same PR that makes it true (S3) |
| 9 | `mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/weekly_plan_screen.dart:36–43` | The screen's own doc already flags its seven-stacked-day layout as an undocumented-in-wireframe judgment call. The meal-instance grouping is a second layer inside each day and is flagged the same way (§19.2.5), not presented as a wireframe the author has seen |
| 10 | `mobile/lib/features/menu/domain/meal_slot_plan.dart` | `plannedSlotsForDay` already computes exactly the right slot instances, honoring `mealsEnabled` and per-role counts, and already returns them in `MealType.values` then `MealSlot.values` order. The grouping is a **regrouping of this function's existing output**, not a second derivation of the cap rule — the §16.5.1 drift class this codebase has warned about twice |
| 11 | §15.5.1 | "The meal-structure-cap enforcement is the week's one genuinely novel piece, and it exists in two places that must agree" — the client `plannedSlotsForDay` and the server `getMealSlotCap`. W13 adds no third place; W14's snapshot changes *where the config is read from* in both, not how it is applied |
| 12 | §14.2.11 / W8 S1 | `router.dart`'s `_redirect` was rewritten in W8 to choose `/home` vs `/first-run` from `meHouseholdsControllerProvider`, with an explicit three-state (resolving / none / some) shape. The welcome-screen decision is a fourth landing spot inside that same already-careful function — it must inherit its "still resolving is not the same as empty" discipline, not re-invent it (§19.2.2) |

**Explicitly out of scope for W13** (owned elsewhere, not oversights): the config **snapshot** onto `menus` and every server read-path switch it implies (**W14**, §20 — W13 changes what a household can configure and how the grid displays it, and deliberately leaves the "when does a change take effect" question entirely to W14, so the two weeks do not both touch the same resolvers); the pantry curated-item picker and `bulkAddPantryItems` wiring (**W14**); the 50-recipe curated library that would give the welcome screen's "Add New Recipe" CTA something to land on (**W15/W16**, Q16 — named in the screen's own copy, §19.2.1); Sweet/Drink as planned slot types (**never in MVP**, Q18); a "reconcile items already placed above a newly-lowered cap" flow (`meal_slot_plan.dart`'s own doc already declares this out of scope for the read-only grid, and lowering a cap mid-week becomes structurally impossible for the *current* week once W14 lands anyway); copy/clear day-and-week (PRD §7.1 — **scheduled W14 S8, added 2026-09-10**, after this section was written; W13 itself is unchanged by that amendment); any change to `onMenuChanged`'s coverage (§17.2.9's separate future slice, not reopened).

### 19.2 Design

#### 19.2.1 D1 — the welcome screen is a fifth Flow 1 screen, built on `first_run_choose_path_screen.dart`'s dual-CTA precedent, and it says out loud that the library is empty until W15/W16

The founder's ask names the precedent directly: the screen should look and behave like the existing Create/Join chooser. That screen (`mobile/lib/features/onboarding/presentation/first_run_choose_path_screen.dart`) is a `ConsumerWidget` with a headline, a one-line body, and two `_PathCard`s — no local state, nothing async, no server-error line, because the mutation it used to own moved into the wizard. **Locked: the welcome screen is the same shape** — `WelcomeChoosePathScreen`, a `ConsumerWidget`, two `_PathCard`-equivalent CTAs ("Add to Pantry" → `AppRoutes.pantryAddChooseMethod`, "Add New Recipe" → the recipe method chooser), zero mutations of its own, and therefore zero loading/error states. It is deliberately *not* a modal, a bottom sheet, or a banner on Home: it is a routed screen at `AppRoutes.welcome` (`/welcome`), because the redirect logic in §19.2.2 needs a location to send people to, and a dialog cannot be a redirect target.

**The forward-reference is in the screen's own copy, not only in this plan.** Tapping "Add New Recipe" in W13 lands on the recipe method chooser against a library that is empty for every household — the 50 curated recipes seed on `createHousehold` only once W16's seeder Lambda exists (Q8's copy-per-household decision, W15/W16's content weeks). The screen's body copy therefore describes adding *your* first recipe rather than promising a catalog, and this file's doc comment carries the forward-reference explicitly: **once W16 ships, a newly created household will already have 50 recipes, and this CTA's target state changes from "empty library, add one" to "50 recipes, add your own" without the screen itself changing.** Naming it here is the point — a reader who finds this screen after W16 should not conclude the CTA was mis-targeted. This is also the whole of W13's answer to feedback item E (users expected a large catalog): the catalog was never missing from the plan, only from the calendar, and Q16 declined to move it.

#### 19.2.2 D2 — the "has this household done anything real?" check is computed in the router's existing redirect, from providers that are already loaded; **where exactly** is a flagged judgment call

Q20 locks the *threshold* — ≥1 recipe OR ≥1 pantry item OR ≥1 planned menu item, evaluated live, never a stored dismiss flag. It does not lock the mechanism, and this plan does not pretend it does.

**What is locked:** no new schema, no new dedicated "hasActivity" query, no new resolver. The data is already reachable: `router.dart:802–862`'s `_redirect` is already the single place that decides a signed-in user's landing spot, already reads `meHouseholdsControllerProvider` to choose `/home` vs `/first-run`, and already distinguishes three states (still-resolving / errored / resolved) rather than two — the exact discipline this check needs, since a household whose recipe list has not resolved yet must not be read as "no recipes" and bounced to the welcome screen it has already outgrown. The recipes, pantry and menu controllers are all already Riverpod providers on the authenticated side.

**What is a CALL, flagged as one** (§11.2.6/§11.2.7's convention, and `weekly_plan_screen.dart:36–43`'s own precedent for flagging an undocumented decision in the code rather than presenting it as locked): whether the three-way OR is computed *inline* in `_redirect` or in a small derived provider (`householdHasActivityProvider`) that `_redirect` reads. This plan's own lean is the derived provider — `_redirect` is already the longest single function in `router.dart` and is guarded by W8 S1's dedicated router test harness, and a derived provider is directly unit-testable without driving a router — but the choice is made at implementation time by whoever writes S6, recorded in that file's doc comment, and is explicitly **not** a decision this document is locking on the founder's behalf. What is not negotiable either way is the three-state handling: **while any of the three sources is still resolving, hold on splash** (`_holdOnSplash`, already in the file) rather than guessing, for the identical reason W8 gave for households — a returning user must never flash the welcome screen before the answer arrives.

**One consequence, named rather than discovered later:** the check re-evaluates on every redirect, so a household that deletes its last recipe, its last pantry item and its last menu item would see the welcome screen again. That is the correct behaviour under Q20's own wording ("a household stops seeing it once it has ≥1 …" is a live predicate, not a latch) and is not treated as a bug.

#### 19.2.3 D3 — `mealsEnabled` becomes editable from Settings for **all four** meal types, and the meal-structure screen becomes per-meal-type

Two related gaps, closed together because they are one surface:

1. **`mealsEnabled` has no post-creation edit path at all.** `settings_hub_screen.dart` lists six editable rows (Household, Members, Meal structure, Cuisine preferences, Dietary tags, Allergens & skip list) and no "Meals to plan" row; `AppRoutes` has `editMealStructurePattern`/`editCuisinePattern`/`editCuisineBiasPattern`/`editDietaryPattern` and no meals equivalent. The server has supported it since W4 (`updateSettingsPartial`'s `meals_enabled` parameter, `api/src/repositories/householdRepository.ts:493–501`). The wizard is the only writer, and it offers three toggles, not four (`meal_type.dart`'s `wizardMealTypes`).
2. **The wizard's three-toggle screen clears Snacks by construction.** `meal_type.dart:45–65` says so itself and is explicit that this is a whole-list replacement, not per-meal flags — *"a meal the user was never shown is a meal they did not opt into"* — and then promises the Settings screen as the escape hatch. That promise has never been kept.

**Locked:** a new Settings row, "Meals to plan", routed to `AppRoutes.editMeals(householdId)`, rendering `WhichMealsScreen` through `HouseholdEditEntry` — the exact adapter the other four edit rows already use, whose own doc says *"adding a sixth editable preference is a route entry and nothing else."* In the **edit** flow the screen offers **all four** `MealType.values`, not `wizardMealTypes`. The create wizard is left at three toggles, unchanged: Q18's neighbouring reasoning applies — changing what the wizard shows is a design change to a shipped onboarding flow nobody asked for, whereas making the settings screen complete is the fix that was already promised. `wizardMealTypes` keeps its name and its three entries, and its doc comment's final sentence is corrected from a promise into a statement of fact with a `W13 S3` citation.

**And, because Q17/§20 needs per-meal-type counts to be actually settable:** `MealStructureScreen` (today hardcoded to Lunch — *"Three steppers, 0–10 each … Lunch only: the screen's own footnote says dinner is edited separately later"*) takes the meal type as a parameter, and Settings grows **two** structure rows, "Lunch structure" and "Dinner structure", replacing the single "Meal structure" row. The screen's own footnote about dinner being edited separately becomes true rather than aspirational. Breakfast and Snacks get no structure row at all — they are single-item meals with no per-role structure anywhere in this system (`api/src/domain/mealStructure.ts`'s `SINGLE_ITEM_MEAL_SLOTS`, `meal_slot_plan.dart`'s matching branch), and inventing one for them would be exactly the kind of silent client/server divergence §15.5.1 warns about.

#### 19.2.4 D4 — the Dinner-clobber fix is a JSONB merge in `updateSettingsPartial`, not a fuller patch built on the client

The live bug, exactly: `meal_structure_screen.dart` builds a patch carrying *one* key (its own doc: *"the patch this builds carries exactly one `lunch` key"*), and `api/src/repositories/householdRepository.ts:493–501` writes `meal_structure = COALESCE($3::jsonb, meal_structure)` — a whole-column replace. Saving Lunch therefore writes `{"lunch": {...}}` over a document that also held `{"dinner": {...}}`, and every Dinner slot silently becomes zero (`getMealSlotCap` returns `0` for a missing entry, deliberately — *"silently allowing an unconfigured slot to fill up unbounded is the more dangerous failure mode"* — so the failure presents as a Dinner row that renders no slots at all rather than as an error, which is exactly why nobody caught it before a real user did).

Two candidate fixes were considered; the choice matters and is recorded rather than assumed:

- **(a) Client sends the whole document.** `HouseholdEditEntry` already hydrates the draft from the server, so the screen *could* submit both `lunch` and `dinner`. Rejected as the primary fix: it makes every settings save a read-modify-write across a window two household members can interleave in (member A opens Lunch structure, member B saves Dinner structure, member A saves — and B's Dinner edit is overwritten by A's stale hydrate). This codebase has already paid explicit attention to exactly this class of race elsewhere (`lockMenu`/`lockMenuSlot`'s TOCTOU comments, §16.2.6).
- **(b, LOCKED) Server merges per top-level key.** `meal_structure = COALESCE(meal_structure, '{}'::jsonb) || $3::jsonb` when `$3` is non-null, `meal_structure` unchanged when it is null. A one-key patch replaces that key's object and leaves every sibling key untouched, inside the single `UPDATE`, with no read-modify-write window at all.

**Locked: (b).** Two bounded consequences, both named: the `||` operator merges only at the **top level**, so a patch carrying `{"lunch": {"carb": 1}}` still replaces Lunch's whole object (correct — the screen always submits all three of that meal's counts together, and a partial-role patch is not a shape any caller builds); and **removing** a meal type's entry from `meal_structure` is no longer expressible through this patch. Nothing needs that today (a household stops planning Dinner by turning it off in `mealsEnabled`, which is a separate column with intentionally different whole-list-replace semantics — see D3), and a future caller that does need it gets an explicit key rather than a silently destructive default. `meals_enabled`, `cuisine_tier1`, `cuisine_tier2_weights`, `dietary_tags`, `allergens` and `skip_ingredients` **keep `COALESCE` replace semantics unchanged** — they are JSONB arrays, where `||` means *concatenate*, which would be a worse bug than the one being fixed. Only `meal_structure` (an object) changes. This asymmetry is deliberate and is stated in `updateSettingsPartial`'s own doc comment, not left for a reader to infer from the SQL.

#### 19.2.5 D5 — meal-instance grouping is a pure regrouping of `plannedSlotsForDay`'s existing output, in a new domain module, and the four headers render in a fixed order

Today `_WeekBody` renders one `_DaySection` per weekday, whose slots come straight from `plannedSlotsForDay(settings, menu.itemsForDay(dayOfWeek))` as a **flat list** — Breakfast's one slot, then Lunch's carb/sabzi-dal/accompaniment instances, then Snacks', then Dinner's, with nothing in the UI marking where one meal ends and the next begins. The feedback is precisely that this reads as an undifferentiated list.

**Locked:** a new pure module `mobile/lib/features/menu/domain/meal_instance_plan.dart` exporting `List<MealInstanceGroup> groupSlotsByMealInstance(List<PlannedSlot> slots)`, where a `MealInstanceGroup` is `{ MealType mealType, List<PlannedSlot> slots }`. It **consumes `plannedSlotsForDay`'s output and derives nothing itself** — no second reading of `mealsEnabled`, no second decode of `mealStructure`, no second cap rule. That constraint is the whole point: §15.5.1 and §16.5.1 both name "the cap rule now lives in N places" as this feature area's standing risk, and a grouping function that re-derived counts would make it three places on the client alone. A meal type absent from `mealsEnabled` contributes no slots to `plannedSlotsForDay` and therefore **no group and no header** — a household that does not plan Snacks sees no Snacks header, and this falls out of the existing function rather than needing its own branch.

**Header order is fixed at Breakfast → Lunch → Snacks → Dinner**, matching the founder's own sketch and `MealType.values`' declaration order, which `plannedSlotsForDay` already iterates in — so grouping preserves order by construction and needs no sort. Within Lunch/Dinner, the sub-slot order is `MealSlot.values` (carb → sabzi/dal → accompaniment), again inherited, not re-sorted. The shape the founder sketched renders as:

```
Monday
├─ Breakfast          →  one standalone slot
├─ Lunch              →  carb ×N, sabzi/dal ×N, accompaniment ×N  (this household's configured counts)
├─ Snacks             →  one standalone slot
└─ Dinner             →  carb ×N, sabzi/dal ×N, accompaniment ×N
```

**Flagged as a judgment call, matching `weekly_plan_screen.dart:36–43`'s own precedent:** no wireframe asset for this grouping exists — the founder's ask is an ASCII tree, not a design. The rendering chosen (a small meal-instance header row per group, with that meal's slot cards indented beneath it, inside the existing `_DaySection`) is this plan's call, flagged here and repeated in the module's doc comment, not presented as locked design. The per-type counts in the founder's sketch (`Carb ×2`, `Sabzi/Dal ×3`, `Accompaniment ×2`) are **illustrative of one household's config, not new defaults** — `DEFAULT_MEAL_STRUCTURE` stays `{carb: 1, sabzi_dal: 2, accompaniment: 1}` (PRD §7.1), and nothing in this week changes it. **Only the three existing types appear**: no Sweet header, no Drink header, no fourth sub-slot type anywhere in the grouping or its labels (Q18).

#### 19.2.6 D6 — W13 reads live household settings, exactly as today; the switch to the menu's snapshot is W14's, in one slice, not smeared across two weeks

`_WeeklyPlanForHousehold` currently watches `currentHouseholdControllerProvider(householdId)` live (`mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/weekly_plan_screen.dart:110–112`) and passes `householdValue.settings` into `_WeekBody`. That is precisely the read path Q17 replaces. **Locked: W13 does not touch it.** The grouping module takes `PlannedSlot`s, not settings, so it is indifferent to where the settings came from — and W14 S4 changes exactly one expression (which settings object is handed to `plannedSlotsForDay`) with no change to grouping, rendering, or tests written this week. Splitting it the other way — having W13 half-introduce the snapshot — would put a schema change in a week whose slices are otherwise all UI, and would leave the server and client reading different sources for a week. This is the same "keep each slice's own agent pipeline and RED-test list focused" reasoning §18.4 used for the S2/S3 split.

### 19.3 Slice breakdown

#### S1 — `updateSettingsPartial`'s `meal_structure` merge (D4), server-side

- **Delivers:** `meal_structure = COALESCE(meal_structure, '{}'::jsonb) || $3::jsonb` when a `mealStructure` patch is present, unchanged when absent (D4, §19.2.4); every other column's `COALESCE` replace semantics left exactly as they are, with the asymmetry explained in `updateSettingsPartial`'s own doc comment. No migration, no SDL change, no new resolver — `updateHouseholdSettings`' argument shape is untouched.
- **Files:** `api/src/repositories/householdRepository.ts` (+ its existing `householdRepository.test.ts`).
- **Depends on:** nothing — starts immediately.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.0 hr / **Medium** — one SQL expression, but it is the live-bug fix and the only slice this week whose failure mode is silent data loss.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `database-reviewer` (mandatory on every SQL change) → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests** (real Testcontainers Postgres, per §3.2 — a mocked `pg` cannot exercise `||` semantics): **patching only `lunch` leaves `dinner`'s configured counts byte-identical** (the direct regression test for the live bug, in Q19's own wording); patching only `dinner` leaves `lunch` intact (the mirror case, so the fix is not accidentally direction-specific); patching `lunch` twice in a row keeps the second write's values (a merge must still overwrite the key it carries); a `mealStructure` patch against a row whose `meal_structure` is somehow `NULL` produces the patch itself, not a `NULL` result (the `COALESCE(…, '{}')` guard); **a `mealsEnabled` patch still replaces the whole array and does not concatenate** (a direct regression test for the asymmetry D4 locks — the most plausible way a future edit breaks this); an `updateSettingsPartial` call carrying no `mealStructure` at all leaves the column untouched; the existing `updateSettingsPartial` RLS-only regression test still passes unmodified.

#### S2 — Per-meal-type meal-structure editing (Lunch **and** Dinner)

- **Delivers:** `MealStructureScreen` parameterised by `MealType` (Lunch or Dinner) instead of hardcoding Lunch; its patch carries that meal type's key only (unchanged shape — S1 is what makes it safe); Settings hub's single "Meal structure" row becomes two rows, "Lunch structure" and "Dinner structure", each routed through `HouseholdEditEntry` exactly as the four existing edit rows are; the screen's footnote about dinner being edited separately updated from a promise into a cross-reference; `AppRoutes.editMealStructure(householdId)` gains a meal-type segment, with the old single-argument form removed rather than left as a silently-Lunch alias.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/household/presentation/create/meal_structure_screen.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/household/presentation/settings/settings_hub_screen.dart`; `mobile/lib/app/router.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/household/state/household_wizard_controller.dart` (draft patch keyed by meal type).
- **Depends on:** S1 (the merge is what makes a one-key patch non-destructive; shipping this screen first would double the blast radius of the live bug).
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Medium — the create wizard drives the same screen, and its Lunch-only step must keep behaving identically.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips (no new I/O, no new trust boundary).
- **RED tests:** the screen opened for Dinner shows Dinner's stored counts, not Lunch's; saving from the Dinner screen submits a patch whose `mealStructure` carries **only** the `dinner` key; saving from the Lunch screen still carries only `lunch` (the wizard's own path, unchanged); the create wizard's step 2/4 still renders Lunch and still advances to the next wizard step (a direct regression against the shared-screen change); Settings shows two structure rows, each navigating to its own meal type; no route can reach this screen without a meal type (a missing/invalid segment renders the router's existing error state, matching `router.dart`'s `_householdId` fallback discipline, not a silent default to Lunch).

#### S3 — `mealsEnabled` editable from Settings, all four meal types (closes the W5-era Snacks gap)

- **Delivers:** `AppRoutes.editMeals(householdId)` + a "Meals to plan" Settings row above the two structure rows; `WhichMealsScreen` rendered through `HouseholdEditEntry` in `WizardFlow.edit`, offering **`MealType.values`** (all four) in edit mode while the create wizard keeps `wizardMealTypes` (three) — the flow context the screen already receives is what selects between them, so no second screen is created; `meal_type.dart`'s doc comment corrected from *"Snacks remains reachable through the Settings → Meal structure screen (wireframe 4.1), which is a later slice"* into a statement of what actually exists, citing `W13 S3` (a §11.2.10-class stale-claim fix, made in the PR that makes it false, not deferred to a docs pass).
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/app/router.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/household/presentation/settings/settings_hub_screen.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/household/presentation/create/which_meals_screen.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/household/domain/meal_type.dart` (doc comment + an `editMealTypes` constant alongside `wizardMealTypes`).
- **Depends on:** nothing structurally, but lands after S1 so that a household toggling Snacks on cannot be the first user of a still-broken settings write path.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Low-Medium — the adapter and the mutation both already exist; this is genuinely a route entry plus a list constant, as `HouseholdEditEntry`'s own doc predicted.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** the edit screen offers **four** toggles including Snacks; the create wizard still offers exactly three (a direct regression — the wizard is deliberately not changed, D3); enabling Snacks from Settings and saving produces a `mealsEnabled` patch containing `snacks`; a household that had Snacks cleared by the three-toggle wizard can turn it back on and it persists across a reload (the end-to-end statement of the gap this closes); the screen's existing "at least one meal" validation, whatever it currently does, is asserted rather than changed; `meal_type.dart` no longer claims a path that does not exist (a review check, named here so it is not forgotten rather than left to chance).

#### S4 — Meal-instance grouping domain module (pure, no widget)

- **Delivers:** `mobile/lib/features/menu/domain/meal_instance_plan.dart` — `MealInstanceGroup` and `groupSlotsByMealInstance(List<PlannedSlot>)` per D5 (§19.2.5): a stable regrouping of `plannedSlotsForDay`'s output, deriving nothing, sorting nothing, with the four-header order inherited from `MealType.values`.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/menu/domain/meal_instance_plan.dart` (+ test).
- **Depends on:** nothing — a pure function over an already-shipped type; starts immediately, in parallel with S1.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.0 hr / Low.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips (no I/O).
- **RED tests:** a full four-meal day groups into exactly four groups in Breakfast → Lunch → Snacks → Dinner order; a household with Snacks disabled produces **three** groups and no empty Snacks group (the header must not render for a meal that is not planned); a household with Lunch disabled but Dinner enabled produces groups for the remaining three, in order; within a Lunch group, slots stay in carb → sabzi/dal → accompaniment order with the configured multiplicity preserved (`{carb: 2, sabzi_dal: 3, accompaniment: 2}` yields 2 + 3 + 2 = 7 slots in one Lunch group, filled and empty interleaved exactly as `plannedSlotsForDay` returned them); an empty input list produces zero groups, not four empty ones; the function never inspects `HouseholdSettings` (asserted structurally — its signature takes no settings argument, which *is* the enforcement, per D5).

#### S5 — Weekly plan renders the four meal-instance headers

- **Delivers:** `_DaySection` renders `groupSlotsByMealInstance(...)` output — a meal-instance header per group with that group's slot cards beneath it — instead of a flat slot list; the empty-slot "+" affordance continues to name the *type* (`+ Carb`, `+ Sabzi/Dal`, `+ Accompaniment`, `+ Breakfast`, `+ Snacks`), now under its meal header rather than carrying the meal name itself; `weekly_plan_screen.dart`'s doc comment gains the D5 judgment-call flag alongside its existing seven-stacked-days one.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/weekly_plan_screen.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/meal_slot_card.dart` (label only, if the meal name is currently repeated per card).
- **Depends on:** S4.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / **Medium** — the highest-visibility change of the week, on the screen the feedback was actually about.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** a day with all four meals enabled renders four header widgets in the locked order; a household with Snacks disabled renders three headers and no Snacks header anywhere in the tree; a Lunch group configured `{carb: 2, sabzi_dal: 3, accompaniment: 2}` renders seven slot cards under the Lunch header and none under any other; tapping an empty slot under Dinner still opens the picker with `mealSlot: dinner` and the correct `slotRole` (a direct regression — the grouping must not change what a tap means); a filled slot still renders its recipe and still routes on tap; the screen's loading/error/empty states are unchanged (the grouping sits strictly inside the resolved-data branch); no header or label anywhere in the rendered tree names Sweet or Drink (Q18, asserted rather than assumed).

#### S6 — Welcome screen + first-run activity threshold (Q20/D1/D2), landing 43/50

- **Delivers:** `WelcomeChoosePathScreen` at `AppRoutes.welcome` (`/welcome`) per D1 (§19.2.1) — the headline "Welcome!", the question "What would you like to do first?", and two CTAs, "Add to Pantry" and "Add New Recipe", built on `first_run_choose_path_screen.dart`'s `_PathCard` shape; the activity predicate per D2 (§19.2.2) — ≥1 recipe OR ≥1 pantry item OR ≥1 planned menu item, computed from already-loaded providers, hold-on-splash while any source is still resolving, no new schema and no new query; `_redirect` sends a signed-in user **with** a household to `/welcome` when the predicate is false and to `/home` when it is true, leaving the existing no-household → `/first-run` branch untouched; the file's doc comment records which of the two placements D2 left open was actually taken, and why.
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/onboarding/presentation/welcome_choose_path_screen.dart` (new); `mobile/lib/app/router.dart`; possibly `mobile/lib/features/household/state/household_activity_provider.dart` (new, if this slice's author takes the derived-provider option D2 flags).
- **Depends on:** nothing structurally; scheduled after S1–S5 so the surfaces it sends first-time users *into* are already correct.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / **Medium** — the screen itself is trivially small; the risk is entirely in `_redirect`, a function W8 S1 already had to rewrite once and which is load-bearing for every signed-in session.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips.
- **RED tests** (driven through W8 S1's existing router test harness, not a new one): a signed-in user with a household and **zero** recipes/pantry items/menu items lands on `/welcome`; the same user with **exactly one recipe** lands on `/home`; with exactly one pantry item and nothing else, `/home`; with exactly one planned menu item and nothing else, `/home` (all three arms of Q20's OR asserted separately, never inferred from one another); **while any of the three sources is still resolving, the user holds on splash and never flashes `/welcome`** (the direct regression for D2's non-negotiable half); a signed-in user with **no household** still lands on `/first-run`, unchanged (W8 S1's own behaviour, asserted so this slice cannot silently regress it); an errored recipes/pantry/menu query is treated as "unknown, hold" rather than "empty, show welcome" — a returning household must never be bounced to a first-run screen by a network blip; the welcome screen's two CTAs navigate to the pantry add-method and recipe-method routes respectively; the screen renders no loading or error state of its own (structural — it performs no async work, D1).

#### S7 — Real-AWS verification + weekly doc pass

- **Delivers:** direct-Lambda-invoke verification of S1 against real dev Aurora (the merge is the only server-side change this week, and the only one whose failure is silent): a throwaway household seeded through real resolvers, `updateHouseholdSettings` called with a Lunch-only `mealStructure` patch, and a follow-up `Query.household` confirming Dinner's counts survive byte-identical — the RUNBOOK.md §2 method every prior week's S7/S9/S12 pass has used, throwaway household deleted afterward, synthetic identities recorded per §17.9's own process-gap fix; §4.2's mandatory weekly pass — actual-vs-planned hours into §4's W13 row, a decisions-versus-shipped audit of §19.7's table; `doc-updater` re-sync of `SYSTEM_DESIGN.md` §6.1's `updateHouseholdSettings` block if its comment describes replace semantics for `mealStructure`.
- **Files:** `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md` (§4 row, a W13-result subsection); `docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md` (§6.1 re-sync only if needed).
- **Depends on:** all slices.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.0 hr / Low, non-optional.
- **Agents:** `doc-updater`.
- **Verification checks:** the Lunch-only patch preserves Dinner (live, not only Testcontainers); a `mealsEnabled` patch still replaces rather than concatenates (live — the asymmetry is the most surprising thing this week ships); non-member denial on `updateHouseholdSettings` unchanged; explicit `null` on `mealStructure` leaves the column untouched (§11.5.5's standing explicit-null convention, this week's exposure).

**Planned total: ~10.0 hrs** against §4's nominal ~10 — the only week since W5 to land on its nominal budget *in planning*, because five of its seven slices are UI work against fully-shipped server surfaces and it ships **no migration, no new resolver, and no new subscription**. That is not slack: §7's amended budget note applies unchanged, and Phase 3b's two weeks are additive to the MVP date regardless of how tidily W13 itself fits.

### 19.4 Sequencing

```
S1 (updateSettingsPartial merge fix, server)     S4 (grouping domain module, pure)
  │                                                │
  ├──────────────┐                                 ▼
  ▼              ▼                              S5 (Weekly plan renders
S2 (per-meal-  S3 (mealsEnabled                     four meal headers)
    type          editable in                        │
    structure     Settings, all 4)                   │
    editing)      │                                  │
  └──────┬────────┘                                  │
         └────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                          ▼
               S6 (welcome screen + activity threshold)
                          │
                          ▼
            S7 (real-AWS verification + doc pass)
```

S1 and S4 are fully independent and both start on day one — different languages, different halves of the repo, zero shared files. S2 and S3 depend on S1 for *safety* ordering rather than compilation: either could technically merge first, but shipping a settings-editing surface before the write path stops clobbering siblings would hand users a second way to trigger the live bug, so the dependency is real even though a compiler would not enforce it. S6 is scheduled last among the feature slices deliberately — it is the screen that *sends first-time users into* the surfaces S2/S3/S5 fix, and pointing new users at a half-corrected Weekly plan or a Settings screen that still zeroes Dinner would be the worst possible ordering of the same week's work.

### 19.5 Risks

#### 19.5.1 `_redirect` is the highest-blast-radius function this week touches, and it is touched by one of the smallest slices

Every signed-in session passes through `router.dart`'s `_redirect` on every navigation. W8 S1 already rewrote it once (§14.2.11 — the unconditional `/first-run` stopgap) and gave it a dedicated test harness precisely because its failure mode is "nobody can reach the app." S6 adds a fourth landing decision driven by three providers rather than one, which multiplies the "still resolving" combinations it must handle correctly from two to eight. Mitigation: the hold-on-splash rule is locked, not optional (D2); the three OR arms are asserted separately rather than as one combined case; and the existing "no household → `/first-run`" branch gets its own regression test in this slice even though the slice does not intend to change it. Residual risk accepted: a *slow* three-provider resolve makes the splash hold measurably longer on cold start than today's single-provider one — a latency regression, not a correctness one, and one the founder should look at on real hardware during S7 rather than trust to a simulator.

#### 19.5.2 The merge fix changes semantics for a column that already has data in it

S1 changes how an *existing*, deployed write path behaves. Every household created before this week has a `meal_structure` document written by `insertDefaultSettings`, and possibly a Lunch-only one written by the clobbering path — meaning **the bug's damage is already done for any household that edited Lunch structure after creation**, and the merge fix does not repair it. It only stops the next edit from doing it again. Mitigation: named here rather than discovered later; a household that has lost its Dinner counts recovers by opening the new "Dinner structure" row (S2) and setting them, which is now a real path where before there was none. No data-repair migration is written — the correct values are unknowable from the row itself (a zeroed Dinner is indistinguishable from a household that genuinely wants no dinner planning), and inventing them would violate the same "never invent data for a value nobody supplied" posture `pantryUnits.ts`/`pantryCategories.ts`/`defaultExpiry.ts` all already hold to.

#### 19.5.3 The grouping is the first UI change to a screen the founder is actively using daily

W12's gate is "founder household using daily," and the Weekly plan is a screen that gate lives on. S5 restructures its per-day body. Unlike every prior week's UI work, this is not a new screen nobody depends on yet — a regression here is immediately visible in real use, and the feedback that prompted it came from that same real use. Mitigation: S4's pure module carries the logic and is fully unit-tested before any widget changes; S5's RED list leads with the two behaviours most likely to break silently (an empty-slot tap still carries the right `mealSlot`/`slotRole`; a filled slot still renders and still routes) rather than only asserting that the new headers exist.

#### 19.5.4 Two weeks of feedback response push the MVP date, and this week's tidy ~10-hour estimate makes that easy to forget

W13 plans at nominal budget, which is unusual for this project and could read as "this was free." It was not: §7's amended table adds ~20 hrs and two calendar weeks against a buffer that has been gone since W7 (§14.5.1's own heading: *"there is no buffer left at all"*). The mitigation is stated rather than structural — §7's budget note, Phase 3b's DoD closing line, and this risk row all say the same thing in three places on purpose, because the failure mode is a future reader concluding the schedule absorbed this.

All closed — verified live against real dev Aurora and audited against what shipped in §19.8's S7 pass; nothing here was left checked by assumption.

- [x] "Meals to plan" is reachable from Settings and offers **all four** meal types; a household whose Snacks was cleared by the three-toggle wizard can turn it back on and it persists — the W5-era "Snacks UI unreachability" gap (§11.1) is closed (S3)
- [x] `meal_type.dart`'s doc comment no longer claims a Settings path that does not exist (S3)
- [x] `groupSlotsByMealInstance` derives nothing from `HouseholdSettings` — its signature takes no settings argument, and the cap rule gains no third client-side home (S4)
- [x] The Weekly plan renders four meal-instance headers per day in Breakfast → Lunch → Snacks → Dinner order, renders no header for a disabled meal, and preserves per-type sub-slot multiplicity under Lunch/Dinner (S4, S5)

### 19.7 W13 locked decisions

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| **D1** | What shape is the welcome screen, and what does it say about the empty library? | **A routed `ConsumerWidget` at `/welcome`**, built on `first_run_choose_path_screen.dart`'s two-`_PathCard` precedent, no mutations and therefore no loading/error states. Its copy asks the user to add *their* first recipe rather than promising a catalog, and its doc comment forward-references W15/W16 as the point where that CTA's target state changes (Q16). |
| **D2** | Where is Q20's "any real activity" predicate computed? | **In `router.dart`'s existing `_redirect`, from already-loaded providers** — no schema, no dedicated query, no dismiss flag. Inline-vs-derived-provider is an explicitly **flagged judgment call**, decided by S6's author and recorded in that file's doc. Non-negotiable either way: hold on splash while any source is resolving; treat an errored source as unknown, never as empty. **Shipped (§19.8 audit): the derived-provider option was taken** — `mobile/lib/features/household/state/household_activity_provider.dart`, whose own doc comment names the choice and both reasons for it (readability of an already-longest `_redirect`; direct unit-testability of the OR's 8-way truth table without a router/widget harness) — matching this row's own stated lean exactly, not a deviation. |
| **D3** | Does `mealsEnabled` become editable, and for how many meal types? | **Yes, from Settings, all four** (`MealType.values`) via `HouseholdEditEntry` + `WhichMealsScreen` in edit mode. The create wizard keeps its three toggles unchanged. Meal-structure editing becomes per-meal-type: two Settings rows, Lunch and Dinner. Breakfast/Snacks get no structure row (single-item meals, `SINGLE_ITEM_MEAL_SLOTS`). |
| **D4** | Where is the Dinner-clobber bug fixed — client patch or server SQL? | **Server SQL.** `meal_structure = COALESCE(meal_structure, '{}'::jsonb) \|\| $3::jsonb`, merging at the top level, inside the single `UPDATE`. Rejected: rebuilding the whole document client-side (a read-modify-write window two members can interleave in). **Only `meal_structure` changes** — every JSONB *array* column keeps `COALESCE` replace, because `\|\|` on an array concatenates, which would be a worse bug than the one being fixed. |
| **D5** | How is meal-instance grouping computed? | **A pure regrouping of `plannedSlotsForDay`'s existing output** in a new `meal_instance_plan.dart`, deriving nothing and sorting nothing — the cap rule gains no third client-side home (§15.5.1/§16.5.1's standing drift risk). Header order Breakfast → Lunch → Snacks → Dinner, inherited from `MealType.values`. Rendering is a **flagged judgment call** (no wireframe exists), same posture as `weekly_plan_screen.dart:36–43`'s own. The founder's `×2`/`×3` sketch is illustrative of one household's config; `DEFAULT_MEAL_STRUCTURE` stays `{carb: 1, sabzi_dal: 2, accompaniment: 1}`. |
| **D6** | Does W13 start reading the menu's config snapshot? | **No.** W13 reads live household settings exactly as today (`weekly_plan_screen.dart:110–112`); the entire switch is W14 S4, one expression, in the week that introduces the column. No half-migrated state across a week boundary. |

**Decision density: 6 locked** — lower than W12's 13, because five of this week's six founder-facing questions were answered *before* the plan was written (Q16–Q21, §10) and this table records only how each is realised. Zero of the six reverse a drafting-pass recommendation; D2 and D5 each deliberately leave one sub-choice **open and flagged** rather than locking a call this plan has neither a wireframe nor profiling data to make.

### 19.8 S7 result — real-AWS verification of the D4 merge fix + weekly doc pass

**Deploy at time of this pass — found stale, redeployed before verifying.** `AWS_PROFILE=parimaan-dev aws sts get-caller-identity` confirmed account `917246556431`/`ap-south-1` before anything else. `aws lambda get-function --function-name Parimaan-dev-Api-UpdateHouseholdSettingsFnFDC1560F-92wKKRodCy3p` showed `LastModified: 2026-09-06T20:09:38Z`, and downloading and inspecting the deployed bundle directly (`code.zip` → `index.js`) confirmed it still carried the **pre-S1** `meal_structure = COALESCE($3::jsonb, meal_structure)` (whole-column replace) — i.e. the dev stack predated `12737ac` (S1's merge-fix PR, merged 2026-09-07 19:37 IST). `cdk diff Parimaan-dev-Api` (with `-c googleClientId=...` resolved live and non-secretly from the deployed User Pool's own Google IdP config via `aws cognito-idp describe-identity-provider`, since `dev_config.dart`/the password manager copy wasn't available to this pass) showed exactly one functional change — `UpdateHouseholdSettingsFn`'s code asset — plus a `MigrationRunnerFn` code-hash-only rebuild with no template/resource/IAM change either. No replacement, no destructive change, nothing outside what S1–S6 actually shipped. Deployed via `cdk deploy Parimaan-dev-Api` (dependency stacks Network/Auth/Data included automatically; Network and Auth reported "no changes", Data updated only `MigrationRunnerFn`'s code asset). Post-deploy, re-downloading the bundle confirmed the merge fix (`meal_structure = CASE WHEN $3::jsonb IS NULL THEN meal_structure ELSE COALESCE(meal_structure, '{}'::jsonb) || $3::jsonb END`) is now live.

**Method:** identical to `RUNBOOK.md` §2 / W11 S9 / W12 S7's own precedent (§17.9, §18.8) — direct Lambda invoke with an AppSync-resolver-shaped `{ arguments, identity }` event and a synthetic Cognito identity (fresh UUID `sub`/`username`, `claims.email` a `<uuid>@example.test`), driven from a throwaway Node script (kept in the session scratchpad, not committed) that shells out to `aws lambda invoke --payload fileb://...` per call. One throwaway household was built end-to-end through real resolvers only — `createHousehold` → `updateHouseholdSettings` (×4, see checks below) → `deleteHousehold` — and fully deleted at the end of the pass. A second synthetic identity (never added to the household) was used for the non-member-denial check. No pre-existing household, user, recipe, or menu was read or modified.

**Synthetic identities used (recorded explicitly, per `RUNBOOK.md` §2's own W11 S9 process-gap fix, §17.9):** owner `sub` `42c965d0-7ce7-447e-b6b0-1cea55aa038e`; outsider `sub` `df36b6b3-0152-4efc-a4f0-5798dbffa8dd`; household id `7807905b-1410-4355-9616-dca875eb2fa9`, name `W13-S7-throwaway-2c64faa9` (deleted, confirmed below).

**Verified live — every check below is a real invoke against real dev Aurora/AppSync, not a simulation:**

1. **The Lunch-only patch preserves Dinner, byte-identical — the direct regression test for the bug S1 fixed.** Baseline set via one `updateHouseholdSettings` call: `mealsEnabled: [breakfast, lunch, snacks, dinner]`, `mealStructure: {lunch: {carb:1, sabzi_dal:2, accompaniment:1}, dinner: {carb:2, sabzi_dal:3, accompaniment:2}}`. A second call patched **only** `mealStructure: {lunch: {carb:5, sabzi_dal:6, accompaniment:7}}`. The mutation's own response and a follow-up `Query.household` both show `dinner: {carb:2, sabzi_dal:3, accompaniment:2}` — **exactly the pre-patch values, untouched** — while `lunch` correctly updated to `{carb:5, sabzi_dal:6, accompaniment:7}`. Pre-S1 (the stale bundle found above), this exact sequence would have zeroed Dinner; post-deploy, it does not.
2. **A `mealsEnabled` patch still replaces the whole array, never concatenates — the asymmetry D4 locks, proven live, not just asserted.** A third call patched `mealsEnabled: [lunch, dinner]` against the 4-element baseline (`[breakfast, lunch, snacks, dinner]`). The response and a follow-up `Query.household` both show `mealsEnabled: [lunch, dinner]` — **exactly 2 elements**, not 4, not 6. A concatenating (`||`-on-array) implementation would have produced at least 4 elements (a dedup'd union) or 6 (a raw concat); this is the sharpest live disambiguator, and it landed on replace.
3. **Non-member denial on `updateHouseholdSettings` is unchanged.** The outsider identity's call (`allergens: [peanuts]`) against the same household returned `FORBIDDEN: "You are not a member of this household."` — `requireHouseholdMember`'s exact, unmodified `DENIAL_MESSAGE`, identical to every other household-scoped resolver's denial.
4. **Explicit `null` on `mealStructure` — found to be REJECTED at the Zod validation layer, not "leaves the column untouched."** This is a correction to this slice's own `§19.3 S7`/`§19.6` wording, made after reading the actual code rather than assuming: `api/src/validation/updateHouseholdSettings.ts`'s `householdSettingsInputSchema` declares every field `.optional()`, deliberately **not** `.nullable()`/`.nullish()` — the file's own doc comment states the convention explicitly ("an explicit `null` is rejected as a `ValidationError` because 'clear this field' isn't a supported operation yet"), matching the codebase-wide convention `§12.2.10`/line 765 already locks for `updateHouseholdSettings`/`updatePantryItem`/`updateRecipe`'s patch inputs. Live: `updateHouseholdSettings(mealStructure: null)` returned `VALIDATION: "Invalid input: expected record, received null"` — the request never reaches `updateSettingsPartial`'s SQL at all. **What §19.2.4/D4 actually describes at the SQL layer is real** (`CASE WHEN $3::jsonb IS NULL THEN meal_structure ELSE ... END` genuinely would leave the column untouched if a `NULL` bind ever reached it — confirmed by reading the shipped SQL directly, `householdRepository.ts:513–516`) — **but that branch is defensive/unreachable through the public mutation today**, since Zod rejects the input one layer up. Recorded here so a future reader doesn't conclude either "the SQL guard is dead code, delete it" (it is the correct behavior *if* a caller ever bypasses Zod, e.g. a future internal caller of `updateSettingsPartial` directly) or "explicit null clears nothing silently" (it does not — it is rejected outright, loudly, exactly per the standing convention). A follow-up `Query.household` after the rejected call confirmed the household's settings were completely unaffected by the failed attempt (still `mealsEnabled: [lunch, dinner]`, `mealStructure` unchanged from check 2).
5. **Cleanup — complete and confirmed, with one live nuance worth recording.** `deleteHousehold(householdId, confirmationName: "W13-S7-throwaway-2c64faa9")` returned `true`. An **immediate** follow-up `Query.household` (same owner identity, same warm invoke sequence, well inside `requireHouseholdMember`'s 30s membership-cache TTL) returned `NOT_FOUND: "Household not found."` rather than the expected no-existence-oracle `FORBIDDEN` denial — because `HouseholdFn`'s own Lambda container still held a **positive**, unevicted cache entry for this owner/household pair from the three earlier `Query.household` calls in this same pass (cache eviction on `deleteHousehold` is container-local per `requireHouseholdMember.ts`'s own doc, and `DeleteHouseholdFn` runs in a *separate* Lambda function/container from `HouseholdFn`, so it can never reach `HouseholdFn`'s cache). Re-querying the same id/identity after a 35s sleep (past the TTL) returned the expected `FORBIDDEN: "You are not a member of this household."` — confirming the household is genuinely gone and the first response was a real, documented artifact of the per-container membership cache's accepted staleness window (`requireHouseholdMember.ts`'s own "≤30s stale-authorization window is accepted, in writing" comment), not a regression or a new gap. Worth flagging for `RUNBOOK.md` §2's own procedure text: a cleanup-verification query run *immediately* after `deleteHousehold`, from a Lambda that has recently queried the same household, can observe `NOT_FOUND` instead of the documented no-existence-oracle `FORBIDDEN` — both are correct, but only one matches step 7's stated expectation, and a future pass should not read the `NOT_FOUND` variant as a bug.

**No CRITICAL or HIGH findings against the API, and no regression.** The one genuine miss this pass caught was **process, not product**: the dev stack was stale for the exact Lambda this pass exists to verify, which would have made every one of checks 1–2 falsely pass against the *old* whole-replace behavior had the stale-bundle check not been done first. `RUNBOOK.md` §2 doesn't currently instruct a pass to confirm the deployed bundle actually contains the change under test before invoking it — every prior week's S7/S9/S12 write-up describes redeploying when a stack is found stale (W8 S12, this week), but none states "download and grep the deployed bundle" as a standing step. Named here as a real, cheap addition worth folding into §2 the next time RUNBOOK.md's procedure text itself is revised, not applied unilaterally in this doc-only pass.

## 20. Week 14: Meal-config snapshot per menu + pantry curated item picker

**Status:** LOCKED. Written 2026-09-07 alongside §19, against the same six founder answers (§10, Q16–Q21); **amended 2026-09-10** to add S8 (copy/clear day-week, PRD §7.1), scheduled into this week per the founder's own call when the W13 close-out audit surfaced it as the one PRD requirement with no assigned week anywhere in the 28-week plan. **D-numbers are W14-local** (D1–D8, D8 added in the same amendment).

### 20.1 What W14 is locked to deliver

Per §4's newly-inserted W14 row: `menus.meal_config_snapshot` (migration + backfill); every generation/placement read path switched from live `findSettingsForHousehold` to the menu's own snapshot once a menu exists; a client-side curated per-category pantry item list with a multi-select picker and a unit-restricted quantity stepper; the first mobile caller of `bulkAddPantryItems`; and **copy day / copy week / clear day / clear week** (S8, PRD §7.1 — added in the 2026-09-10 amendment). **No new wireframe screen** — the count stays at 43/50 (§4's screen-count amendment, §20.2.6; S8 adds no screen either, §20.2.8). Gate, in §4's own words: "A settings edit mid-week never changes the week already in progress; ticking curated items writes them in one `bulkAddPantryItems` call" — S8's own gate, added with it: "clearing or copying a day/week never touches an item the household already cooked."

**Forward-references this plan accounts for** (via `grep -n "W14" docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md` against the post-renumber doc, plus the four live read paths the feedback investigation named — every one addressed below):

| # | Source | What it commits W14 to |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | §4 W14 row (new) | Snapshot column, read-path switch, curated picker, `bulkAddPantryItems` wiring |
| 2 | §10 Q17 | Config versioning is a **snapshot onto the menu at creation time**, not an effective-date column and not a settings-version table |
| 3 | §10 Q21 | The curated list is hardcoded in the Flutter client — no table, no resolver — and the commit path is the already-shipped `bulkAddPantryItems` |
| 4 | §10 Q18 | The snapshot carries exactly the three existing types under `lunch`/`dinner`; snapshotting a config shape is not an invitation to widen it |
| 5 | §11.2.1 decision 2 (W5) | `bulkAddPantryItems` is deliberately **not** on `onPantryChanged`'s `@aws_subscribe` list ("a list-shaped payload can't fan out to that subscription's single-`PantryItem` shape"). W14 becomes its first mobile caller and **inherits that gap unchanged** (§20.2.7) |
| 6 | §11.2.4 / D-Option-C | `pantryUnits.ts`/`pantryCategories.ts` are *"a guess from `PRD.md` §7.1, not a locked taxonomy — meant to grow via edits here, never a migration"* — the exact precedent Q21 cites for putting the curated list in code |
| 7 | `api/src/domain/pantryUnits.ts:10–21` | `KNOWN_PANTRY_UNITS` = `g, kg, ml, l, piece, packet, bunch, tsp, tbsp, cup` — the ten the quantity stepper is restricted to, read from the existing constant (and its hand-mirrored `mobile/lib/features/pantry/domain/pantry_unit.dart`), never re-listed |
| 8 | `api/src/resolvers/addMenuItem.ts:57–77` | `validateAddMenuItem` calls `findSettingsForHousehold(client, householdId)` live and feeds it to `isMealSlotEnabled` + `getMealSlotCap` — read path #1 to switch |
| 9 | `api/src/resolvers/autoFillWeek.ts:172` | Same live call inside `withUserTransaction`, feeding the commit-time cap re-validation — read path #2 |
| 10 | `api/src/resolvers/autoFillPreview.ts:156` | Same live call, feeding `enumerateEmptySlots` — read path #3 |
| 11 | `mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/weekly_plan_screen.dart:110–112` | `_WeeklyPlanForHousehold` watches `currentHouseholdControllerProvider` **live** and passes `householdValue.settings` to `plannedSlotsForDay` — read path #4, the client one |
| 12 | §15.5.2 / `shared/schema.graphql`'s `MenuItem` doc | *"`slotRole` is captured at placement time and does NOT track the recipe's own `role` if that's changed later … a menu is a snapshot of what was planned, not a live view"* — the precedent this week extends from one column to the whole config |
| 13 | §16.2.6 / §16.5.1 | `lockMenu`/`lockMenuSlot` ordering and "the cap rule now lives in three places" — the snapshot changes the *source* the rule reads in each place, and must not add a fourth place or reorder a lock |
| 14 | §19.2.6 / W13 D6 | W13 deliberately left the client read path alone so this week can switch it in one expression |
