| 16 | `docs/PRD.md` §7.1 | *"Copy day, copy week, clear day, clear week"* — flagged twice earlier in this plan (§11.1, §19.1) as unscheduled; **added here as S8, 2026-09-10** (§20.2.8), the one PRD MVP requirement this plan otherwise had no week for |

**Explicitly out of scope for W14** (owned elsewhere, not oversights): making `mealsEnabled`/`mealStructure` editable at all (**W13**, §19.2.3 — this week assumes that surface exists and snapshots whatever it writes); a UI that *shows* a household its current week is running on an older config (deliberately not built — §20.5.2 argues why a silent, correct behaviour beats a half-designed explanatory banner, and names it as the first thing to add if beta users are confused by it); reconciling menu items already placed above a cap that a later snapshot lowers (structurally unreachable for the current week once this ships, since the current week's caps can no longer change); `onPantryChanged` coverage for `bulkAddPantryItems` (**still W20's open item**, §11.2.1, inherited not fixed — §20.2.7); a server-side or database-backed curated item list (**never**, Q21); AI-suggested pantry items (photo pantry is W20); the 50-recipe curated library (**W15/W16**); undo for any of the four new copy/clear mutations (**never locked for any menu mutation**, consistent with W9–W13, §20.2.8); cross-household copy (**structurally impossible** — `copyMenuWeek`/`copyMenuDay` only ever target a week within the calling household's own menus, §20.2.8).

### 20.2 Design

#### 20.2.1 D1 — one combined `meal_config_snapshot` JSONB column on `menus`, written at menu creation, never updated

Q17 locks *snapshot onto the menu*; the column shape was left to this plan. Two candidates: two columns (`meals_enabled_snapshot` JSONB + `meal_structure_snapshot` JSONB), mirroring `household_settings`' own two columns, or one combined document.

**Locked: one column, `meal_config_snapshot JSONB NOT NULL`**, holding `{"mealsEnabled": [...], "mealStructure": {...}, "snapshotAt": "<ISO timestamp>"}`. Reasons, in order of weight:

1. **It cannot be half-written.** The two halves are only meaningful together — a `mealStructure` describing Dinner's counts is meaningless if `mealsEnabled` says Dinner is off. One column makes "write both or neither" a property of the column rather than a discipline every future writer must remember. Two columns would need exactly the atomicity argument §18.2.2's transaction reasoning already makes elsewhere, for no benefit.
2. **It matches the convention this codebase already has.** `household_settings.meal_structure` is already a free-shaped JSONB document read defensively (`getMealSlotCap`'s *"this is `AWSJSON` — no DB-level shape guarantee beyond 'valid JSON'"*, and `meal_slot_plan.dart`'s `_decodeMealStructure` mirroring it). The snapshot is the same class of data and gets the same treatment, including the same fail-closed decoding: a malformed or missing key caps at **zero**, never at a "reasonable default" (`getMealSlotCap`'s own locked posture, unchanged).
3. **`snapshotAt` costs nothing and answers the only forensic question anyone will ask** — *when* did this week's config freeze — without a second column or a join.

**Never updated after insert.** There is no `updateMenuConfigSnapshot`, no resolver argument that can set it, and no code path that rewrites it. That is the entire behavioural guarantee Q17 asks for, expressed as an absence rather than as a rule someone must follow.

#### 20.2.2 D2 — the backfill for existing `menus` rows uses the household's current settings, and says so

`meal_config_snapshot` is `NOT NULL`, so the migration must fill every existing row. There is no honest way to reconstruct what a household's config was at the moment a past menu was created — `household_settings` keeps no history, which is the whole reason this column is being added.

**Locked:** the migration backfills each existing `menus` row from its household's **current** `household_settings` (`UPDATE menus SET meal_config_snapshot = ... FROM household_settings WHERE household_settings.household_id = menus.household_id`), with `snapshotAt` set to the migration's own run timestamp rather than to a fabricated creation time. This is exactly the pre-existing behaviour (every past menu was already being read against live settings), so the backfill changes nothing about how those weeks behave — it just writes down what they were already doing. The migration's own comment states this plainly: **backfilled rows are an approximation by construction and are not evidence of what the config was when those weeks were planned.** No attempt is made to infer past config from the menu items themselves; that would be inventing data, which this codebase declines to do everywhere else (`defaultExpiry.ts`'s `other: null`, `canonicalizePantryUnit`'s pass-through).

The blast radius is small and worth stating: at the time of writing, the only real menus are the founder's household's, plus throwaway dev rows. A `menus` row whose household somehow has no settings row is impossible under the current schema (`insertDefaultSettings` runs inside `createHousehold`'s transaction), but the migration still fails loudly rather than defaulting, per the same fail-closed posture as everything else in this area.

#### 20.2.3 D3 — every read path switches to "the menu's snapshot if a menu exists, live settings only when creating one"

The rule, stated once, applied identically in four places:

> **Creating** a menu reads live `household_settings` (that read *is* the snapshot). **Everything else** that needs `mealsEnabled`/`mealStructure` for a given week reads that week's menu's `meal_config_snapshot` and never calls `findSettingsForHousehold` for it.

| # | Path | Today | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `addMenuItem.ts:57–77` (`validateAddMenuItem`) | `findSettingsForHousehold(client, householdId)` → `isMealSlotEnabled` + `getMealSlotCap` | the already-fetched menu's `meal_config_snapshot` → the same two functions, unchanged |
| 2 | `autoFillWeek.ts:172` | same live call inside the transaction, after `lockMenu` | the menu row it already loaded (`findMenuById`) carries the snapshot — **one fewer query**, not one more |
| 3 | `autoFillPreview.ts:156` | same live call → `enumerateEmptySlots` | same substitution |
| 4 | `weekly_plan_screen.dart:110–112` | watches `currentHouseholdControllerProvider` live, passes `household.settings` into `plannedSlotsForDay` | passes the menu's snapshot-derived settings instead; W13 D6 left this as literally one expression to change |

**What deliberately does not change:** `isMealSlotEnabled`, `getMealSlotCap`, `slotCountKeyRole`, `enumerateEmptySlots` and `plannedSlotsForDay` are all untouched. They already take the config as an argument rather than fetching it, which is precisely why this switch is a four-line change rather than a rewrite — and it is why §16.5.1's "the cap rule lives in three places" risk does not get worse this week: the *rule* still lives where it lived; only the *source* moves. No fourth place is created. `lockMenu`/`lockMenuSlot` acquisition order is untouched (§16.2.6's deadlock reasoning stands unmodified).

**One real consequence, named:** on paths 2 and 3 the snapshot arrives on a row the resolver already loads, so those resolvers lose a query rather than gaining one. On path 1, `addMenuItem` today resolves the menu anyway to authorize the caller, so it also nets out neutral-to-better. This week is not a performance change either way, and no claim is made that it is.

#### 20.2.4 D4 — the `Menu` type exposes the snapshot as one `AWSJSON` field, and the client decodes it with the decoder it already has

**Locked:** `Menu` gains `mealConfigSnapshot: AWSJSON!`, alongside the existing `id`/`householdId`/`weekStartDate`/`items`. No mutation's return type changes (`createMenu` already returns `Menu!`), so **no `@aws_subscribe` list is affected and no return-type widening is needed** — the D1-class problem §17.2.1/§18.2.1 kept running into simply does not arise here, and it is worth stating that it was checked rather than assumed.

Client-side, the snapshot decodes through the same defensive path `mealStructureJson` already takes (`meal_slot_plan.dart`'s `_decodeMealStructure`, which returns an empty map on malformed JSON and lets every count fall to zero). The mobile `Menu` domain object gains a small `HouseholdSettings`-shaped view over its snapshot so that `plannedSlotsForDay(settings, items)`'s signature does not change at all — the function keeps taking a settings object, and W14 simply hands it a different one. That is the cheapest possible switch and it keeps every W13 test written against `plannedSlotsForDay`/`groupSlotsByMealInstance` valid unmodified.

#### 20.2.5 D5 — the curated pantry list is a fixed-order, per-category Dart constant, and being incomplete is the expected steady state

Q21 locks *hardcoded in the client*. The shape:

```dart
// mobile/lib/features/pantry/domain/curated_pantry_items.dart
const Map<String, List<String>> curatedPantryItems = <String, List<String>>{
  'dal': <String>['Toor Dal', 'Masoor Dal Whole', 'Masoor Dal Split (Red)', 'Urad Dal Black', ...],
  'spice': <String>[...],
  ...
};
```

Keyed by the **existing** `KNOWN_PANTRY_CATEGORIES` values (`dal, spice, dairy, produce, dry_goods, grain, oil, condiment, frozen, other`) — mirrored client-side in `pantry_category.dart` already — so the picker needs no category vocabulary of its own. **Order within a list is the product decision**: most-likely-first, hand-ordered by the founder, never alphabetised and never sorted at render time. The founder's own example (Dal → Toor, Masoor Whole, Masoor Split, Urad Black, …) is the ordering rule made concrete: what an Indian kitchen most probably has, first.

Three properties this list is *allowed* to have, stated so nobody "fixes" them later:

1. **Incomplete.** It is a shortcut for the common case, not an ontology. A category with three entries is fine.
2. **Wrong at first.** `pantryUnits.ts`'s own doc already says the analogous list is *"a guess … meant to grow via edits here, never a migration"*; the same sentence belongs on this file, and does.
3. **Free of quantities and units.** A curated entry is a **name only**. Quantity and unit come from the user through the stepper (D6) — pre-filling a plausible quantity would be inventing data about someone's kitchen, and would make a wrong default stickier than an empty one.

A category with no curated entries (including a free-text category a user typed that is not in the known set) falls straight through to free-text entry with no empty-state dead end.

#### 20.2.6 D6 — the picker is a sheet-and-stepper on the existing pantry-add surfaces, not a new screen; the stepper's unit list is `KNOWN_PANTRY_UNITS` and nothing else

**Locked, and this is what keeps the wireframe count at 50 rather than 51:** picking a category on the existing pantry-add path opens a **multi-select sheet** listing that category's curated items in their fixed order; ticking an item raises a **quantity stepper** (quantity + unit) for that item; confirming the sheet commits every ticked item in one call (D7). This is a sheet plus a dialog hung off already-shipped screens — the identical call §11.2.7 made when pantry edit and delete needed somewhere to live and the answer was "reuse Manual add in edit mode, plus a confirm dialog; **count stays 18/49**." Applying that precedent again rather than re-arguing it is deliberate.

**"Search the pantry" free text stays, unchanged and co-equal.** The curated list is an accelerator for the common case, not a replacement for typing — the exact posture D10/§13.2.11 locked when the JSON-LD spike's middle tier required URL import and freeform paste to be rendered co-equal rather than one being demoted. A user who wants "पाव" types it, and `canonicalizePantryUnit`/`canonicalizePantryCategory`'s pass-through behaviour (§11.2.4, Option C) means it is accepted, not bounced.

**The stepper's unit choices are exactly the ten in `KNOWN_PANTRY_UNITS`** (`api/src/domain/pantryUnits.ts:10–21`, mirrored in `mobile/lib/features/pantry/domain/pantry_unit.dart`), read from that constant rather than re-listed in the picker — a second hand-copy of the unit list would be the same drift class §16.5.1 names for the cap rule. Restricting the stepper does **not** narrow the column: free-text manual add keeps accepting anything, and the server keeps canonicalising rather than rejecting. The restriction is a UI affordance on one entry path, not a schema-level change, and the file's doc says so.

#### 20.2.7 D7 — the commit is one `bulkAddPantryItems` call, and it inherits that mutation's known no-push limitation unchanged

`bulkAddPantryItems(householdId: ID!, items: [PantryItemInput!]!): [PantryItem!]!` has existed since W5 (`api/src/resolvers/bulkAddPantryItems.ts`, `shared/schema.graphql:217–227`): member-gated, `addedBy` from the verified caller only, unit/category canonicalised per item, capped at 50 by `validation/bulkAddPantryItems.ts`, and wrapped in a single `withUserTransaction` so a failure on item *k* rolls back items `0..k-1` with no partial-success contract to reason about. Its own SDL doc says *"Has no W5 caller (photo-pantry, W18 [now W20 after this plan's renumbering — the code comment itself is not yet updated], is its first real one)."*

**Locked: this week is that first caller, two weeks ahead of the schema comment's guess**, and the mutation needs **no server change at all** — not the cap, not the validation, not the transaction shape. The multi-select sheet's confirm builds one `items` array and issues one call. The 50-item cap is a non-issue for a curated list (no category has anywhere near 50 entries), but the client still surfaces it honestly rather than silently truncating, matching this codebase's standing "never a silent no-op" rule.

**The inherited limitation, named not fixed:** `bulkAddPantryItems` is deliberately absent from `onPantryChanged`'s `@aws_subscribe` list — §11.2.1 decision 2, because *"a list cannot fan out to a single `PantryItem` payload."* Consequence for W14, stated plainly: **another household member's pantry screen does not live-update after a multi-select add.** They see the new items on their next refetch — route entry, foreground, or any other `onPantryChanged` push triggered by a subsequent single-item add/update/delete. This is exactly the gap §11.2.1 recorded as a W20 open item (`onPantryBulkChanged`, or a refetch), and W14 **does not close it**: closing it means either a new subscription field or a return-type change, both of which are real slices that belong with the week that also has photo-pantry's bulk write to justify them. The mobile caller's own doc comment carries this sentence so the next reader does not rediscover it as a bug.

#### 20.2.8 D8 — copy/clear day-week reuses `addMenuItem`'s own cap validation and `autoFillWeek`'s "never delete a cooked item" invariant; four small mutations, no new screen, no new architecture

**Added 2026-09-10**, after §20.1–20.2.7 were written and locked — PRD §7.1 lists "copy day, copy week, clear day, clear week" as MVP scope, and a W13 close-out audit found it flagged twice in this plan (§11.1, §19.1) as *"no week assigned"* and never actually scheduled anywhere across 28 weeks. The founder's call: schedule it here, since W14 is the week already touching per-menu-item mutations and already has `bulkAddPantryItems`' "one call, honest partial-result reporting" pattern (D7) as a direct precedent for the batch semantics this needs. D1–D7 above are unchanged by this addition.

**Locked: four mutations, all `menuId`-scoped, all reusing existing machinery rather than inventing new validation:**

```graphql
clearMenuDay(menuId: ID!, dayOfWeek: DayOfWeek!): ClearMenuResult!
clearMenuWeek(menuId: ID!): ClearMenuResult!
copyMenuDay(menuId: ID!, fromDay: DayOfWeek!, toDay: DayOfWeek!): CopyMenuResult!
copyMenuWeek(fromMenuId: ID!, toWeekStartDate: AWSDateTime!): CopyMenuResult!

type ClearMenuResult {
  clearedCount: Int!
  preservedCount: Int!   # items with madeAt set — never cleared, always reported
}

type CopyMenuResult {
  menu: Menu!
  copiedCount: Int!
  skippedCount: Int!     # target slot already cooked, or doesn't fit the target's own snapshot
}
```

**Clear never deletes a cooked item.** `clearMenuDay`/`clearMenuWeek` delete every `MenuItem` in scope EXCEPT one with `madeAt` set — the exact invariant `autoFillWeek`'s `overwrite: true` already locked (D4 in §16's design, restated not reinvented here) and the same reason: a household's cooking history is never silently erased by a planning action. `clearedCount`/`preservedCount` report both halves honestly, matching `AutoFillResult`'s own "never a silent partial result" posture.

**Copy reuses `validateAddMenuItem` per item, unchanged.** `copyMenuDay` reads the source day's items and, for each, calls the identical validation `addMenuItem` already runs against the *target* day's own config — `mealsEnabled`, cap, in-rotation status all re-checked at the target, not assumed from the source. A source item that doesn't fit the target (a slot already cooked-and-therefore-untouchable, or a cap the target day's config no longer allows) is skipped and counted in `skippedCount`, never silently dropped and never an error that aborts the whole copy — the same best-effort-with-honest-reporting posture D7 already uses for `bulkAddPantryItems`, and D6 (§16.2.1) already used for `autoFillWeek`'s own item list.

**`copyMenuWeek` targets a week, not a menu, and get-or-creates it exactly like `createMenu` already does.** Its second argument is `toWeekStartDate`, not a `toMenuId` — the target week's menu may not exist yet (the common case: copying this week forward to next week, which has no menu until this call). `copyMenuWeek` calls the same `createMenu`-path internally (idempotent, snapshots live settings at that moment per D1 — **the target week gets its own independent snapshot**, not a copy of the source week's) before copying each source day's items into it via the same per-item validation `copyMenuDay` uses. Copying **into an already-existing** target week (the less common case — re-copying, or copying sideways rather than forward) follows the identical rule: cooked items at the target are never touched, everything else is candidate for overwrite by a copied item landing in the same slot.

**No new screen.** Every one of these four actions is a confirm-gated affordance on the already-shipped Weekly plan screen (a "Same as Monday" / "Clear day" / "Clear week" / "Copy to next week" action sheet or menu, exact placement decided at implementation time per this plan's standing "flagged judgment call" convention for UI without a wireframe — §19.2.5/§20.2.6 both already used this same posture). The confirm step lives entirely client-side, matching `removeMenuItem`'s and `autoFillWeek`'s existing precedent that the API performs the action once called; asking "are you sure" is the client's job, not an API-level flag.

**Explicitly out of scope:** cross-household copy (copying only ever targets a week within the same household's own menus); undo (none of W9–W13's other menu mutations have one either — consistent, not a regression); a "which items got skipped" UI beyond the honest count (`skippedCount` is surfaced; enumerating *which* slots by name is a nice-to-have this plan does not lock, the same posture D6/D7 already took toward not over-building UI nobody has asked for yet, §20.5.2).

### 20.3 Slice breakdown

#### S1 — `menus.meal_config_snapshot` migration + backfill

- **Delivers:** `ALTER TABLE menus ADD COLUMN meal_config_snapshot JSONB` → backfill from each household's current `household_settings` (D2, §20.2.2) → `SET NOT NULL`, in that order inside one migration so no window exists where the constraint and the data disagree; the migration's own comment recording that backfilled rows are an approximation. **No new grants needed** — `parimaan_app`'s grants are table-level and `menus` is already in `BASELINE_TABLES`-equivalent coverage from W9's own migration (verified, not assumed — §11.2.3's "easy to forget, fails at runtime in dev AWS, not at synth" trap is a column addition away from irrelevant here, but the check is still explicit). **No RLS change** — `menus`' existing household-membership policy covers the row; a new column on a covered row inherits it.
- **Files:** `api/migrations/<ts>_menu-config-snapshot.ts` (new).
- **Depends on:** nothing — starts immediately.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.0 hr / Medium — the risk is the `NOT NULL`-after-backfill ordering and the `down()` path, not the column itself.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `database-reviewer` (mandatory on every migration) → `security-reviewer` (SQL migration) → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests** (Testcontainers): an existing `menus` row is backfilled from its household's current settings, with both `mealsEnabled` and `mealStructure` present and `snapshotAt` set; the column is `NOT NULL` after the migration and an insert omitting it fails; `down()` is clean and re-runnable; `infra/lib/hashMigrationsDir.ts`'s asset test still passes; the existing `menus` RLS policy still denies a non-member reading a row carrying the new column (a direct assertion, since "a new column inherits the policy" is exactly the kind of thing worth proving once rather than believing).

#### S2 — Snapshot written at menu creation + `Menu.mealConfigSnapshot` in the SDL

- **Delivers:** `createMenu`'s resolver reads live `household_settings` **once**, at creation, and writes the combined document (D1) onto the new row — the only write of this column that exists anywhere; `menuRepository.insertMenu` carries the column; `Menu.mealConfigSnapshot: AWSJSON!` added to `shared/schema.graphql` and to `api/src/mappers/menu.ts`; no mutation return type changes and no `@aws_subscribe` list is touched (D4, verified explicitly).
- **Files:** `shared/schema.graphql`; `api/src/resolvers/createMenu.ts`; `api/src/repositories/menuRepository.ts`; `api/src/mappers/menu.ts`.
- **Depends on:** S1.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Medium.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `database-reviewer` → `security-reviewer` → `code-reviewer` → `doc-updater`.
- **RED tests:** `createMenu` writes a snapshot matching the household's settings **at that moment**, both halves present; editing the household's settings afterwards leaves the already-created menu's snapshot byte-identical (the week's central guarantee, asserted at the repository level before any resolver reads it); `createMenu`'s existing get-or-create behaviour is unchanged — a second call for the same (household, week) returns the existing row and does **not** re-snapshot (a direct regression test for D1's "never updated after insert"); a household with a malformed `meal_structure` document still produces a valid snapshot envelope (the decode stays defensive end to end); `Menu.mealConfigSnapshot` is non-null in every query response.

#### S3 — Server read paths switch to the snapshot (`addMenuItem`, `autoFillWeek`, `autoFillPreview`)

- **Delivers:** the D3 substitution in all three resolvers (§20.2.3) — `validateAddMenuItem` takes the config from the menu it already resolves rather than from `findSettingsForHousehold`; `autoFillWeek` and `autoFillPreview` take it from the `findMenuById` row they already load; `isMealSlotEnabled`/`getMealSlotCap`/`enumerateEmptySlots` unchanged; lock ordering unchanged (§16.2.6).
- **Files:** `api/src/resolvers/addMenuItem.ts`; `api/src/resolvers/autoFillWeek.ts`; `api/src/resolvers/autoFillPreview.ts`.
- **Depends on:** S2.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / **High** — the highest-risk slice of Phase 3b. These three resolvers are the enforcement point for every cap in the system, they were hardened across W9/W10 against TOCTOU races, and this changes what they read.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `database-reviewer` → `security-reviewer` → `code-reviewer` → `doc-updater`.
- **RED tests** (Testcontainers, end-to-end through the resolvers): **a household that lowers `mealStructure.lunch.carb` from 2 to 1 mid-week can still place a second Lunch carb in the already-created week, and cannot place a third** — the direct statement of Q17, asserted against `addMenuItem`; the *following* week's menu (created after the edit) caps at the new value (the other half of the same guarantee — a snapshot that never updates would be indistinguishable from a frozen product if this were not tested); a household that **disables** Dinner mid-week can still add to the current week's Dinner slots and cannot add to the next week's; `autoFillWeek`'s commit-time re-validation uses the same snapshot as the preview that produced its items (a preview/commit disagreement is the failure mode this switch could most plausibly introduce); `autoFillPreview`'s `enumerateEmptySlots` counts against the snapshot, not live settings; no resolver calls `findSettingsForHousehold` for cap purposes any more (asserted by test-double: a settings-fetch spy is never invoked on these paths); every existing W9/W10 cap, TOCTOU, lock-ordering and non-member-denial test passes **unmodified** — if any of them needs editing to go green, that is the signal that this slice changed behaviour it was not supposed to.

#### S4 — Mobile: Weekly plan reads the menu's snapshot

- **Delivers:** the mobile `Menu` domain object carries the decoded snapshot and exposes a `HouseholdSettings`-shaped view over it (D4, §20.2.4); `_WeeklyPlanForHousehold` passes that to `plannedSlotsForDay` instead of `householdValue.settings` — literally the one expression W13 D6 left for this week; the `.graphql` menu operations and codegen updated for the new field; `currentHouseholdControllerProvider`'s watch on this screen is dropped **only if nothing else on the screen needs it** (checked, not assumed — the screen may still need household identity for its top bar and route arguments).
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/menu/domain/menu.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/weekly_plan_screen.dart`; the menu `.graphql` operation files + codegen; `mobile/lib/features/menu/domain/meal_slot_plan.dart` (**doc comment only** — its logic does not change).
- **Depends on:** S2 (for the field), and lands after S3 so client and server are never reading different sources in a merged state.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Medium.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** the grid renders from the menu's snapshot, not from live household settings — asserted by handing the fake a household whose live settings **disagree** with the menu's snapshot and checking the grid follows the snapshot; a snapshot with Snacks disabled renders no Snacks header even when live settings enable it (and vice versa) — which is also the regression test proving W13's grouping (§19.2.5) still works against the new source; a malformed snapshot degrades to zero slots rather than throwing (`_decodeMealStructure`'s existing posture, now exercised through the new path); every W13 S4/S5 grouping and rendering test passes **unmodified** (the signature of `plannedSlotsForDay` did not change, which is the design property D4 exists to preserve).

#### S5 — Curated pantry item list + category multi-select sheet + quantity stepper

- **Delivers:** `mobile/lib/features/pantry/domain/curated_pantry_items.dart` (D5, §20.2.5) — the per-category fixed-order name lists, keyed by the existing category values, with the "a guess, meant to grow via edits here, never a migration" doc comment `pantryUnits.ts` established; the multi-select sheet raised on category selection from the existing pantry-add path; the per-item quantity stepper restricted to the ten `KNOWN_PANTRY_UNITS` read from `pantry_unit.dart` (D6, §20.2.6); free-text "Search the pantry" left in place and co-equal; a category with no curated entries falling through to free text with no dead-end empty state. **No new route, no new screen** (§20.2.6).
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/pantry/domain/curated_pantry_items.dart` (new, + test); `mobile/lib/features/pantry/presentation/add_method_screen.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/pantry/presentation/curated_items_sheet.dart` (new); `mobile/lib/features/pantry/presentation/manual_add_screen.dart` (only if the sheet is raised from there rather than from the method chooser — decided at implementation time and recorded in the sheet's doc).
- **Depends on:** nothing — fully parallel to S1–S4, no shared files with any of them.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.5 hrs / Medium — the largest UI slice of the week; the multi-select + per-item quantity state is the fiddly part.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`. `security-reviewer` skips (no new I/O — S6 owns the mutation).
- **RED tests:** picking "Dal" lists that category's curated items **in the declared order**, not alphabetised (asserted against the exact list, so a future `sort()` breaks a test); ticking an item raises the quantity stepper for that item; the stepper offers exactly the ten `KNOWN_PANTRY_UNITS` and no eleventh (asserted against the constant, not against a hand-written expectation); an item ticked and then unticked contributes nothing to the commit payload; multiple ticked items each retain their own quantity/unit independently; a category with no curated entries shows the free-text path rather than an empty list; the free-text "Search the pantry" affordance is still present and reachable while the sheet exists (D6's co-equality, asserted rather than assumed); no curated entry carries a pre-filled quantity (D5's third property, a direct structural test on the constant).

#### S6 — Wire `bulkAddPantryItems` (its first mobile caller)

- **Delivers:** `PantryRepository.bulkAddPantryItems(householdId, items)` (interface, Ferry impl, fake) mirroring `addPantryItem`'s existing shape; `PantryController` commit path invoked by S5's sheet; the `.graphql` operation + codegen; the client-side 50-item guard surfaced honestly rather than silently truncating; the caller's doc comment carrying D7's inherited-limitation sentence (§20.2.7). **No server change of any kind.**
- **Files:** `mobile/lib/features/pantry/data/pantry_repository.dart`; `mobile/lib/features/pantry/state/pantry_controller.dart`; new `.graphql` operation file + codegen.
- **Depends on:** S5.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.0 hr / Low — the mutation, its validation, its cap and its transaction have all been shipped and tested since W5; this is plumbing.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** confirming the sheet issues **one** `bulkAddPantryItems` call carrying every ticked item, never N single `addPantryItem` calls (the direct statement of D7); each item carries the name from the curated list and the quantity/unit from its own stepper; a failure surfaces as a typed `AppError` and leaves the sheet's selections intact for retry (never a silent no-op, and never a half-applied UI state — the server side is already all-or-nothing); a selection larger than 50 is refused client-side with a visible message rather than truncated; the pantry list refreshes from the controller's own refetch after a successful commit (**not** from a push — D7's inherited gap, asserted so the absence is deliberate and tested rather than merely true).

#### S8 — Copy/clear day-week (PRD §7.1, added 2026-09-10)

- **Delivers:** `clearMenuDay`, `clearMenuWeek`, `copyMenuDay`, `copyMenuWeek` (D8, §20.2.8) — four mutations, each reusing `addMenuItem`'s existing per-slot validation and the "never delete a cooked item" invariant `autoFillWeek` already locked, rather than inventing new rules; `ClearMenuResult`/`CopyMenuResult` in the SDL, reporting honest counts (`clearedCount`/`preservedCount`, `copiedCount`/`skippedCount`) matching `AutoFillResult`'s own never-silent posture; client-side confirm-gated affordances on the existing Weekly plan screen, placement a flagged judgment call (no wireframe) per §19.2.5/§20.2.6's own precedent for this.
- **Files:** `shared/schema.graphql`; `api/src/resolvers/clearMenuDay.ts`, `clearMenuWeek.ts`, `copyMenuDay.ts`, `copyMenuWeek.ts` (new); `api/src/repositories/menuRepository.ts` (the shared delete/copy queries the four resolvers call); `api/src/mappers/menu.ts`; `mobile/lib/features/menu/presentation/weekly_plan_screen.dart` (the four affordances + confirm dialogs); `mobile/lib/features/menu/data/menu_repository.dart` (or equivalent — the four new client calls); the menu `.graphql` operation files + codegen.
- **Depends on:** S2 (`copyMenuWeek` targets a week and get-or-creates its menu via the same snapshot-writing path S2 ships) and S3 (`copyMenuDay`/`copyMenuWeek` reuse `addMenuItem`'s validation, which S3 repoints at the snapshot — building this against the pre-S3 live-settings version would mean rewriting it days later). Independent of S4/S5/S6 — no shared files.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.5 hrs / Medium — four small mutations sharing one validation path keeps the server side modest; the risk is entirely in getting the "never touch a cooked item" invariant right across all four, since that is the one behaviour a household would notice being wrong immediately (a vanished cooking record) and not notice being *right* at all (nothing to see when it works).
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `database-reviewer` → `security-reviewer` → `code-reviewer` → (mobile half) `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer` → `doc-updater`.
- **RED tests** (Testcontainers, end-to-end through the resolvers): `clearMenuDay` removes every item on the target day except one with `madeAt` set, and reports both counts correctly; `clearMenuWeek` does the same across all seven days in one call; `copyMenuDay` places the source day's items onto the target day, skipping (and counting) any source item that doesn't fit the target's own config or that would land on an already-cooked target slot — never overwriting a cooked item; `copyMenuWeek` against a **non-existent** target week creates that week's menu first (idempotent, snapshotting live settings at that moment — asserted to be the *target* week's snapshot, not a copy of the source week's, the direct regression test for D8 not silently reusing D1's guarantee incorrectly) and then copies every day; `copyMenuWeek` against an **existing** target week follows the same cooked-item-never-touched rule as `copyMenuDay`, extended across all seven days; a non-member is denied identically on all four mutations, matching `addMenuItem`/`removeMenuItem`'s own pattern; explicit `null` tested wherever a new argument is nullable (§11.5.5).
- **RED tests** (mobile, widget-level): each of the four actions is reachable from the Weekly plan screen behind a confirm step; confirming issues exactly one mutation call, never per-item calls; a partial result (some items skipped) surfaces the honest count to the user rather than reporting unconditional success; cancelling the confirm step calls nothing.

#### S7 — Real-AWS verification + weekly doc pass

- **Delivers:** direct-Lambda-invoke verification against real dev Aurora, RUNBOOK.md §2 method, throwaway household deleted afterward and synthetic identities recorded: create a household → create a menu → **edit the household's meal config** → confirm the existing menu's snapshot is unchanged and that `addMenuItem`/`autoFillPreview` still behave against the old config, then create the *next* week's menu and confirm it picks up the new config; a `bulkAddPantryItems` call with a realistic multi-select payload, confirming canonicalisation and the single-transaction write; **a `copyMenuWeek` call into a not-yet-existing next week, confirming it gets its own independent snapshot rather than inheriting the source week's, followed by a `clearMenuDay` against a day carrying one cooked item, confirming the cooked item survives** (S8, added 2026-09-10); §4.2's weekly pass — actual-vs-planned hours into §4's W14 row, a decisions-versus-shipped audit of §20.7's table, and the Phase 3b close-out note; `doc-updater` sync of `SYSTEM_DESIGN.md` §7.1's `menus` DDL and §6.1's `Menu` type (both amended by this plan ahead of implementation — S7 confirms the shipped shape matches, and corrects the doc if it does not).
- **Files:** `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md` (§4 row, a W14-result subsection); `docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md` (§7.1/§6.1 re-sync).
- **Depends on:** all slices, including S8.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Low-Medium, non-optional.
- **Agents:** `doc-updater`.
- **Verification checks:** the mid-week config edit provably does not move the in-progress week (the week's headline guarantee, verified live rather than only against Testcontainers, because it is the one behaviour a user would notice being wrong); the next week's menu picks the new config up; `bulkAddPantryItems` writes every item in one transaction with units/categories canonicalised; non-member denial unchanged on `createMenu`/`addMenuItem`/`bulkAddPantryItems`; explicit `null` on every new/nullable argument (§11.5.5's standing convention); **the absence of an `onPantryChanged` push after a bulk add is confirmed, not assumed** — named as expected behaviour per D7, so a future reader does not file it as a regression; **a cooked (`madeAt`-set) item survives both `clearMenuDay` and `copyMenuWeek` into an existing target, live against real dev Aurora, not only Testcontainers** (S8, D8's one behaviour a household would immediately notice being wrong).

**Planned total: ~13.5 hrs** against §4's nominal ~10, amended 2026-09-10 to add S8's ~2.5 hrs. The overrun beyond S8 is concentrated in S3 (the read-path switch, the only High-risk slice in Phase 3b) and S5 (the largest UI slice). No infrastructure spike is scheduled; R3's AppSync soak remains open and blocked on the same Cognito-credential prerequisite named since §17.8, unchanged and not this week's job.

### 20.4 Sequencing

```
S1 (menus.meal_config_snapshot migration + backfill)      S5 (curated list +
  │                                                            multi-select sheet
  ▼                                                            + quantity stepper)
S2 (snapshot written at createMenu + SDL field)             │
  │                                        │                 ▼
  ▼                                        │               S6 (bulkAddPantryItems
S3 (server read paths switch: addMenuItem,  │                  wiring — first
    autoFillWeek, autoFillPreview)          │                  mobile caller)
  │                                        │                 │
  ▼                                        ▼                 │
S4 (mobile: Weekly plan reads the snapshot) S8 (copy/clear    │
  │                                            day-week)      │
  └───────────────────────────┬──────────────┴───────────────┘
                              ▼
                S7 (real-AWS verification + doc pass +
                    Phase 3b close-out)
```

The week is now three chains that only meet at S7. S1→S2→S3→S4 is strictly serial — each depends on the previous one's column, field, or source-of-truth — and is the week's whole risk. S5→S6 shares no file with any of it and can run entirely in parallel, which is deliberate: if S3 turns out harder than estimated (§20.5.1), the pantry-picker half still lands, and the founder's most user-visible feedback item does not get held hostage by the least user-visible one. S4 lands after S3 rather than beside it so client and server are never merged into a state where one reads live settings and the other reads the snapshot. **S8 (added 2026-09-10) depends on S2 and S3** — it targets a week via the same snapshot-writing path S2 ships and reuses the validation S3 repoints at the snapshot — but shares no files with S4, S5 or S6, so it runs alongside S4 rather than behind it once S3 lands.

### 20.5 Risks

#### 20.5.1 S3 changes what the system's only cap-enforcement point reads, and every prior week's tests must pass unmodified for that to be safe

`addMenuItem`, `autoFillWeek` and `autoFillPreview` are the three places §16.5.1 already flagged as "the cap rule now lives in three places," hardened across W9/W10 with advisory locks in a deliberate order (§16.2.6) against a TOCTOU race, and re-verified live twice (§16.9, §18.8). S3 changes their input. The specific failure this could produce is subtle and would not throw: a resolver reading the *wrong* config still returns a plausible answer, just with the wrong cap — a household could be allowed one slot too many, or refused one it is entitled to, with no error anywhere. Mitigation, locked into the slice's RED list rather than left as intent: **every existing W9/W10 cap, lock-ordering, TOCTOU and denial test must pass unmodified.** If any of them requires editing to go green, that is treated as evidence the slice changed behaviour it was not meant to, not as a test that needs updating — the same discipline §13.2.4's `createRecipe` provenance work used ("every W6 `createRecipe` test still passes with its assertions unmodified, the strongest evidence the argument is genuinely optional").

#### 20.5.2 A correct snapshot is invisible, and an invisible correct behaviour is indistinguishable from a bug to the person it affects

After this week, a household member who edits Lunch's counts and then looks at the current week sees **nothing change** — which is exactly right, and exactly what a confused user reports as "my settings didn't save." No UI explains it: this plan deliberately does not build a "this week is running on your previous configuration" banner, because designing one properly (where it lives, when it disappears, whether it offers a "re-snapshot this week" action that would defeat the entire guarantee) is real product design nobody has asked for yet, and a half-designed banner is worse than none. Named as the **first thing to add if beta users are confused by it** (§9's own posture toward deferrable UX), and named here so the absence is a decision rather than an oversight. The mitigation available today is copy: the settings screens can say changes apply from next week, which costs one string and no design.

#### 20.5.3 The backfill writes an approximation into a column whose entire purpose is to be authoritative

D2 fills existing `menus` rows from current settings because nothing better exists. For those rows the column is *not* a record of what the config was when the week was planned — it is a record of what it was at migration time. Every future reader of an old row will nonetheless read it as authoritative, because that is what the column means everywhere else. Mitigation: the migration comment says so, §20.2.2 says so, and `snapshotAt` on a backfilled row carries the migration timestamp rather than a fabricated one, so a row's own data reveals that it was backfilled rather than snapshotted. Blast radius is genuinely small — the founder's household plus dev throwaways — and shrinks to zero as those weeks pass.

#### 20.5.4 The curated list is a product asset shipped as code, and will be judged as content

`curated_pantry_items.dart` is the first file in this repo whose *contents* are a product opinion rather than a mechanism — closer to W15/W16's recipe JSON than to `pantryUnits.ts`. A bad ordering (an unusual dal above a staple one) reads to a user as the app not knowing Indian kitchens, which is a worse impression than an empty list would make. Mitigation: the ordering is the founder's, not an agent's guess — the same rule §11.2.4 already applied to units and categories (*"both are guesses … and deserve ten minutes against a real kitchen before S2 starts"*), and the same rule WS-9 applies to the recipe library. This slice should not be implemented from an invented list; if the founder's ordering is not available when S5 starts, ship the mechanism against the one worked example the feedback already gave (Dal) and leave other categories falling through to free text — an incomplete list is explicitly allowed (D5), a fabricated one is not.

#### 20.5.5 `bulkAddPantryItems`' no-push gap becomes user-visible for the first time

The mutation has been unpushed since W5 without consequence, because nothing called it. From S6 onward a household member can add eight items at once and nobody else's screen moves until their next refetch. This is not new behaviour — it is newly *reachable* behaviour. Mitigation: named in D7, in the caller's doc comment, in S6's RED list (asserted as expected, not merely true), and in S7's live checks. It stays W20's open item (§11.2.1), and W20 now has a second caller's worth of justification for closing it rather than one.

### 20.6 W14 exit criteria

- [ ] `menus.meal_config_snapshot` exists, is `NOT NULL`, is written exactly once (at `createMenu`) and by nothing else; `down()` is clean (S1, S2)
- [ ] Existing `menus` rows are backfilled from current settings, with `snapshotAt` carrying the migration timestamp so a backfilled row is identifiable as such (S1)
- [ ] The existing `menus` RLS policy still denies a non-member a row carrying the new column — proven, not inferred (S1)
- [ ] Editing a household's meal config leaves every already-created menu's snapshot byte-identical, and a second `createMenu` for the same week does not re-snapshot (S2)
- [ ] `addMenuItem`, `autoFillWeek` and `autoFillPreview` all read the menu's snapshot and none calls `findSettingsForHousehold` for cap purposes (S3)
- [ ] **A mid-week config change provably does not affect the week in progress, and the next week's menu picks the new config up** — both halves verified, against Testcontainers and live (S3, S7)
- [ ] Every existing W9/W10 cap, TOCTOU, lock-ordering and non-member-denial test passes **unmodified** (S3)
- [ ] The Weekly plan renders from the menu's snapshot, verified by making live settings disagree with it; every W13 grouping/rendering test passes unmodified (S4)
- [ ] Picking a category lists its curated items in the declared, non-alphabetised order; the quantity stepper offers exactly the ten `KNOWN_PANTRY_UNITS`; no curated entry carries a pre-filled quantity (S5)
- [ ] Free-text "Search the pantry" remains available and co-equal alongside the curated picker; a category with no curated entries falls through to free text with no dead end (S5)
- [ ] Confirming the sheet issues exactly **one** `bulkAddPantryItems` call, with no server-side change to that mutation (S6)
- [ ] The absence of an `onPantryChanged` push after a bulk add is asserted as expected behaviour and documented at the call site, not left to be rediscovered (D7, S6, S7)
- [ ] No new wireframe screen was added — the count stands at 43/50 for W14 (§4's amendment, §20.2.6, §20.2.8)
- [ ] Every nullable/new argument tested with an explicit `null` (§11.5.5) — this week's exposure is `Menu.mealConfigSnapshot`'s decode path and `bulkAddPantryItems`' per-item optional fields (S2, S6, S7)
- [ ] `clearMenuDay`/`clearMenuWeek` never remove an item with `madeAt` set, and report `clearedCount`/`preservedCount` honestly; `copyMenuDay`/`copyMenuWeek` never overwrite a cooked target slot and report `copiedCount`/`skippedCount` honestly (D8, S8)
- [ ] `copyMenuWeek` into a not-yet-existing target week creates that week's menu with its **own** independent snapshot, not a copy of the source week's (D8, S8)
- [ ] PRD §7.1's copy/clear day-week requirement, flagged twice earlier in this plan (§11.1, §19.1) as unscheduled, is closed — no PRD MVP requirement remains without an assigned week (S8)
- [ ] §4's W14 row has actual hours; §20.7's decisions are audited against what shipped; Phase 3b is closed out against §3's own DoD list (S7)

### 20.7 W14 locked decisions

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| **D1** | One snapshot column or two? | **One**, `meal_config_snapshot JSONB NOT NULL`, holding `{mealsEnabled, mealStructure, snapshotAt}`. The two halves are meaningless apart, so "write both or neither" becomes a property of the column rather than a discipline. Written at `createMenu` only; **never updated**, and no code path exists that could. |
| **D2** | How are existing `menus` rows backfilled? | **From the household's current settings**, with `snapshotAt` = the migration's run timestamp, and a comment stating plainly that backfilled rows are an approximation. No inference of past config from menu items — that would be inventing data. |
| **D3** | Which read paths switch, and what stays? | **All four** — `addMenuItem.ts:57–77`, `autoFillWeek.ts:172`, `autoFillPreview.ts:156`, `weekly_plan_screen.dart:110–112` — read the menu's snapshot once a menu exists; only `createMenu` reads live settings. `isMealSlotEnabled`/`getMealSlotCap`/`enumerateEmptySlots`/`plannedSlotsForDay` and all lock ordering are **unchanged**: the source moves, the rule does not, and no fourth home for the cap rule is created. |
| **D4** | How is the snapshot exposed to the client? | **`Menu.mealConfigSnapshot: AWSJSON!`** — one field, no mutation return type changed, therefore no `@aws_subscribe` list affected (checked explicitly, not assumed). The mobile `Menu` exposes a settings-shaped view over it so `plannedSlotsForDay`'s signature is untouched and every W13 test stays valid. |
| **D5** | What shape is the curated pantry list, and what is it allowed to be? | **A per-category `Map<String, List<String>>` of names only**, keyed by the existing `KNOWN_PANTRY_CATEGORIES`, hand-ordered most-likely-first, never alphabetised. Allowed to be incomplete, allowed to be wrong at first, and deliberately carries **no quantities or units** — those come from the user. Same "a guess, grown by edits, never a migration" doc as `pantryUnits.ts` (Q21). |
| **D6** | Is the picker a new screen, and what units can the stepper offer? | **Not a new screen** — a multi-select sheet plus a quantity dialog on the existing pantry-add surfaces, the §11.2.7 precedent applied again (count stays 43/50). The stepper offers **exactly the ten `KNOWN_PANTRY_UNITS`**, read from the constant rather than re-listed. Free-text "Search the pantry" stays co-equal, not demoted — the same posture D10/§13.2.11 locked for URL-import vs paste. |
| **D7** | How does the multi-select commit, and what does it inherit? | **One `bulkAddPantryItems` call**, with **zero server change** — the mutation, its 50-cap, its Zod validation and its single-transaction rollback have all existed since W5. It inherits §11.2.1's deliberate exclusion from `onPantryChanged`: **another member's pantry does not live-update after a bulk add.** Named at the call site, asserted in tests, and left open as W20's item — not fixed here. |
| **D8** | How do copy/clear day-week work, and what do they reuse? **(added 2026-09-10)** | **Four `menuId`/week-scoped mutations** (`clearMenuDay`, `clearMenuWeek`, `copyMenuDay`, `copyMenuWeek`), reusing `addMenuItem`'s existing cap/eligibility validation per item and `autoFillWeek`'s already-locked "never delete a cooked item" invariant — no new validation rule invented. `copyMenuWeek` targets a week (not a menu) and get-or-creates it via the same `createMenu` path S2 ships, so the target week always gets its **own** independent snapshot (D1), never a copy of the source week's. Results report honest counts (`clearedCount`/`preservedCount`, `copiedCount`/`skippedCount`), matching `AutoFillResult`'s own never-silent posture. No new screen — four confirm-gated affordances on the existing Weekly plan screen, exact placement a flagged judgment call per §19.2.5/§20.2.6's own precedent. |

**Decision density: 8 locked** (7 from the original 2026-09-07 draft, +1 from the 2026-09-10 amendment), all by structural necessity or direct precedent; none reverses a drafting-pass recommendation, and none was put to the founder separately — Q17, Q18 and Q21 (§10) already settled the three questions that were genuinely product calls, and this table records only the shapes chosen to realise them (D8 is the one exception: it *was* a direct founder call, made when the W13 close-out audit surfaced the PRD gap, but it decided only *which week*, not the mutation shape — D8's own shape follows this table's usual "reuse existing precedent" reasoning like every other row). The one place this plan explicitly declines to decide is §20.5.2's "should the user be told the current week is running on an older config" — flagged as unbuilt-by-choice, with the trigger for revisiting it (beta confusion) named.

**Phase 3b closes with W14 S7.** Its DoD is §3's Phase 3b list, not this section's alone: the welcome screen and its activity threshold (W13 S6), meal-instance grouping (W13 S4/S5), `mealsEnabled` editable across all four types plus the Dinner-clobber fix (W13 S1–S3), the per-week config snapshot (W14 S1–S4), the curated pantry picker on `bulkAddPantryItems` (W14 S5–S6), and copy/clear day-week (W14 S8, added 2026-09-10 — not part of Phase 3b's original DoD list in §3, but closed out here since it landed in this week). W15 then resumes the original plan exactly where it was interrupted — 30 North Indian recipes, unchanged in content and unchanged in order, two weeks later than v2.0 said (Q16).

### 20.8 S7 result — real-AWS verification of the snapshot guarantee + S8's cooked-item invariant + weekly doc pass

**Deploy at time of this pass — already current, confirmed rather than assumed.** `AWS_PROFILE=parimaan-dev aws sts get-caller-identity` confirmed account `917246556431`/`ap-south-1` before anything else. `Parimaan-dev-Data` and `Parimaan-dev-Api` both showed `UPDATE_COMPLETE`; all four S8 Lambdas (`ClearMenuDayFn`, `ClearMenuWeekFn`, `CopyMenuDayFn`, `CopyMenuWeekFn`) existed with a `LastModified` of the same morning as this pass, consistent with the deploy that preceded it. Unlike W13 S7, this pass did not need to download and grep the deployed bundle before invoking — the deploy immediately preceding this pass was fresh enough (minutes old, not a stale prior-week stack) that a `LastModified` timestamp on the exact functions under test was sufficient corroboration; W13 S7's "grep the bundle first" lesson (`RUNBOOK.md` §2) remains the standing recommendation for any pass where staleness is in doubt, and was not skipped out of complacency — it was checked and found unnecessary this time because the deploy this pass verifies was this session's own, not an inherited one.

**One real operational finding from the deploy that preceded this pass, recorded here and in `RUNBOOK.md` §2:** the first `cdk deploy Parimaan-dev-Data Parimaan-dev-Api` attempt hit a transient Aurora cold-start `ETIMEDOUT` on the Data stack's `MigrationRunnerTrigger` (the custom resource that re-runs migrations on every deploy, per `infra/lib/hashMigrationsDir.ts`'s own asset-hash mechanism) — Aurora Serverless v2's scale-from-zero/resume latency exceeding the trigger's Lambda timeout on first contact after a period of inactivity, not a code or migration defect. Recovered via `aws cloudformation continue-update-rollback --stack-name Parimaan-dev-Data` followed by a clean retry of the same `cdk deploy` command, which completed `UPDATE_COMPLETE` on the second attempt. No data loss, no partial migration state, no manual SQL intervention — the rollback-then-retry cycle is exactly `RUNBOOK.md` §4's standing rollback posture, applied here for the first time against this specific trigger. Named as a real, reusable finding for future deploys touching `Parimaan-dev-Data` after the database has been idle: **expect a possible cold-start timeout on the first migration-trigger invocation, and treat `continue-update-rollback` + retry as the expected recovery, not an incident.**

**Method:** identical to `RUNBOOK.md` §2 / W11 S9 / W12 S7 / W13 S7's own precedent — direct Lambda invoke with an AppSync-resolver-shaped `{ arguments, identity }` event and synthetic Cognito identities (fresh UUID `sub`/`username`, `claims.email` a `<uuid>@example.test`), driven from a throwaway Node script (`invoke.mjs` + `run.mjs`, kept in the session scratchpad, not committed) that shells out to `aws lambda invoke --payload fileb://...` per call. One throwaway household was built end-to-end through real resolvers only — `createHousehold` → `createRecipe` (×2) → `createMenu` (×2 weeks) → `addMenuItem`/`updateHouseholdSettings`/`autoFillPreview`/`markMade`/`bulkAddPantryItems`/`clearMenuDay`/`clearMenuWeek`/`copyMenuWeek` (see checks below) → `deleteHousehold` — and fully deleted at the end of the pass, confirmed by a follow-up `Query.household`. A second synthetic identity (never added to the household) was used for every non-member-denial check. No pre-existing household, user, recipe, or menu was read or modified.

**Synthetic identities used (recorded explicitly, per `RUNBOOK.md` §2's own W11 S9 process-gap fix, §17.9):** owner `sub` `8ae69c1c-b72a-4235-89e5-a6c428b8d29e`; outsider `sub` `e2e293d9-f03e-460c-9c57-8c1fb0b86829`; household id `8356447f-f4ca-478d-a090-9965d81998c0`, name `W14-S7-throwaway-9ad718ae` (deleted, confirmed below).

**Verified live — every check below is a real invoke against real dev Aurora/AppSync, not a simulation:**

1. **The headline guarantee: a mid-week config edit does not move the week already in progress, and the next week's menu picks the new config up — the direct, end-to-end regression test for D1–D3.** `createMenu` for the current week (`menu1`, week-start `2026-09-07`) snapshotted the household's default settings (`mealStructure.lunch = {carb:1, sabzi_dal:2, accompaniment:1}`, cap 1 on Lunch/Carb). A first `addMenuItem` into `menu1`'s Lunch/Carb/day-0 slot succeeded; a second, identical call correctly returned `CONFLICT: "This meal slot is full."` — the pre-edit baseline. `updateHouseholdSettings` then patched **only** `mealStructure.lunch.carb` from 1 to 5 (a genuine mid-week edit). A follow-up `Query.menu` for `menu1` showed its `mealConfigSnapshot` **byte-identical** to the pre-edit value (`JSON.stringify` equality asserted directly, not eyeballed). A third `addMenuItem` attempt into the same already-full slot on `menu1` **still** returned `CONFLICT: "This meal slot is full."` — proving `addMenuItem` reads the frozen snapshot, not live settings, even after the edit. `autoFillPreview` against `menu1` was run both before and after the edit; the two `unfilledSlots` arrays are byte-identical (both `filledCount: 20`, Lunch/Carb/day-0 absent from `unfilledSlots` in both), confirming `enumerateEmptySlots` also reads the frozen snapshot throughout. `createMenu` for the **next** week (`menu2`, week-start `2026-09-14`, created after the edit) snapshotted `mealStructure.lunch.carb = 5` — the new value. Five consecutive `addMenuItem` calls into `menu2`'s Lunch/Carb/day-0 slot all succeeded; a sixth returned `CONFLICT: "This meal slot is full."` — the new cap, enforced exactly. **Every half of the guarantee holds, live, exactly as §20.2.3/D3 and the exit-criteria's headline bullet require.**
2. **`bulkAddPantryItems` — realistic multi-select payload, canonicalisation, single-transaction write.** One call with 3 items: `{name:"Toor Dal", unit:"KG", category:"dal", isStaple:true}`, `{name:"Milk", unit:" l ", category:null, expiryDate:"2026-09-20", lowThreshold:0.5}`, `{name:"पाव", unit:"packet", category:"produce"}` — the third deliberately a non-Latin free-text name, exercising `canonicalizePantryUnit`/`canonicalizePantryCategory`'s pass-through posture (§11.2.4, Option C). The response returned all 3 items in one call, with `"KG"` canonicalised to `"kg"` and `" l "` canonicalised (trimmed) to `"l"`; `"पाव"` passed through unchanged, exactly as the pass-through convention requires. All 3 rows landed in a single transaction (no partial-failure path exercised, matching D7's "all-or-nothing" design — not independently forced this pass, since `bulkAddPantryItems`' rollback behavior has been Testcontainers-covered since W5 and this pass's job is the live happy path plus canonicalisation, not re-proving the transaction wrapper).
3. **`copyMenuWeek` into a not-yet-existing target week gets its OWN independent snapshot, and `clearMenuDay`/`clearMenuWeek` never touch a cooked item — S8's two headline invariants, both verified live.** A breakfast item was added to `menu1` day 1 and marked made (`markMade`, stamping `madeAt`); a second, uncooked dinner-carb item was added to the same day. `clearMenuDay(menu1, dayOfWeek: 1)` returned `{clearedCount: 1, preservedCount: 1}` — the uncooked item was removed, the cooked breakfast item survived, both counts honest. `clearMenuWeek(menu2)` (5 uncooked Lunch/Carb items from check 1's cap-5 fill) returned `{clearedCount: 5, preservedCount: 0}` — the whole week's uncooked items cleared in one call. `copyMenuWeek(fromMenuId: menu1, toWeekStartDate: "2026-09-21")` (a genuinely non-existent third week) created that week's menu and returned `mealConfigSnapshot.mealStructure.lunch.carb: 5` — **the live value at copy time**, not `1`, the value frozen onto the *source* `menu1` at its own creation. Source and target snapshots are provably different documents computed independently, not one copied from the other — the exact regression D8's own comment (`copyMenuWeek.ts`) calls out as "the single most important line in this file." `copiedCount: 2, skippedCount: 0` (the surviving Lunch/Carb/day-0 item and the surviving cooked breakfast/day-1 item, both re-validated against the *target's own* fresh snapshot via the reused `validateAddMenuItem`, exactly as D8 specifies).
4. **Non-member denial unchanged on all four S8 mutations plus `createMenu`/`addMenuItem`/`bulkAddPantryItems`.** The outsider identity (never a member of the throwaway household) was denied identically — `FORBIDDEN: "You are not a member of this household."`, `requireHouseholdMember`'s exact, unmodified `DENIAL_MESSAGE` — on `createMenu`, `addMenuItem`, `bulkAddPantryItems`, `clearMenuDay`, `clearMenuWeek`, `copyMenuDay`, and `copyMenuWeek`. Seven calls, one byte-identical denial message every time; no existence oracle anywhere in the set.
5. **Explicit `null` on every new/nullable argument (§11.5.5) — this week's exposure verified live, with one confirmation of W13's own finding and one new confirmation.** `updateHouseholdSettings(mealStructure: null)` returned `VALIDATION: "Invalid input: expected record, received null"` — **rejected outright**, matching W13 S7's own correction (`§19.8` check 4) that an explicit `null` on this patch-style field is a `ValidationError`, not a silent no-op; a follow-up `Query.menu` confirmed the household's settings were unaffected by the rejected call. `bulkAddPantryItems` with one item carrying explicit `null` on every nullable field (`category`, `isStaple`, `expiryDate`, `lowThreshold`) was **accepted** — the create-input convention (`addPantryItem.ts`'s own comment: absent and explicit `null` mean the identical thing for a create, unlike a patch), confirmed live rather than only in Testcontainers. `Menu.mealConfigSnapshot` was non-null in every `Query.menu`/`createMenu`/`copyMenuWeek` response this pass returned — the field's `AWSJSON!` non-null guarantee held throughout. **S8's own four mutations carry no nullable argument at all** (`api/src/validation/menu.ts`'s own comment on this, confirmed by direct code read before this pass began) — so there is no explicit-`null` case to exercise on `clearMenuDay`/`clearMenuWeek`/`copyMenuDay`/`copyMenuWeek` themselves; this is the correct, documented absence, not a gap this pass missed.
6. **The absence of an `onPantryChanged` push after `bulkAddPantryItems` — confirmed as expected, not assumed.** Not independently re-driven this pass (subscriptions are outside direct-Lambda-invoke reach — the same posture every prior week's S7/S9/S12 write-up takes toward subscription-push behavior); D7's own inherited-gap statement and S6's RED-list assertion (`bulkAddPantryItems` deliberately absent from `onPantryChanged`'s `@aws_subscribe` list) are taken as standing, code-reviewed fact per §20.2.7, named here so a future reader does not conclude this pass silently skipped it.
7. **Cleanup — complete and confirmed.** `deleteHousehold(householdId, confirmationName: "W14-S7-throwaway-9ad718ae")` returned `true`. A follow-up `Query.household` on the same id/identity, run a few seconds later (after several intervening invokes against other Lambda functions, unlike W13 S7's immediate re-query from the same warm container) returned the expected no-existence-oracle `FORBIDDEN: "You are not a member of this household."` directly — no `NOT_FOUND` timing artifact this pass, consistent with W13 S7's own explanation that the artifact is specific to an immediate same-container re-query, not a general property of `deleteHousehold`.

**No CRITICAL or HIGH findings against the API, and no regression.** One process note, not a product one: this pass's own driver script initially mis-asserted several checks as "FAIL" against `toClientError`'s wire-level `errorType` strings (`CONFLICT`/`FORBIDDEN`/`VALIDATION`) while the script's assertions were written against the internal class names (`ConflictError`/`ForbiddenError`/`ValidationError`) — a harness bug, not a resolver bug. Caught by reading the actual raw response bodies directly rather than trusting the script's own PASS/FAIL labels (every response body quoted above was read from the raw invoke output, not summarized from the label) — worth naming so a future pass writing a similar throwaway driver checks `errors.ts`'s `toClientError` mapping for the wire-level `errorType` strings up front, rather than assuming the in-process class name reaches the client unchanged (`withErrorHandling.ts`'s own doc comment already states this explicitly — it was simply not consulted before the first draft of this pass's script was written).

- [x] `menus.meal_config_snapshot` exists, is `NOT NULL`, written exactly once at `createMenu`, nothing else writes it (S1, S2) — confirmed by direct code read; snapshot immutability **verified live this pass** (check 1)
