## 23. Week 17: Share-as-image + AI staples note

**Status:** LOCKED. Written 2026-09-11, immediately after W16 closed (§22.6). Two genuinely different halves stitched into one week, same shape as W16: a mobile-only, no-AI half (share-as-image) and this plan's **third** AI feature (after W6's non-AI recipe CRUD and W7's freeform-parse/URL-import pair) — staples note. Unlike W7, this AI feature's Lambda needs BOTH real Aurora access AND real internet egress in the same execution, which no prior week's Lambda has needed — this tension, and how it's resolved, is this week's one genuinely novel architectural decision. **D-numbers are W17-local** (D1–D8).

### 23.1 What W17 is locked to deliver

Per §4's W17 row, PRD §7.1 ("AI feature 4" — `docs/PRD.md:270-275`) and SD §17 Open Q#3 (`docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md:1628-1638`): **share-as-image** — a Flutter-rendered, categorized PNG of the current shopping list, pushed through the native share sheet (`share_plus`) to WhatsApp/Swiggy/Blinkit or wherever the user picks, with a fire-and-forget backup upload to S3 via `exportShoppingListImage`; and **the AI staples note** — a short LLM-generated reminder line ("You might want to check: Kitchen King Masala, jeera, hing," per the PRD's own example) appended to `ShoppingList.aiStaplesNote` (a field the schema has reserved since W11, per `shared/schema.graphql:1020-1032`, and left permanently `null` until this week). Gate, in §4's own words: **"Share sheet works to WhatsApp/Swiggy/Blinkit."**

**D11's provider deviation (§13.2.2) explicitly left W17's AI provider open** — this week re-verifies against a real call rather than trusting W7's Gemini pick by name, per that same section's own warning that a model name in a doc can be deprecated before the plan that named it ships.

**Explicitly out of scope for W17** (owned elsewhere, not oversights): a queryable "export history" screen or table — `exportShoppingListImage`'s S3 upload is the backup itself (30-day bucket lifecycle, already provisioned at `infra/stacks/data-stack.ts:287-292`), not a new DB-tracked feature; live subscription push for the staples note the instant it's generated (D5 below narrows this to "appears on next natural refetch," not an instant push — see D5's own reasoning); a household-scoped (rather than per-user) rate-limit primitive — D7 reuses the existing per-user limiter unchanged; any change to `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList`'s own return latency or transaction shape beyond firing one new async invoke at the very end; W19/W20/W21's own AI features (vision, photo pantry, cook-from-pantry) — this week's NAT Gateway addition unblocks them architecturally but does not implement any of them.

### 23.2 Design

#### 23.2.1 D1 — a NAT Gateway is added to the shared VPC, and a new egress-enabled subnet group is added alongside (not instead of) the existing fully-isolated one

`infra/stacks/network-stack.ts`'s own doc comment (lines 15-22) is explicit that `natGateways: 0` was a deliberate cost call — "a NAT Gateway costs ~$32/mo + data transfer that these [VPC] endpoints avoid entirely." That reasoning held for every Lambda built through W16, because every one of them needed either DB access (VPC-isolated, AWS-service traffic only, covered by the existing Gateway/Interface endpoints) or public-internet access (W7's non-VPC AI Lambdas, no VPC membership at all) — never both in the same execution. W17's staples-note Lambda is the first to need both: it must read the week's real menu/recipe data (Aurora) to build its prompt, and it must reach Gemini's public HTTPS endpoint (no AWS PrivateLink exists for a third-party API), in one execution.

**Locked (founder decision, confirmed AWS Activate credits are active on this account, making the NAT Gateway's ~$32-35/mo immaterial against the $1,000 credit balance):** add exactly one NAT Gateway (`natGateways: 1`, single-AZ — this feature is explicitly best-effort/non-blocking per D5, so a single NAT Gateway's availability profile is an acceptable trade for half the cost of two) and a **new** subnet group, `{ name: 'private-egress', subnetType: SubnetType.PRIVATE_WITH_EGRESS, cidrMask: 24 }`, added to `network-stack.ts`'s existing `subnetConfiguration` array **alongside**, never in place of, the current `{ name: 'isolated', subnetType: SubnetType.PRIVATE_ISOLATED }` group. Aurora and every existing DB-only Lambda (`createDbResolverFunction`'s current `vpcSubnets: { subnetType: SubnetType.PRIVATE_ISOLATED }`, `infra/stacks/api-stack.ts:388`) stay exactly where they are, with zero internet route, unchanged — today's defense-in-depth for the database is not weakened by this week's work. Only the new `staplesNoteFn` (and, by design, any future W19/W20/W21 Lambda that hits this same DB+AI tension) opts into the new egress-enabled subnet, mirroring the exact `needsCuratedRecipes`-flag precedent W16 S5 already established in this same factory (`infra/stacks/api-stack.ts`, `createDbResolverFunction`) for "one resolver needs something the others don't, gated by an opt-in parameter, not a wholesale change to the factory's default."

**Rejected: the two-hop Lambda-chain design** (a non-VPC AI-calling Lambda invoked from the VPC resolver, itself invoking a second VPC Lambda back to write the result) — no recurring AWS cost, but two sequential async hops with their own cold-start variance, a genuinely new cross-Lambda partial-failure story this codebase has never had to design before (AI call succeeds, write-back fails — now what?), and a pattern that would need repeating or generalizing three more times for W19/W20/W21. **Rejected: enabling RDS Data API** on the existing Aurora Serverless v2 cluster — cheaper today at this traffic volume, but `HttpEndpointEnabled` is currently `false` (confirmed live via `aws rds describe-db-clusters` during W16 S6's verification pass) and Data API's request-scoped transaction model is unverified against this codebase's `withUserTransaction`/RLS session-variable design — a real correctness/security risk, not just a latency one, that would need its own spike to trust. The NAT Gateway is the only one of the three options with no unresolved technical unknown, the best latency, and the only one that pays forward into three more weeks' worth of AI features without being redesigned.

**Verification requirement for whichever slice implements this:** `cdk diff` against the real, deployed `Parimaan-dev-Network`/`Parimaan-dev-Data`/`Parimaan-dev-Api` stacks must show only ADDITIONS (the NAT Gateway, the new subnet group, the new Lambda and its security group) — zero replacement or modification of the existing Aurora cluster, existing Lambda functions, or the existing `isolated` subnet group's resources. This is the same "confirm scope via `cdk diff` before deploying anything" discipline used for W16 S6's own live-AWS pass (§22.6).

#### 23.2.2 D2 — the staples-note AI call is a separate, VPC+egress-enabled Lambda (`staplesNoteFn`), invoked asynchronously after `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` commit, never inline in those resolvers' own request/response cycle

`generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` (`api/src/resolvers/generateShoppingList.ts`, `regenerateShoppingList.ts`) return today with `aiStaplesNote: null` (confirmed: `api/src/mappers/shoppingList.ts:96` hardcodes this for a fresh list; line 120's regenerate path just preserves whatever was already on the row). **Locked: this behavior does not change for the synchronous return path.** After each resolver's own `withUserTransaction` scope commits successfully, it fires one asynchronous Lambda invoke (`InvocationType: 'Event'`) at `staplesNoteFn`, passing only `{ listId, householdId }` — never the full menu/recipe payload, keeping the invoke small and avoiding staleness (the async Lambda re-reads the menu fresh, in its own transaction, rather than trusting a payload snapshot that could be seconds stale by the time it runs). This directly mirrors PRD §7.1's own framing for this feature — **"Non-blocking, no writes"** (`docs/PRD.md:270-275`) — read here as: list generation must never wait on, or fail because of, the AI call.

`staplesNoteFn` itself: VPC-attached via D1's new `private-egress` subnet group (so it has both a real Aurora connection AND real internet egress in one execution) — reads the menu/recipes for the given list via its own short read-only transaction, builds the staples-note prompt (D3), calls `invokeModel` (D4, reused unchanged from W7), and on success writes `ai_staples_note` directly via its own Aurora connection — the exact same "VPC Lambda does its own DB write, no intermediary" pattern every other resolver in this codebase already uses; no new mutation, no new AppSync auth mode, no round-trip through GraphQL for the write itself.

**Rejected: firing the async invoke *inside* the open transaction, before commit.** `InvocationType: 'Event'` only guarantees the invoke is queued, not that it will complete before the invoking Lambda's execution ends — firing it before the transaction commits risks `staplesNoteFn` reading a menu/list state that a concurrent rollback could still undo. The invoke fires only after `withUserTransaction`'s callback has returned successfully.

**Deliberately no DLQ for this invoke.** A missed staples note is a UX nicety loss, not a correctness defect — Lambda's own built-in automatic retry (two attempts on an async invocation failure) plus a CloudWatch error-rate alarm on `staplesNoteFn` is judged sufficient visibility for a feature this non-critical; a DLQ-and-redrive story is real infrastructure this week does not need to invent for a best-effort text annotation. Revisit only if a real, observed failure rate in dev/prod later argues otherwise.

#### 23.2.3 D3 — the staples-note prompt inspects the week's menu, not raw pantry/recipe dumps, and the output schema mirrors `invokeModel`'s existing asymmetric-leniency pattern

Per PRD's own description (`docs/PRD.md:270-275`, `docs/PRD.md:120-123`), the feature is: "LLM inspects the week's meal plan [and] produces a short human-readable note." **Locked:** the prompt receives the week's planned recipes' titles, roles, and ingredient lists (already fully available from the same menu read `generateShoppingList` itself does) — never raw pantry contents (this is explicitly NOT a "what's low in your pantry" feature; that's W20/W21's job) and never the shopping list's own post-pantry-subtraction item list (the note is about the PLAN, not the list — asking "what staples does this week's cooking lean on" is a materially different, and more useful, question than "what's still un-bought," which the list itself already shows). Output schema: `{ note: string }`, capped at a short length (locked: **200 characters**, generous enough for the PRD's own multi-item example ("You might want to check: Kitchen King Masala, jeera, hing") with headroom, tight enough that a runaway generation can't produce an essay-length "note" a shopping-list UI has no room for) — validated the same way every other `invokeModel` caller's schema is (§13.2.5's precedent: a draft that cannot be rendered must never be proposed), reusing `invokeModel<T>`'s existing Zod-validation/reinforcement-retry machinery unchanged (`api/src/ai/invokeModel.ts`).

An empty/degenerate week (no recipes planned yet, or a week entirely of dishes with no meaningfully "stockable" staples) is a **valid, non-error output**: the model may return an empty or near-empty note, and `staplesNoteFn` treats that as success (writes it, or writes `null` if the model explicitly signals "nothing worth flagging" via an empty string) — never retries or alarms on a genuinely-empty result, only on a real transport/parse/validation failure per D4's inherited retry taxonomy.

#### 23.2.4 D4 — reuse `invokeModel`/`geminiClient`/`config` from W7 unchanged; re-verify the model choice with a real call before trusting it

**Locked:** `staplesNoteFn` imports `invokeModel` (`api/src/ai/invokeModel.ts`), `callGemini`/`geminiClient` (`api/src/ai/geminiClient.ts`), and `loadAiConfig` (`api/src/ai/config.ts`) exactly as W7 built them — same `AI_DEADLINE_MS = 15_000` shared deadline, same transport/output retry chains, same six-code error taxonomy (`AI_BUSY`, `AI_UNPARSEABLE`, `AI_UNAVAILABLE`, `AI_TIMEOUT`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `VALIDATION`), same `parimaan/gemini-api-key` Secrets Manager secret and cold-start-memoized fetch. **No new AI-invocation code is written this week** — this is exactly the seam §13.3 S2's own doc comment predicted W17 would exercise ("this seam is what lets W17/W19/W20/W21 pick a different provider without touching a resolver").

Per D11's own explicit deferral (§13.2.2 point 7) and this week's own §23.1 framing, the model choice is **re-verified, not assumed**: the slice implementing D3/D4 makes real calls against the staples-note prompt shape (not the freeform-parse prompt W7 measured) before considering `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` locked for this feature too, following the identical three-round discipline W7's own S2 used when its first-choice model turned out deprecated (§13.2.2's own retelling). If the real-call verification holds (expected, since the prompt is shorter and structurally simpler than freeform-parse's), no provider change is needed and W17 stays on Gemini; if it doesn't, the deviation is recorded here exactly as honestly as D11 recorded W7's own.

#### 23.2.5 D5 — the staples note appears on the client's next natural refetch, not as an instant subscription push

`onShoppingListChanged` (`shared/schema.graphql:686-689`) is `@aws_subscribe`d to exactly four named mutations (`generateShoppingList`, `regenerateShoppingList`, `haveIt`, `markPurchased`) — AppSync's subscription fan-out fires only when one of those specific mutations resolves through AppSync itself; a Lambda writing directly to Aurora (D2's own design) does not trigger it. Making the note's arrival push live would require either a fifth `@aws_subscribe`-listed mutation callable only by `staplesNoteFn` (a new IAM-authorized additional-auth-mode on an API that has been single-auth-mode Cognito-only since W1, per `infra/stacks/api-stack.ts:204`) or another new mechanism entirely — real, buildable infrastructure, but for a feature PRD itself frames as "non-blocking" and secondary to the list itself.

**Locked: no live push for MVP.** The note lands in the database asynchronously (typically within `staplesNoteFn`'s own `AI_DEADLINE_MS` budget of the list being generated — a few seconds in the common case) and becomes visible the next time the client fetches the shopping list — reopening the screen, a pull-to-refresh, or any of the four existing subscription pushes that already cause a refetch for an unrelated reason (e.g., a second household member checking off an item triggers `onShoppingListChanged` for `haveIt`, which refetches the whole list including whatever `aiStaplesNote` value is present by then). This is a deliberate, named scope-narrowing: **future work, not this week's job**, is wiring a fifth `@aws_subscribe` mutation + IAM auth mode if instant arrival turns out to matter in practice. Flutter's shopping-list screen needs one small addition regardless: its existing GraphQL query/fragment for `ShoppingList` gains the `aiStaplesNote` field (already schema-present, simply unread by any client query today) and a conditional text widget that renders it when non-null.

#### 23.2.6 D6 — the 24-hr cache key is content-addressed (a hash of the planned recipe set), not the raw `listId`

SD §7.3's own sketch (`docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md`) proposes `"aiCache#staplesNote#{listId}"` as the cache key. **This has a real bug, found and deviated from here rather than copied verbatim:** `generateShoppingList` refuses to create a second list while one is already open, and `regenerateShoppingList` operates on that SAME existing row (same `id`) after the menu's contents change — so a raw `listId`-keyed cache would serve a **stale** note (generated against the pre-regenerate meal plan) for a full 24 hours after a real regenerate changed what's actually planned, precisely the kind of staleness bug this plan has caught before in other weeks (W13's Dinner-clobber bug, §19.2.4; the pantry-hash design already locked for W21's own cook-from-pantry cache, borrowed here).

**Locked:** the cache key is `aiCache#staplesNote#{recipeSetHash}`, where `recipeSetHash` is a deterministic hash (locked: SHA-256, hex-encoded, truncated to 16 chars — plenty of collision resistance for a cache key, short enough to stay a reasonable DynamoDB `SK`) over the sorted list of `(recipeId, servingsOverride)` pairs actually planned for the week the list was generated from — the same shape `generateShoppingList`'s own aggregation step already reads, so `staplesNoteFn` computes this hash from data it's already fetching, no new read. A regenerate that changes even one recipe or one `servingsOverride` produces a different hash, a cache miss, and a fresh (correct) note; a regenerate that changes nothing about the plan (e.g., only had/manual-item state, per D8's own "preserves had/manual items live" framing referenced in §17.2's own S1) correctly reuses the cached note instead of spending another Gemini call on an unchanged question. TTL: 24 hours (`ttl` attribute on `parimaan-cache-{env}`, the existing table from `infra/stacks/data-stack.ts:298-305` — no new table, reusing D1's already-established single-cache-table convention).

#### 23.2.7 D7 — rate limiting reuses `checkAndIncrementDailyAction` unchanged, keyed by the calling user, action name `'staplesNote'`, capped at 20/day

`'staplesNote'` has been a reserved-but-unused action name in the DynamoDB rate-limit table's scheme since W7 (§13.2.9 D8, `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md:1304`). **Locked:** `staplesNoteFn` calls `checkAndIncrementDailyAction(ddbClient, tableName, 'staplesNote', <the user who triggered generateShoppingList/regenerateShoppingList>, 20, <message>)` — the existing per-user-keyed function (`api/src/rateLimit/dailyActionLimiter.ts`), completely unchanged, no household-scoped variant written. The cap (20/day) matches `'freeformParse'`'s own precedent exactly rather than being freshly derived — PRD gives no explicit target for this action, usage is naturally self-limiting (one list can only be generated once, regenerated occasionally, per household per day, per D8's own confirm-gated friction design from W11), and this cap will realistically never bind; matching an existing number rather than inventing a new one is the simpler, equally-correct choice. If the rate limit is ever hit, `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` themselves are **unaffected** — the limiter check happens inside `staplesNoteFn`'s own async execution, after the list has already been generated and returned; a `RATE_LIMITED` result there just means no note gets written this time, logged, not surfaced as a user-facing error anywhere (there is no synchronous caller left to show one to, per D2's own async design).

#### 23.2.8 D8 — `exportShoppingListImage` returns a bare presigned PUT URL (`String!`, matching SD's own sketch exactly), the client renders and uploads, the upload is fire-and-forget

Per SD's own schema sketch (`docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md:709`): `exportShoppingListImage(listId: ID!): String!` — a presigned URL, not the sibling `PresignedUpload!` type SD separately sketches for W20's `getPantryPhotoUploadUrl` (`docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md:712,723-727`). **Locked: keep it a bare `String!`, do not widen it to `PresignedUpload!`.** The two mutations solve different problems — `getPantryPhotoUploadUrl`'s caller needs to remember the `s3Key` to later reference a specific uploaded photo; `exportShoppingListImage`'s caller uploads immediately, once, and never needs to look the object up again (it's a backup, not a referenced asset) — so the extra `s3Key`/`expiresAt` fields `PresignedUpload` carries would go unused. Matching SD's own literal sketch here is a deliberate "don't widen a type beyond what this week's actual use needs," the same restraint this plan already applied in W16 (§22.2.4 D4's choice not to special-case `inRotation` for seeded rows).

**Flow, locked:** the mobile client renders the categorized shopping-list image client-side (`RepaintBoundary` + `RenderRepaintBoundary.toImage()` — Flutter's own built-in mechanism, matching SD §17 Q3's own "lean: client-side Flutter render" framing exactly, no new rendering package needed) into PNG bytes. On a successful share-sheet dismissal (or immediately after rendering — implementation's call, not a locked product decision), the client calls `exportShoppingListImage(listId)`, receives a presigned S3 PUT URL scoped to the household's own list (server-side: verify household membership for `listId` exactly like every other list-scoped mutation, generate a key under `exports/{householdId}/{listId}/{timestamp}.png`, presign a PUT with a short expiry — locked: **10 minutes**, comfortably enough for an immediate client-side upload, tight enough to bound the window a leaked URL is exploitable), and PUTs the same PNG bytes directly to S3. **This upload is fire-and-forget from the user's perspective** — a failure never blocks or interrupts the share-sheet flow the user actually came for; it's logged, not surfaced. `share_plus` (new `mobile/pubspec.yaml` dependency — confirmed absent today, per the research pass above) drives the native share sheet itself, given the locally-rendered PNG as a temp file (`path_provider`, already a dependency).

### 23.3 Slice breakdown

#### S1 — NAT Gateway + egress subnet group (infra only)

- **Delivers:** D1 in full — `network-stack.ts`'s `natGateways: 1` and the new `private-egress` `PRIVATE_WITH_EGRESS` subnet group, added alongside the existing `isolated` group; `createDbResolverFunction` (`api-stack.ts`) gains an opt-in parameter (mirroring `needsCuratedRecipes`'s own shape) so a resolver can request the egress subnet instead of the default isolated one, without changing any existing resolver's placement.
- **Files:** `infra/stacks/network-stack.ts`; `infra/stacks/api-stack.ts` (the opt-in parameter only — no resolver uses it yet, that's S3).
- **Depends on:** nothing — this is the week's first slice, and the only one with no code dependency on any other.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / **Medium** — low code complexity, but a real, stateful VPC change to a live environment; the risk is entirely in verifying zero unintended drift to existing resources, not in the new resources themselves.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` (infra test suite — `infra/test/network-stack.test.ts`, `api-stack.test.ts`) → `database-reviewer` (confirm the isolated subnet's Aurora placement is genuinely untouched) → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** `network-stack.test.ts` asserts exactly one NAT Gateway and exactly one new `PRIVATE_WITH_EGRESS` subnet group exist in the synthesized template, alongside the pre-existing `PRIVATE_ISOLATED` group (both present, neither replacing the other); `api-stack.test.ts` asserts a resolver entry with the new opt-in flag places its Lambda in the egress subnet's route table while every other resolver's synthesized Lambda keeps the exact subnet IDs it had before this change (a template-diff-shaped test, mirroring how `needsCuratedRecipes`' own test confirmed only one Lambda asset carried `recipes/`).
- **Verification:** `cdk diff` against the real deployed dev stacks (not just synth) before merging, confirming only additive changes — the same discipline named in D1's own "Verification requirement" paragraph above.

#### S2 — Staples-note AI module: prompt, output schema, real-call model verification

- **Delivers:** D3/D4 — `api/prompts/staplesNote.ts` (prompt template, `PROMPT_VERSION` constant, matching W7's existing `api/prompts/*.ts` convention); the `{ note: string }` output Zod schema (200-char cap per D3); a small pure function `buildStaplesNoteContext(menu, recipes)` that shapes the menu/recipe data the prompt needs (titles, roles, ingredients — never pantry, never the shopping list itself, per D3). This slice makes real Gemini calls against a handful of representative planned-week shapes (a normal mixed week, an all-South-Indian week, a week with only 1-2 recipes, an edge-case near-empty week) to re-verify `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` holds for this prompt shape (D4's own re-verification requirement) before anything downstream depends on the model choice.
- **Files:** `api/prompts/staplesNote.ts` (new); `api/src/ai/` (no changes — `invokeModel`/`geminiClient`/`config` reused as-is, confirmed by this slice's own real-call tests).
- **Depends on:** nothing code-wise (pure prompt/schema authoring + real-call verification); does not depend on S1.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / **Medium** — the same "prompt quality is the uncertainty, not code" risk framing W7's own S2 used for its own prompt work (§13.3, `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md:1410`).
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** the context-builder produces the expected shape from a fixture menu/recipe set, explicitly excluding any pantry-shaped field; the output schema accepts a well-formed `{ note }` under 200 chars and rejects one over; a real-call smoke test (run manually/recorded, not part of the automated Testcontainers suite the way W7's own real-call verification wasn't either) confirms the chosen model returns a valid, on-topic note for each of the four representative week shapes.

#### S3 — `staplesNoteFn` Lambda: async invocation, DB read/write, cache, rate limit

- **Delivers:** D2, D6, D7 in full — the new Lambda itself (`api/src/resolvers/staplesNoteFn.ts` or a dedicated non-resolver entry point, since this is invoked directly by another Lambda, never by AppSync — naming/location to follow whatever convention this codebase already uses for a Lambda that isn't an AppSync field resolver, if any exists, otherwise a clearly-named new convention documented here); the `recipeSetHash` function (D6); the cache read-before-call / write-after-call against `parimaan-cache-{env}` (reusing `loadCacheTableName`, D6); the `checkAndIncrementDailyAction('staplesNote', ..., 20, ...)` call (D7); the async-invoke trigger added to `generateShoppingList.ts`/`regenerateShoppingList.ts` (fired after `withUserTransaction` commits, per D2's own ordering requirement) using the new `private-egress` subnet from S1.
- **Files:** the new Lambda entry point; `api/src/resolvers/generateShoppingList.ts`; `api/src/resolvers/regenerateShoppingList.ts`; `api/src/repositories/shoppingListRepository.ts` (a small `updateAiStaplesNote(client, listId, note)` function, mirroring existing repository functions' shape exactly); `infra/stacks/api-stack.ts` (registers the new Lambda with S1's opt-in egress flag, grants it `lambda:InvokeFunction` permission from `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList`'s execution roles).
- **Depends on:** S1 (the egress subnet), S2 (the prompt/schema module).
- **Size/Risk:** ~3.5 hrs / **High** — this is this week's highest-risk slice: it's the first Lambda-to-Lambda async invocation in this codebase, touches the two resolvers `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` (both already covered by W11/W12's own extensive test suites, which must keep passing with zero assertion edits, matching W16 S4's own "zero assertion edits" discipline exactly), and is the first real caller of D1's new infrastructure.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `typescript-reviewer` → `database-reviewer` (mandatory — the read/write transaction shape, the cache table access pattern) → `security-reviewer` (mandatory — confirm `staplesNoteFn` cannot be invoked by anything other than the two intended callers' execution roles; confirm no path lets client-influenced data reach the prompt unsanitized beyond what D3 already scopes) → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** `generateShoppingList`'s full pre-existing test suite passes unmodified, plus a new test confirming an async invoke fires exactly once after a successful generation (mocked/spied Lambda client, not a real invoke in the Testcontainers suite) and zero times if the transaction itself fails/rolls back; identical pair of tests for `regenerateShoppingList`; `staplesNoteFn` itself, tested directly (not through the other two resolvers): a cache hit skips the model call entirely and writes the cached note; a cache miss calls the model, writes the result, and writes the cache entry with a 24-hr TTL; a rate-limit exhaustion produces no write and no thrown error visible to any caller (there is none, per D2); a model failure (any of the six taxonomy codes) produces no write, logged, no retry beyond `invokeModel`'s own internal retry chain; two different recipe sets (different `recipeSetHash`) never collide in the cache; the SAME recipe set queried twice within 24 hours produces exactly one real model call.

#### S4 — Mobile: categorized image render + share sheet + backup export

- **Delivers:** D8 in full — a `RepaintBoundary`-wrapped categorized shopping-list render widget (reusing the existing Shopping List screen's own category-grouping logic, not a second implementation of it); a "Share" affordance that captures the boundary to PNG bytes, drives `share_plus`'s native share sheet, and fire-and-forgets the `exportShoppingListImage` upload afterward; the **Share image preview** wireframe screen itself (44/50); the small `aiStaplesNote` field addition to the existing Shopping List screen's GraphQL query/fragment plus a conditional text render (D5).
- **Files:** `mobile/pubspec.yaml` (`share_plus` added); a new share-preview widget/screen under `mobile/lib/features/shopping_list/`; the existing Shopping List screen's GraphQL document (field addition only).
- **Depends on:** nothing server-side for the image/share path (pure client rendering); depends on S3 for the `aiStaplesNote` field to ever be non-null in practice, though the client-side field addition itself can be written and tested against a fixture/mock before S3 ships.
- **Size/Risk:** ~3.0 hrs / Medium — no new architectural surface, but real device-level testing (share sheet behavior genuinely differs iOS vs. Android, and WhatsApp/Swiggy/Blinkit's own share-target handling can't be fully verified in a simulator alone).
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` (widget tests) → `flutter-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** the render widget produces the expected category grouping from a fixture shopping list (same grouping order/logic as the existing Shopping List screen, asserted equal, not re-derived); the share action captures a non-empty PNG (widget test, mocked `share_plus` call, asserting it's invoked with the right file); a failed `exportShoppingListImage` call never blocks or errors the share-sheet flow (D8's own "fire-and-forget" requirement, directly tested); the Shopping List screen renders the staples-note text when `aiStaplesNote` is non-null and renders nothing (no empty box, no placeholder) when it's null.

#### S5 — Real-AWS verification + weekly doc pass

- **Delivers:** live verification, per this plan's own standing convention (every server-shipping week gets one, §22.2.6 D6's own "the standard S7 posture applies unmodified" framing) — a real `generateShoppingList` invoke against dev Aurora/AppSync for a throwaway household with a real planned week confirms: the async invoke to `staplesNoteFn` actually fires and actually reaches Gemini through the new NAT Gateway (the single most safety-critical claim from S1/S3, re-verified live exactly the way W16 S6 re-verified S5's own packaging fix); `ai_staples_note` is written and visible on a subsequent fetch; a regenerate that changes the planned recipes produces a different (correctly cache-missed) note, not a stale cached one (D6's own bug-fix, verified live, not just in Testcontainers); the rate limit and cache both function against the real `parimaan-cache-dev` table; `exportShoppingListImage` returns a real presigned URL and a real PUT to it succeeds against the real `parimaan-exports-dev` bucket. §4.2's mandatory weekly pass — actual-vs-planned hours, a decisions-versus-shipped audit of §23.4's locked-decisions table below.
- **Files:** `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md` (§4 row, a W17-result subsection); `docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md` (only if its own §7.3/§17 sketches have gone stale relative to what actually shipped — D6 already found one real gap in SD's own cache-key sketch, worth reconciling here the same way W16 §22.6 reconciled SD-adjacent gaps).
- **Depends on:** all slices.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hrs / Medium, non-optional — higher than W16 S6's own ~1.0 hr estimate specifically because this week's live verification includes a genuinely new infrastructure claim (NAT Gateway egress actually works end-to-end) that W16's verification didn't have an equivalent of.
- **Agents:** `doc-updater`.
- **Verification checks:** the exact list in the "Delivers" line above, each confirmed against real dev Aurora/AppSync/DynamoDB/S3, not only Testcontainers; throwaway household deleted afterward, matching every prior week's own throwaway-household discipline.

**Planned total: ~11.5 hrs** — higher than W16's ~9.0 hrs, concentrated in S3 (this week's S4-equivalent highest-risk slice) and S1's genuine infrastructure change, the two slices with no direct precedent anywhere earlier in this plan.

### 23.4 Locked decisions (D1–D8)

| # | Decision | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| **D1** | How does one Lambda get both real Aurora access and real internet egress? | **A NAT Gateway** (single-AZ) plus a new `PRIVATE_WITH_EGRESS` subnet group, added alongside (never replacing) the existing fully-isolated one. Founder-confirmed given active AWS Activate credits make the ~$32-35/mo cost immaterial. Rejected: a two-hop Lambda chain (no cost, but new untested orchestration); RDS Data API (cheapest, but an unverified RLS/session-variable risk). |
| **D2** | Where does the AI call happen relative to `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList`? | **A separate Lambda (`staplesNoteFn`), invoked asynchronously after the transaction commits** — never inline, never before commit. List generation's own latency and correctness are completely unaffected by the AI call's success or failure. |
| **D3** | What does the prompt see, and what does it return? | **The week's planned recipes (titles, roles, ingredients) — never pantry, never the shopping list itself.** Output: `{ note: string }`, capped at 200 characters, validated via `invokeModel`'s existing Zod machinery. |
| **D4** | Which AI provider/invocation layer? | **Reuse `invokeModel`/`geminiClient`/`config` from W7 unchanged** — no new AI-invocation code. The model choice (`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`) is re-verified with real calls against this feature's own prompt shape before being trusted, per D11's own standing "don't trust a model name in a doc" warning. |
| **D5** | Does the note appear via live subscription push? | **No — next natural refetch only**, for MVP. `onShoppingListChanged`'s `@aws_subscribe` list is not extended this week (would require a new IAM auth mode on a Cognito-only API); deferred as named future work, not silently dropped. |
| **D6** | What's the 24-hr cache key? | **A content hash of the planned recipe set (`recipeSetHash`), not the raw `listId`** — a real bug found in SD §7.3's own `listId`-keyed sketch (a regenerate reuses the same list id, so a raw-id key would serve a stale note for 24 hours after the plan actually changed) and deviated from here, deliberately. |
| **D7** | Rate limiting? | **Reuse `checkAndIncrementDailyAction` unchanged, per-user, `'staplesNote'` action (reserved since W7), capped at 20/day** — matching `'freeformParse'`'s own precedent rather than deriving a fresh number PRD never specified. |
| **D8** | `exportShoppingListImage`'s return shape and upload flow? | **Bare `String!` presigned PUT URL** (matching SD's own sketch, not widened to `PresignedUpload!` — the extra fields would go unused). Client renders client-side (`RepaintBoundary`, no new rendering package), uploads fire-and-forget after sharing — a failed backup never blocks the share-sheet flow the user actually came for. |

**Decision density: 8 locked** — one more than W16's 6, driven by D1's genuinely novel architecture question (the first week to need this plan's own explicit founder sign-off on a recurring infrastructure cost since Q3/Q11's original AWS-account decisions) and D6's real gap-catch against SD's own sketch. Unlike W16 (which mostly executed prior commitments), W17 makes several new product/architecture calls fresh — closer in decision shape to W11/W12 than to W15/W16.

### 23.5 S5 result — real-AWS verification (a second production-breaking bug found and reproduced live) + weekly doc pass

`AWS_PROFILE=parimaan-dev aws sts get-caller-identity` succeeded before anything else (account `917246556431`, `ap-south-1`), so this pass had real deploy access from the start — unlike §22.5's own position, this is closer in shape to §22.6.

#### The `exportShoppingListImage` gap, checked first, per this slice's own instruction

`grep -rn "exportShoppingListImage" shared/schema.graphql` returns **zero matches**. `grep -rln "exportShoppingListImage" api/src/resolvers/` also returns **zero matches**. The mutation does not exist on `main`, server-side, at all — not in the SDL, not as a resolver. §23.3 S3's own "Delivers" line only ever committed to `staplesNoteFn` (D2/D6/D7); S3 built no resolver for `exportShoppingListImage`, and §23.3 S4's own "Delivers" line built only the mobile client's rendering/share-sheet/upload-attempt half, against a repository interface it explicitly stubs: `mobile/lib/features/shopping_list/data/shopping_list_image_export_repository.dart`'s own doc comment states outright, *"`exportShoppingListImage` is not wired to the real GraphQL mutation yet."* **This is confirmed here as a real, load-bearing gap in what W17 as a whole was supposed to deliver.** D8's entire design (§23.2.8 — the presigned-PUT-URL shape, the 10-minute expiry, the `exports/{householdId}/{listId}/{timestamp}.png` key convention) is fully speculative until a future slice actually builds it through the normal TDD pipeline; none of it was built or verified this week, and it is **not** built here either, per this slice's own explicit scope (a docs-and-verification slice does not implement gaps it finds). Every D8-specific item in §23.3 S5's original "Delivers" line — a real presigned URL, a real PUT against `parimaan-exports-dev` — is therefore **not verifiable and not verified**, named plainly rather than skipped.

#### `cdk diff` and deploy

`cdk diff` against real, deployed `Parimaan-dev-Network`/`Parimaan-dev-Api` (with `Parimaan-dev-Data`/`Parimaan-dev-Auth` pulled in automatically as dependency stacks by `cdk diff`/`cdk deploy`, not by choice) showed exactly what D1's own verification requirement calls for: **Network** — only additions (one NAT Gateway + EIP, two new `private-egress` subnets with their route tables/associations/default routes, matching the two-AZ shape the existing `isolated` group already has). **Api** — only additions/expected updates (new `StaplesNoteFn` + its role/policy/error alarm; new `lambda:InvokeFunction` grants from `GenerateShoppingListFn`/`RegenerateShoppingListFn`'s roles to `StaplesNoteFn`; both those two functions' code assets and a new `STAPLES_NOTE_FN_NAME` env var — all exactly what S3's own diff should look like). **Auth** — no changes. **Data** — one unrelated `MigrationRunnerFn` code-asset hash change (a bundler/Node-version artifact from this environment, not a W17 code change — `git diff` on `api/src/migrations/` against `main` is empty); `cdk deploy Parimaan-dev-Network Parimaan-dev-Api` pulled `Parimaan-dev-Data` in anyway as a stack dependency and deployed that one hash change along with it. This is named honestly as **not fully matching the instruction to touch only Network/Api** — it wasn't a deliberate choice, it's `cdk deploy`'s own default behavior of including dependency stacks, and the change deployed was a no-op content-wise (same migration code, different bundle hash), but a future pass should know `--exclusively` is the flag to prevent this if strict two-stack scoping is required again.

Deploy succeeded: `Parimaan-dev-Api` deployment time 207.6s, total 454s. `StaplesNoteFn` created; `GenerateShoppingListFn`/`RegenerateShoppingListFn` updated with the new env var and IAM grant.

#### A second production-breaking bug, found live, on the very first real invocation of D2's design — the async invoke never fires

A throwaway household (`createHousehold`, direct Lambda invoke with a synthetic Cognito identity mirroring `createHousehold.test.ts`'s own `buildEvent` shape, per this session's own established pattern) received its full **53-recipe** curated corpus, confirming W16's seeder is still healthy. A real menu was created and three real curated recipes were planned onto it (`addMenuItem` × 3: Aloo Paratha, Aloo Gobi, later Avial).

**`generateShoppingList` timed out** — `FunctionError: "Unhandled"`, `Sandbox.Timedout`, `"Task timed out after 45.00 seconds"`. A second call for the same menu returned `CONFLICT: "A shopping list already exists for this menu"` in under two seconds, proving the shopping list **had** in fact been written correctly by the first call's transaction — `withUserTransaction`'s own commit is not the problem, D2's ordering is implemented exactly as designed. What hangs is the line immediately after: `await invoke({ listId, householdId })` (`generateShoppingList.ts`), which calls `invokeStaplesNoteFn` (`api/src/aiInvoke/staplesNoteInvoker.ts`) — a plain `LambdaClient.send(InvokeCommand)` to the AWS Lambda control-plane API.

**Root cause, confirmed by direct code read of `infra/stacks/network-stack.ts`:** `GenerateShoppingListFn`/`RegenerateShoppingListFn` are **not** in D1's new `private-egress` subnet (`resolverEntries.ts` sets no `needsInternetEgress` flag for either — correct, per D1's own design, since neither needs to call Gemini or the internet). They stay in the `isolated` subnet, which has **zero route to the internet and zero route to the new NAT Gateway** by design. The `isolated` subnet's only outbound paths are the four VPC endpoints network-stack.ts provisions: S3, DynamoDB (gateway endpoints), Bedrock Runtime, Secrets Manager (interface endpoints). **There is no `InterfaceVpcEndpointAwsService.LAMBDA` endpoint anywhere in this file.** So when a Lambda sitting in the `isolated` subnet tries to call `lambda.ap-south-1.amazonaws.com`'s `InvokeFunction` API — exactly what firing an async invoke to `staplesNoteFn` requires — the outbound TCP SYN has no route out of the VPC at all and is silently dropped (no ICMP unreachable, no fast connection-refused), so the SDK call hangs until *something* kills it. Nothing does, until the Lambda's own 45-second function timeout does, well past `invokeStaplesNoteFn`'s own `catch` block ever getting a chance to run (confirmed: the CloudWatch log for the timed-out invocation shows no `console.error` at all — the function died mid-`await`, before its own error-swallowing code could execute).

**This directly violates D2's own explicit, named guarantee** — *"list generation's own latency and correctness are completely unaffected by the AI call's success or failure"* (§23.2.2) — because right now, on real dev, every genuinely new `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` call **times out and returns an error to the caller**, even though the shopping list itself is correctly persisted server-side. This is worse than "the staples note silently doesn't arrive" (D2's own stated acceptable-failure mode); it is a visible, blocking failure of the feature `generateShoppingList` exists to provide, introduced entirely by W17's own change (env var + invoke call added to an otherwise-unmodified resolver) landing on infrastructure (D1's subnet split) that never gave the caller a way to reach the Lambda API at all. **No local check could have caught this** — the same class of finding as W16's own `import.meta.url` bug (§22.6): `generateShoppingList.test.ts`/`regenerateShoppingList.test.ts` both inject a spied `invokeStaplesNoteFn` (by design, per S3's own RED-test list), so they never exercise the real network path; `cdk synth`/`cdk diff` only inspect declared resources, never actual reachability between them; Testcontainers has no VPC networking model at all. Only a real invocation against real dev VPC networking could surface this, and this pass is the first time D2's design has ever actually run end-to-end against real infrastructure.

**Not fixed here, per this slice's own explicit scope** ("if you find yourself wanting to... fix any other gap you find, stop and just document the finding instead"). The fix is well-understood and small — either add a `com.amazonaws.ap-south-1.lambda` interface endpoint to the `isolated` subnet's endpoint list (letting every isolated-subnet Lambda reach the Lambda control plane the same way it already reaches Secrets Manager/Bedrock), or move `GenerateShoppingListFn`/`RegenerateShoppingListFn` onto the `private-egress` subnet themselves — but implementing either is real infrastructure work belonging to its own slice through the normal TDD pipeline (a new `network-stack.test.ts` assertion, a real `cdk diff`/deploy verification cycle of its own), not a docs-and-verification pass.

#### Working around the bug to still verify D1/D3/D4/D6/D7 in isolation

Because the intended calling path is broken, `staplesNoteFn` was invoked **directly** (bypassing the broken `invokeStaplesNoteFn` call) to verify everything downstream of the invoke that D1/D3/D4/D6/D7 actually claim, since `staplesNoteFn` itself sits correctly in the `private-egress` subnet and was never implicated in the bug above:

1. **Direct invoke with a fake `listId`, real `householdId`:** executed in 390ms, logged `staplesNoteFn: could not resolve a planned menu for listId=...` and exited cleanly — no hang. Confirms the `private-egress` subnet's Aurora connectivity (unrelated to D1's own new NAT/egress claim, but a useful negative control ruling out "the whole subnet is broken").
2. **Real `listId`, obtained via `regenerateShoppingList(confirmed: false)`** (the preview branch, which per its own doc "never writes anything" and **never invokes `staplesNoteFn`** — the one read path unaffected by the bug above) against the already-committed list. Direct invoke of `staplesNoteFn` with this real `listId`/`householdId` completed in **1355ms with no error logged** — **the single most safety-critical infrastructure claim of the week, confirmed live**: `staplesNoteFn` genuinely reached Gemini through the new NAT Gateway and got a real response back. A follow-up `regenerateShoppingList(confirmed: false)` fetch confirmed `aiStaplesNote` was written: *"You might need Amchur (Dry Mango Powder) and Garam Masala this week — check you have some."*
3. **Cache confirmed directly against `parimaan-cache-dev`:** `aws dynamodb scan` found `PK = "aiCache#staplesNote#75a3ea211f154e62"`, `SK = "NOTE"`, the exact note text above, with a 24-hour-out `ttl` — D6's key shape confirmed live, not only read from source.
4. **Cache-hit path confirmed:** re-invoking `staplesNoteFn` with the identical `listId`/`householdId` (same planned recipe set, same hash) completed in **302ms** — no Gemini round-trip latency, consistent with `resolveNote`'s own cache-hit short-circuit (source-read-confirmed to skip the model call and the rate-limit check entirely on a hit).
5. **D6's own bug-fix confirmed live, not only in Testcontainers:** a fourth recipe (Avial) was added to the same menu via a real `addMenuItem` call, changing the planned recipe set without changing `listId`. Re-invoking `staplesNoteFn` with the **same `listId`** produced a **different** note — *"You might need Cumin Seeds and Amchur (Dry Mango Powder) this week — check you have some."* — and a **second, distinct** DynamoDB item, `PK = "aiCache#staplesNote#9c5ae158b03a0222"`. Two different hashes, two different cached notes, same `listId` throughout: this is a direct, live reproduction of exactly the staleness bug D6 named and fixed relative to SD §7.3's own `listId`-keyed sketch — proof the fix holds under real regenerate-after-commit conditions, not just a unit test's simulation of one.
6. **Rate limit confirmed directly against `parimaan-cache-dev`:** `PK = "RATELIMIT#staplesNote#14196639-47ac-4dff-b6d3-f315a390ad0a"` (the household's `primaryUserId` — D7's own documented deviation, confirmed live, see below), `attempts: 2` — exactly the two real cache-miss/model-call invocations above (steps 2 and 5); the cache-hit invocation (step 4) correctly did **not** increment it, confirming `resolveNote`'s cache-hit-skips-rate-limit-check ordering live.
7. **Cleanup:** the throwaway household was deleted via a real `deleteHousehold` invoke (`confirmationName` matching, per the mutation's own existing contract) and returned `true`, matching every prior week's throwaway-household discipline. The DynamoDB cache/rate-limit rows created during this pass were left in place to expire via their own TTLs (24h/26h) rather than manually deleted — consistent with how this plan has never manually cleaned up cache rows in any prior week's verification pass either.

#### Local verification (clean `main` + this PR's own worktree checkout)

`pnpm test` in `api/`: **136 test files, 1511 tests, all passed** (308s), including `generateShoppingList.test.ts`/`regenerateShoppingList.test.ts` with zero assertion edits, `staplesNoteFn`'s own direct-unit-test file, and `createHousehold.test.ts` unmodified. `pnpm run validate:recipes` in `api/`: **17 tests, all passed** — `find recipes/north-indian -name '*.json' | wc -l` → 30, `find recipes/south-indian -name '*.json' | wc -l` → 23, combined **53**, matching what the live-created household actually received. `pnpm test` in `infra/`: first run showed 4 failures under a `vitest-worker: Timeout calling "onTaskUpdate"` error — a resource-contention flake from this environment's own concurrent CDK-bundling load, not a code regression; an immediate clean rerun (no other bundling running concurrently) passed **193/193, 7/7 files**, confirmed clean.

#### Decisions-versus-shipped audit (§23.4 D1–D8)

| # | Decision | Shipped as decided? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | NAT Gateway (single-AZ) + new `private-egress` subnet group, additive only | **Yes, as literally decided — but the design built around it (D2) has an unaddressed gap.** | `cdk diff` against real dev showed additions only (NAT Gateway, EIP, two `private-egress` subnets + route tables) — D1's own verification requirement met exactly. `StaplesNoteFn` correctly opted in (`needsInternetEgress: true`, confirmed via `createDbResolverFunction(..., false, true)` in `api-stack.ts`) and its NAT-routed Gemini reachability is directly confirmed live (see above). The gap: D1 never provisioned any path for an **isolated-subnet** Lambda to reach the Lambda control-plane API, which D2's own design requires of `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` — see the production-breaking bug above. |
| D2 | Async invoke after commit, never inline/before-commit, latency/correctness of list generation unaffected by the AI call | **Shipped exactly as decided IN CODE — but the "unaffected" guarantee is currently false in production.** | Source read confirms the invoke fires only after `withUserTransaction` resolves in both resolvers, exactly D2's own ordering requirement. But live testing shows every real caller now blocks for 45 seconds and receives a timeout error, because the invoke call itself hangs — directly contradicting D2's own explicit "latency and correctness... completely unaffected" claim. This is the week's most important finding, named in full above. |
| D3 | Prompt sees planned recipes only, never pantry/list; output `{ note }`, 200-char cap | **Yes.** | `api/prompts/staplesNote.ts`'s `StaplesNoteRecipeInput` read directly — `id`/`title`/`role`/`ingredients` only, explicit doc comment naming everything deliberately excluded. Both live-generated notes ("Amchur... Garam Masala", "Cumin Seeds... Amchur") are staples/spice-shaped, consistent with the intended framing, well under 200 characters. |
| D4 | Reuse `invokeModel`/`geminiClient`/`config` unchanged; re-verify `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` for this prompt shape | **Yes.** | `staplesNoteFn.ts` imports `invokeModel` directly, no new AI-invocation code (confirmed via S2/S3 diffs). Two live model calls both succeeded (no `AI_*` error codes, ~1.0-1.4s latency each) — the model choice holds for this prompt shape, confirmed live in addition to S2's own real-call verification. |
| D5 | No live subscription push; next-refetch only | **Yes (by design; not independently re-exercised this pass).** | Confirmed via source read only — `onShoppingListChanged`'s `@aws_subscribe` list is unchanged (still the four W11-era mutations); `staplesNoteFn` writes directly to Aurora with no AppSync mutation in the path. Not re-verified live this pass since it requires a connected subscription client, which was out of scope for a Lambda-invoke-based verification pass. |
| D6 | Content-addressed `recipeSetHash` cache key, not raw `listId` | **Yes — the strongest live confirmation in this pass.** | Two distinct `aiCache#staplesNote#{hash}` DynamoDB items, two distinct notes, same `listId` throughout, directly reproducing and closing the exact staleness scenario D6 named relative to SD §7.3's sketch. See step 5 above. |
| D7 | Reuse `checkAndIncrementDailyAction`, `'staplesNote'` action, 20/day, keyed by the calling user | **Shipped differently — a documented, deliberate deviation, confirmed live.** | `staplesNoteFn.ts`'s own `resolveActingUserId` doc names this explicitly: D2's invoke payload carries no caller identity, so the household's `primaryUserId` is used as a stable substitute instead of "the calling user" literally. Confirmed live: `RATELIMIT#staplesNote#14196639-...` (the household's `primaryUserId`, not any per-request caller id), `attempts: 2`, incrementing only on real model calls (not on the cache hit). Matches §22.5's own precedent for recording a self-documented deviation as "shipped differently," not silently absorbed. |
| D8 | `exportShoppingListImage` — bare `String!` presigned PUT, client renders + fire-and-forget uploads | **Not shipped — a real, confirmed gap, not a doc oversight.** | `grep` of `shared/schema.graphql` and `api/src/resolvers/` both return zero matches for `exportShoppingListImage`. No resolver, no SDL field, nothing to verify against real S3/`parimaan-exports-dev`. The client-side stub (`shopping_list_image_export_repository.dart`) already names this itself in its own doc comment. See the gap section above. |

**Two of eight decisions did not land as their own text promises: D1/D2's combined design has an unaddressed gap (blocking, confirmed reproducible, not fixed here) and D8 was never built server-side at all (confirmed absent, not fixed here). D6/D7 are the strongest results of this pass — both fully confirmed live, including a real reproduction of D6's own staleness fix under live regenerate conditions. D3/D4 hold. D5 is unchanged and untested this pass by design.**

#### Actual-vs-planned hours (W17)

Inferred from commit timestamps (`git log`, IST timestamps), per this doc's own standing convention (§19.8/§20.8/§21.5/§22.5) — not a timesheet, and these are AI-agent-session times, not directly comparable to human hours:

| Slice | Planned | Commit | Rough actual |

| Plan lock | — | `3919fee` (2026-09-11 22:42:33) | — (this week's practical start, same convention as prior weeks) |
| S2 — staples-note AI module (shipped first — see note below) | 2.0 hr | `69832d5` (23:00:17) | ~0.30 hr from plan lock |
| S1 — NAT Gateway + egress subnet (shipped second) | 1.5 hr | `2dea15b` (23:11:18) | ~0.18 hr from S2 |
| S4 — mobile share-as-image + staples note display (shipped third, ahead of S3) | 3.0 hr | `2637104` (23:55:08) | ~0.73 hr from S1 |

**One real sequencing deviation, named here rather than silently absorbed:** the actual ship order was **plan lock → S2 → S1 → S4 → S3**, not §23.3's own S1→S2→S3→S4 heading order. §23.3 S2's own dependency text already allowed this ("does not depend on S1"), and S3's own text allowed shipping after S4 was never explicitly ruled out either — but S4 (mobile) landing *before* S3 (the Lambda `aiStaplesNote` actually depends on) is notable: §23.3 S4's own "Depends on" line already named this as expected ("depends on S3 for the `aiStaplesNote` field to ever be non-null in practice, though the client-side field addition itself can be written and tested against a fixture/mock before S3 ships"), so this is a plan-anticipated deviation, not an unplanned one — recorded here for completeness the way W16's own S4-before-S2/S3 deviation was.

**Rough total actual (plan lock through S3): ~1.59 hr against those four slices' planned ~10.0 hr** (2.0 + 1.5 + 3.0 + 3.5) — under, continuing the same direction as every week since W12, though this week's own real-AWS pass found the highest-stakes live bug of any week's verification slice so far, which no amount of the underlying code-writing speed would have caught.

**W17 closes here, honestly incomplete.** Share-as-image's client half (S4) and the AI staples-note generation/cache/rate-limit half (S2/S3) both work as designed **in isolation** — confirmed live, including the single hardest infrastructure claim of the week (real NAT-routed Gemini egress) and D6's own staleness-bug fix reproduced under real regenerate conditions. But the two halves do not currently compose correctly in production: **the feature's own intended trigger path — a real user calling `generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` — is broken right now on dev**, timing out every such call because the isolated subnet those resolvers live in has no route to the Lambda control-plane API needed to fire the async invoke at all. And **`exportShoppingListImage`, the other named deliverable of the week, was never built server-side**, confirmed absent rather than assumed. Both are real, load-bearing gaps, named here plainly rather than rounded up to "shipped" — each needs its own slice, through the normal TDD pipeline, before this week's own gate ("Share sheet works to WhatsApp/Swiggy/Blinkit," §23.1) or PRD §7.1's AI-feature-4 claim can honestly be called done.

**Addendum (2026-09-12, same day): both gaps above are now closed, not left for a future week.** A dedicated same-day fix PR added the missing Lambda service VPC interface endpoint to the `isolated` subnet — live-reverified at 824ms (was a 45s timeout), `staplesNoteFn`'s own downstream Gemini call/cache write confirmed succeeding on the same trace. A follow-up slice (S6) built `exportShoppingListImage` server-side exactly per D8's locked design and wired the mobile client's existing stub to the real mutation — live-verified with a real presigned PUT landing an object in `parimaan-exports-dev` and a real non-member denial. Both fixes are recorded in full in `docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md`'s own W17 amendment (§12.3) rather than re-litigated here — this line exists only so a reader of this section in isolation doesn't mistake a same-day-closed gap for a standing one.
