## 24. Week 18: Web dashboard (read + limited edit) + Month 4 demo

**Status:** LOCKED. Written 2026-09-12, immediately after W17 closed (including its same-day follow-up fixes, addendum above). This is the first week building an entirely new client surface — the first web app, the first non-Flutter/non-Ferry GraphQL client (urql, not Ferry), the first AWS Amplify Hosting resource, and the first new top-level CDK stack (`frontend-stack`) since `network-stack`/`data-stack`/`auth-stack`/`api-stack` were built in W1/W3/W4. **D-numbers are W18-local** (D1–D8).

### 24.1 What W18 is locked to deliver

Per §4's W18 row, WS-4 (§2), SD §9.2, and PRD's own web-dashboard description (`docs/PRD.md:238-242`): four web-only screens — **sign-in**, **dashboard** (read-only pantry + meal-plan + shopping-list views), **recipes** (full CRUD — create/edit/delete, including URL import and freeform-paste entry, explicitly called out by PRD as "easier on desktop"), and **household settings admin** (meal structure, cuisine, dietary — same reasoning) — deployed to `dev.parimaan.app` via a new `frontend-stack` CDK stack wrapping AWS Amplify Hosting.

**A real inconsistency found in this plan's own prior text, resolved here rather than silently picked:** §4's own Phase-4 goal one-liner (`docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md:124`) says "read-only web dashboard," but that same Phase's own DoD bullet three lines later (`:130`) says "edit for recipes + settings admin" — a real edit capability, not read-only. **Locked: the DoD bullet is authoritative.** It matches WS-4's own fuller description (§2) and SD §9.2's explicit scope line ("add + edit recipes... household settings admin") word for word; the shorter "read-only" phrasing in the Phase goal is the imprecise one, and is not followed literally. Recipes and settings get real edit capability this week; pantry and meal-plan stay view-only, matching PRD's own explicit "NOT on web MVP: meal-plan calendar interactions (view-only)" — full parity is named v1.1 scope (`docs/PRD.md:307`), not this week's job.

**Explicitly out of scope for W18** (owned elsewhere, not oversights): pantry photo AI on web (PRD's own explicit exclusion, `docs/PRD.md:241`); meal-plan calendar editing on web (same line, and SD §9.2's own "No meal-plan calendar editing on web MVP" — view-only, per D5 below); a household switcher for users belonging to more than one household (D3 below — a real gap this week is the *first* to need an answer for, since mobile has never needed one either, per `docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md:1323`'s own "there is no household-switcher UI in the app today"); Detox/Playwright E2E test authoring — a 2026-09 re-read of `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md:3116`'s own "(R16, W18)" parenthetical found this was an early aspiration that never actually made it into §4's own W18 row or DoD; named here as a found inconsistency rather than silently carried forward or silently dropped — E2E authoring is deferred to a to-be-assigned future week, not claimed as done here and not abandoned either; any AI feature on web (staples note, cook-from-pantry, etc.) — none of W7/W17's AI features get a web surface this phase.

### 24.2 Design

#### 24.2.1 D1 — the Cognito web app client already exists; this week consumes it, never builds it

`infra/stacks/auth-stack.ts` already defines a second, confidential app client (`webClient`, `generateSecret: true`) alongside the mobile app's public PKCE client (`mobileClient`) — built ahead of this week, with callback/logout URLs already shaped correctly for NextAuth's own default Cognito-provider path (`https://{webDomain}/api/auth/callback/cognito`) and for both `dev`/`prod`'s own `webDomain` (`dev.parimaan.app`/`parimaan.app`). **Locked: W18 does not touch `auth-stack.ts`'s client definitions at all** — `frontend-stack.ts` takes `AuthStack.webClient` as a cross-stack prop (the same pattern `ApiStack` already uses for `vpc`/`dbCluster`/etc.), exactly as if this were any other already-built dependency.

The one real gap: the web client's generated secret is deliberately **not** exported via `CfnOutput` (`auth-stack.ts`'s own comment: "not mobile-facing config"), so there is no already-published value for `frontend-stack` to read the way the mobile app's own config values are published. **Locked: the web client's id and secret are written into a new Secrets Manager secret (`parimaan/web-client-credentials` or similar, named consistently with the existing `parimaan/gemini-api-key`/`parimaan/google-oauth-secret` convention) by `auth-stack.ts` at deploy time**, and NextAuth's own server-side config reads it at cold start via the exact same `fetchXFromSecretsManager`-shaped pattern `geminiClient.ts` already established (memoized per warm instance, fetched once). **Rejected: relying on Amplify Hosting's own "compute role" + a runtime `cognito-idp:DescribeUserPoolClient` call** — this is a real, documented AWS pattern for exactly this problem, but Amplify Hosting's compute-role IAM support is a newer, less-exercised corner of the CDK Amplify L2 construct (confirmed: `@aws-cdk/aws-amplify-alpha` isn't even a dependency yet, §24.2.6 D6), and this codebase already has a working, reviewed, test-covered Secrets-Manager-fetch pattern it can reuse unchanged rather than learning a second secret-delivery mechanism for one client.

#### 24.2.2 D2 — NextAuth's JWT session persists the raw Cognito ID token; both SSR and client components read it for urql's `Authorization` header

AppSync's API has exactly one auth mode — `AuthorizationType.USER_POOL` (`infra/stacks/api-stack.ts:221-232`), no IAM/API-key additional modes — so every urql request, server or client side, must carry a real Cognito-issued JWT in its `Authorization` header, the identical requirement Ferry's mobile client already satisfies via Amplify Auth's own token management. NextAuth's default behavior re-encrypts its OWN session token for the browser cookie and does **not**, by default, expose the underlying provider's raw ID/access token to application code.

**Locked:** NextAuth's `jwt` callback persists the Cognito ID token (and its expiry, and the refresh token) into NextAuth's own JWT payload; the `session` callback surfaces the ID token onto the session object read by both `getServerSession` (server components, Route Handlers) and `useSession` (client components) — this is NextAuth's own documented extension point for exactly this "I need the raw provider token, not just a session" need, not a workaround. Token refresh reuses NextAuth's own built-in OAuth refresh-token rotation (Cognito's OAuth code flow already issues a refresh token; NextAuth's `jwt` callback re-runs on every session check and is where an expired-ID-token refresh already belongs) — **no custom refresh logic is written this week**.

#### 24.2.3 D3 — the web dashboard defaults to the caller's first household; no switcher this week

`User.households: [HouseholdMembership!]!` already supports a user belonging to more than one household, but neither mobile nor web has ever built a switcher (`docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md:1323`'s own "no household-switcher UI in the app today," confirmed true for mobile as of this week). **Locked: the web dashboard resolves `me.households[0]`** (the caller's first/primary membership, matching whichever ordering `Query.me`'s own resolver already returns — read it before assuming array order, don't re-derive a new ordering rule) **as the active household for the whole session**, with no UI to change it. This is not a regression against mobile (which has the identical gap) and is named explicitly as a real, deliberate scope line — not an oversight — so it doesn't get silently assumed solved later. A switcher, for either client, is future work.

#### 24.2.4 D4 — a new, additive `Query.shoppingList(menuId: ID!): ShoppingList` read query; the web dashboard never calls a mutation to "read" the list

`generateShoppingList`/`regenerateShoppingList` are the only two ways to reach a `ShoppingList` in the current schema, and both are mutations — `generateShoppingList` specifically **throws `CONFLICT`** if a list already exists for the menu (confirmed live during W17 S5's own verification pass, §23.5), so it is not safely callable as an idempotent "get." **Locked:** add `shoppingList(menuId: ID!): ShoppingList` to `Query` — a small, pure read resolver (household-membership-gated, identical denial pattern to every other list-scoped field) reusing whatever repository lookup `generateShoppingList`'s own "does a list already exist" check already performs (read `generateShoppingList.ts` first; do not write a second, parallel lookup). Returns `null` when no list has been generated yet for that menu — the dashboard's own empty state, not an error. This is new server surface, genuinely needed (the gap was real, not assumed), and small enough to ship alongside the web work itself rather than needing its own week.

#### 24.2.5 D5 — meal-plan and pantry stay view-only on web; recipes and settings get real edit capability

Per §24.1's own resolution of the Phase-4-goal-vs-DoD inconsistency: **locked,** matching PRD's and SD's explicit, word-for-word scope lines. The dashboard screen (pantry + plan + list) renders `Query.pantry`, `Query.menu`, and D4's new `Query.shoppingList` — read-only, no mutations wired from this screen at all this week. The recipes screen wires `createRecipe`/`updateRecipe`/`deleteRecipe` (all already shipped, W6) — including the URL-import and freeform-paste entry paths W7 already built server-side (`importRecipeFromUrl`/`parseFreeformRecipe`), since PRD explicitly calls out desktop as the *easier* place to use them, not a new capability. The settings screen wires `updateHouseholdSettings` (already shipped, W4/W8) for meal structure/cuisine/dietary config.

#### 24.2.6 D6 — Amplify Hosting via `@aws-cdk/aws-amplify-alpha`, version-matched and re-verified against a real synth/deploy before trusting its API shape

`frontend-stack.ts` does not exist yet, and no Amplify-Hosting-specific CDK package is a dependency anywhere in this repo. **Locked:** add `@aws-cdk/aws-amplify-alpha` pinned to the matching alpha version for this repo's already-pinned `aws-cdk-lib@^2.208.0` (alpha packages version-lock to a specific `aws-cdk-lib` minor — confirm the exact compatible alpha version at implementation time, don't guess one into the plan text). `frontend-stack.ts` provisions one `amplify-alpha.App` with a single branch (`dev`/`main`, environment-appropriate) wired to this repo (check what source-control connection method the CDK Amplify construct actually requires — GitHub App connection token vs. a CodeCommit-style source — this is exactly the kind of API-shape detail this decision's own re-verification requirement exists to catch rather than assume), a custom domain mapping to `dev.parimaan.app`/`parimaan.app` (Q7's already-locked domain, `auth-stack.ts`'s own `webDomain` computation reused verbatim, not re-derived), and environment variables for the Next.js app's own non-secret config (the AppSync endpoint URL, the Cognito User Pool id, the web client id — all already-public values other stacks already export via `CfnOutput`, reused directly).

**Explicitly re-verify, don't trust from a doc:** the exact Amplify Hosting L2 construct API (constructor props, branch/build-spec shape, whether SSR Next.js needs an explicit "compute" platform setting distinct from pure static hosting) has had real churn across CDK versions — the implementing slice's first step is a real `cdk synth` against this repo's actual pinned `aws-cdk-lib` version, not an assumption carried over from whatever the alpha package's README showed at some other version. This mirrors D4's own "re-verify the AI model choice with a real call" discipline from W17 — applied here to an infrastructure API shape instead of a model name.

#### 24.2.7 D7 — server-side changes this week are deliberately minimal: one new query field (D4), nothing else

Every mutation the web dashboard needs (`createRecipe`/`updateRecipe`/`deleteRecipe`, `updateHouseholdSettings`, `importRecipeFromUrl`/`parseFreeformRecipe`) already exists, already ships, and needs zero changes — confirmed by direct schema read before locking this plan, not assumed. The only new server-side surface this week is D4's single read query. **Locked, stated explicitly so no implementing slice invents server work that isn't needed:** no new mutations, no new Lambda resolvers beyond `shoppingList`, no changes to any existing resolver's behavior.

### 24.3 Slice breakdown

#### S1 — `frontend-stack` CDK: Amplify Hosting + domain + env wiring

- **Delivers:** D1 (the new Secrets Manager secret for the web client's id/secret, written by `auth-stack.ts`) and D6 (the `frontend-stack.ts` Amplify Hosting construct itself) in full.
- **Files:** `infra/stacks/frontend-stack.ts` (new); `infra/stacks/auth-stack.ts` (the new secret only — no change to the client definitions themselves, per D1); `infra/bin/parimaan.ts` (wires the new stack in, taking `AuthStack.webClient`/the new secret and `ApiStack`'s AppSync endpoint as cross-stack props).
- **Depends on:** nothing code-wise; this is the week's own first slice, the same "infra first, so everything downstream has real resources to target" sequencing W17 S1 used.
- **Size/Risk:** ~3.0 hrs / **High** — the week's highest-risk slice, for the same reason every "first new CDK construct type" slice in this plan has been high-risk (W1's VPC, W17 S1's NAT Gateway): real, potentially-churny external API surface, verified only by actually synthesizing/deploying against it, never by reading documentation alone.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `database-reviewer` (the Secrets Manager secret — same review bar as `parimaan/gemini-api-key`) → `security-reviewer` (mandatory — confirm the web client secret never lands in a CloudFormation template parameter, Amplify console environment variable, or any other plaintext-visible location) → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** `infra/test/frontend-stack.test.ts` asserts the synthesized template contains exactly one Amplify `App`, the expected branch/domain association, and the expected (non-secret) environment variables — and asserts the web client's SECRET never appears anywhere in the synthesized template as a literal string (a real regression test for the security requirement above, not just a synth-succeeds check).
- **Verification:** `cdk synth`/`cdk diff` against real dev, confirming the API shape assumed in §24.2.6 actually holds before any later slice depends on it.

#### S2 — `Query.shoppingList` read resolver

- **Delivers:** D4 in full.
- **Files:** `api/src/resolvers/shoppingList.ts` (new, or wherever this codebase's naming convention puts a bare-query resolver — check `api/src/resolvers/menu.ts`'s own naming for the closest precedent); `shared/schema.graphql` (the new `Query` field).
- **Depends on:** nothing — independent of S1, can and should run in parallel with it.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.0 hr / Low — a small, pure read resolver reusing an existing lookup.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `database-reviewer` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** a household member gets the real list back when one exists; gets `null` (not an error) when none exists yet; a non-member is denied identically to every other list-scoped field's own denial pattern; an unauthenticated caller is denied.

#### S3 — Next.js scaffold + NextAuth + urql wiring

- **Delivers:** D2 in full — the actual Next.js App Router app (`next`, `next-auth`, `urql`, `react`/`react-dom` added to `web/package.json` from the current near-empty scaffold), the Cognito NextAuth provider config, the `jwt`/`session` callbacks persisting the raw ID token (D2), and a urql client instance (one for server components/Route Handlers, one for client components — check whether this needs two distinct client configurations or one that reads the token from context either way) that attaches `Authorization: Bearer <idToken>` on every request. No real dashboard screens yet — this slice proves sign-in works end-to-end and a single authenticated GraphQL call (e.g. `Query.me`) succeeds, nothing more.
- **Files:** `web/package.json`, `web/app/layout.tsx`, `web/app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts` (or wherever this Next.js version's own App Router convention puts the NextAuth handler), a `urql` client module under `web/src/` or `web/lib/`.
- **Depends on:** S1 (needs the real Cognito web client id and a real deployed Amplify app to test sign-in against — though local `next dev` against the real dev Cognito pool can start before the Amplify deploy itself is verified, the callback URL must match what's actually deployed).
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.5 hrs / Medium-High — new client library, new auth flow, real OAuth redirect testing needed (not fully mockable).
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `security-reviewer` (mandatory — confirm the ID token never lands in a location readable by client-side JS beyond what `useSession` deliberately exposes, e.g. never logged, never embedded in a non-httpOnly cookie alongside NextAuth's own) → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** a signed-in session's `useSession`/`getServerSession` result includes a real, non-expired-looking ID token shape; a urql request built from that session carries the correct `Authorization` header; an expired token triggers NextAuth's own refresh path (mock the provider's token endpoint, don't hit real Cognito for this specific test); an unauthenticated page/Route Handler redirects to sign-in rather than rendering with no data.

#### S4 — Dashboard screen (pantry + plan + list, read-only)

- **Delivers:** D3 (household resolution), D5's read-only half — the dashboard screen itself, rendering `Query.pantry`, `Query.menu` (current week), and D4's new `Query.shoppingList`.
- **Files:** `web/app/dashboard/page.tsx` (or this Next.js version's own routing convention) and supporting components under `web/components/`.
- **Depends on:** S2 (the new query), S3 (auth/urql plumbing).
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / Medium.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** renders real pantry/menu/list data for the resolved household; renders an honest empty state for each section independently (a household with no pantry items, no menu yet, no list yet are three independent empty states, not one blanket "nothing here"); never renders a second household's data (membership-gated, same denial pattern as every other screen).

#### S5 — Recipes screen (full CRUD, reusing existing mutations)

- **Delivers:** D5's recipes-edit half — list/detail/create/edit/delete, wired to `createRecipe`/`updateRecipe`/`deleteRecipe`, plus the URL-import and freeform-paste entry paths wired to `importRecipeFromUrl`/`parseFreeformRecipe`.
- **Files:** `web/app/recipes/**` and supporting components.
- **Depends on:** S3. Independent of S4 — can run in parallel with it.
- **Size/Risk:** ~3.0 hrs / Medium — no new server surface, but the most form-heavy screen of the week (recipe editing has the most fields of anything in this schema).
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** create/edit/delete round-trip correctly against the real mutations (Testcontainers-backed API tests already cover the mutations themselves — this screen's own tests are about correct wiring/validation/error display, not re-proving the mutations work); a URL-import/freeform-paste draft can be reviewed and edited before saving, mirroring the mobile app's own AIProposal-review pattern rather than auto-saving a model's raw output; a validation error from the server renders inline, never a silent failure.

#### S6 — Household settings admin screen

- **Delivers:** D5's settings-edit half — meal structure, cuisine regions/bias, dietary/allergens, wired to `updateHouseholdSettings`.
- **Files:** `web/app/settings/**` and supporting components.
- **Depends on:** S3. Independent of S4/S5 — can run in parallel with both.
- **Size/Risk:** ~1.5 hr / Low-Medium — one mutation, already-shipped, already-tested server-side; the work here is form UX, not new logic.
- **Agents:** `tdd-guide` → `code-reviewer`.
- **RED tests:** a partial patch (one field changed) leaves every other field unchanged (the exact `updateHouseholdSettings` partial-patch contract W8/W13 already locked server-side — this screen's own test confirms the web form sends a genuine partial patch, not a full-object overwrite that could silently clobber a field it didn't render a control for).

#### S7 — Real-AWS verification + weekly doc pass

- **Delivers:** live verification, per this plan's own standing convention — a real sign-in against the real deployed `dev.parimaan.app`, exercising all four screens against real dev Aurora/AppSync/Cognito with a real (throwaway) household: dashboard renders real data, a recipe is created/edited/deleted for real, a settings change is saved and confirmed persisted, `Query.shoppingList` is confirmed to return `null` before a list exists and the real list after one is generated (via the mobile/API path, proving the new query reads real data, not just its own test fixtures). §4.2's mandatory weekly pass — actual-vs-planned hours, a decisions-versus-shipped audit of §24.4's locked-decisions table below, and the End-of-Month-4 milestone checklist itself (§4's own row: "curated library + share + web read live").
- **Files:** `docs/E2E_MVP_PLAN.md` (§4 row, a W18-result subsection); `docs/SYSTEM_DESIGN.md` (reconciling its own §9.2 sketch with whatever the implementation actually did differently, the same "don't leave a stale sketch uncorrected" discipline W17's own closing slice just established).
- **Depends on:** all slices.
- **Size/Risk:** ~2.0 hrs / Medium-High, non-optional — higher than a typical week's closing slice because this week's live verification includes a genuinely new kind of claim (a real browser-based OAuth sign-in flow against a real deployed Amplify Hosting app) that no prior week's verification pattern has ever had to exercise.
- **Agents:** `doc-updater`.
- **Verification checks:** the exact list in "Delivers" above, each confirmed against real dev infrastructure, not only component/unit tests; throwaway household/recipe/settings changes cleaned up afterward where this codebase already has a mutation to do so (no new cleanup mechanism invented if one doesn't already exist — name any cleanup gap honestly rather than inventing a workaround).

**Planned total: ~15.0 hrs** — the largest single-week estimate in this plan so far, reflecting genuinely new surface area (an entire second client application) rather than scope creep; for comparison, W17's own planned total was ~11.5 hrs and was itself called out as larger than W16's ~9.0 hrs for the same reason.

### 24.4 Locked decisions (D1–D7)

| # | Decision | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| **D1** | How does the web app get the Cognito web client's secret? | **A new Secrets Manager secret, written by `auth-stack.ts`, fetched at NextAuth cold-start** — reusing `geminiClient.ts`'s own proven fetch-and-memoize pattern. Rejected: Amplify Hosting's compute-role + runtime `DescribeUserPoolClient` call (a real pattern, but a newer/less-exercised corner of the CDK Amplify construct this codebase has no reason to be first to lean on). The Cognito client itself (`webClient`) already exists — this week only consumes it. |
| **D2** | How does urql get a real Cognito JWT for every request, server and client side? | **NextAuth's `jwt`/`session` callbacks persist the raw Cognito ID token** (not just NextAuth's own re-encrypted session) onto the session object both `getServerSession` and `useSession` read. Token refresh reuses NextAuth's own built-in OAuth refresh rotation — no custom refresh logic. |
| **D3** | Does the web dashboard support switching between households? | **No — defaults to `me.households[0]`, no switcher.** Matches mobile's own current no-switcher state exactly; not a regression, a shared, named gap for future work. |
| **D4** | How does the dashboard read a shopping list without triggering `generateShoppingList`'s own `CONFLICT`-on-exists behavior? | **A new `Query.shoppingList(menuId: ID!): ShoppingList` read field** — the one piece of genuinely new server surface this week, returning `null` (not an error) before a list exists. |
| **D5** | What's actually editable on web — read-only per the Phase-4 goal line, or edit-capable per its own DoD bullet? | **Edit-capable, per the DoD/WS-4/SD's matching word-for-word scope** — recipes and settings get real CRUD; pantry and meal-plan stay view-only. The shorter "read-only" Phase-goal phrasing was imprecise, not authoritative, and is named as such rather than silently picked either way. |
| **D6** | Which CDK construct provisions Amplify Hosting, and how much is trusted from documentation vs. re-verified? | **`@aws-cdk/aws-amplify-alpha`, version-matched to this repo's pinned `aws-cdk-lib@^2.208.0`**, with its exact API shape re-verified against a real `cdk synth` before any later slice depends on it — the infrastructure equivalent of W17 D4's "don't trust a model name in a doc, re-verify with a real call." |
| **D7** | Does W18 need any new mutations or resolver behavior changes beyond D4's one new query? | **No.** Every mutation the dashboard needs already exists and already ships unchanged since W4/W6/W7/W8. Stated explicitly so no implementing slice invents unneeded server work. |

**Decision density: 7 locked** — smaller than W17's 8 despite this week's larger total hour estimate, because most of this week's size comes from genuinely new SURFACE AREA (an entire second client, four screens, a new hosting stack) rather than from a high density of hard product/architecture calls — most of the hard calls here (D1/D2/D6) are "how do we wire up something whose shape is already decided elsewhere" questions, closer in kind to execution risk than to fresh decision-making, the same distinction W16's own closing note drew about itself.

### 24.5 S7 result — real-AWS verification (a genuinely new kind of claim, and what could and couldn't be checked from this position) + weekly doc pass

`AWS_PROFILE=parimaan-dev aws sts get-caller-identity` succeeded before anything else (account `917246556431`), so this pass had real deploy access throughout — the same starting position as §22.6/§23.5.

#### The Amplify app's real deployment state, checked first, per this slice's own instruction

`aws amplify get-app --app-id d2pue2qcqjgrvp` and `aws amplify list-branches`/`list-jobs` confirm exactly what §24.2.6 D6's own finding #1 implied would be true: the app (`parimaan-dev-web`, `Platform.WEB_COMPUTE`) and its `dev` branch exist and are correctly configured (the five environment variables S1/the NEXTAUTH_SECRET fix wired — `COGNITO_USER_POOL_ID`, `COGNITO_WEB_CLIENT_ID`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_APPSYNC_GRAPHQL_URL`, `WEB_CLIENT_CREDENTIALS_SECRET_ARN`, `NEXTAUTH_SECRET_ARN` — all present, all ARNs/ids, never a secret value), but `aws amplify get-app --query 'app.repository'` returns `null` and `aws amplify list-jobs --app-id d2pue2qcqjgrvp --branch-name dev` returns an empty `jobSummaries` array. **There has never been a single build or deploy job run against this app.** This is the direct, fully-expected consequence of the GitHub-connection gap named in this slice's own brief and in `frontend-stack.ts`'s own doc comment (finding #1): with no `sourceCodeProvider` configured, there is no connected repository for Amplify to build from, so `dev.parimaan.app` and the default `d2pue2qcqjgrvp.amplifyapp.com` domain currently serve **no running Next.js application at all** — only the CDK-provisioned `App`/`Branch`/`Domain` resources exist, correctly, with nothing built or deployed behind them. `aws amplify list-domain-associations` separately confirms the custom domain itself is still `PENDING_VERIFICATION` (DNS/cert validation not yet done), which would block a browser test even if a build existed.

#### Step 4's real-AWS sign-in test — not reachable, and named plainly rather than rounded up

Given the above, **a real end-to-end browser-based sign-in against `dev.parimaan.app` or the Amplify default domain was not attempted, because there is nothing there to sign into.** Separately and independently, this agent has no interactive browser/OAuth-redirect capability from this position in any case — the same honesty bar §21.5/§22.5 have already applied to a claim this shape can't actually exercise. Both facts are named together because either one alone would already make step 4 unreachable; the GitHub-connection gap is the blocking one, and a human completing that gap would still leave "drive a real OAuth redirect through a browser" as a claim this agent cannot make for itself even once a build exists — a human (or a browser-automation tool this session was not given) would need to perform that specific check.

#### What WAS verified from this position

1. **`cdk diff` against real, deployed `Parimaan-dev-Network`/`Parimaan-dev-Api`/`Parimaan-dev-Auth`/`Parimaan-dev-Frontend`**, using the real Google OAuth client id read directly from the deployed Cognito identity provider (`aws cognito-idp describe-identity-provider`, client ids are not sensitive, same precedent as §22.6). All four target stacks showed **zero differences** — merged `main` matches what's actually deployed exactly. `Parimaan-dev-Data` was pulled in automatically as a dependency stack (same `cdk diff`/`cdk deploy` default behavior §23.5 already named) and showed only `MigrationRunnerFn`'s code-asset hash changing — a bundler-artifact difference, not a real code change (no W18 PR touches any migration file).
2. **`Query.shoppingList` re-confirmed live, end to end**, via direct Lambda invokes (the same synthetic-Cognito-identity pattern `createHousehold.test.ts`'s own `buildEvent` establishes, used by every prior week's verification pass): a throwaway household (`38addd90-b2f5-44e9-8f32-0087209b9ea9`) → a real menu (`b8420597-29ab-4a45-bcd3-8b08dead7c45`) → `Query.shoppingList(menuId)` returned **`null`** before generation → `generateShoppingList(menuId)` returned a real list (`564e1751-2efb-4bfc-8b15-2d9940f61488`), completing fast with no timeout (directly confirming W17's NAT/Lambda-endpoint fix — §23.5's addendum — still holds) → `Query.shoppingList(menuId)` immediately after returned the **identical `id`**, not a second/different list. The throwaway household was deleted afterward via a real `deleteHousehold` invoke (returned `true`).
3. **`next build` against a clean `main` checkout** (`web/`, with dummy env vars standing in for the real Secrets-Manager-fetched values, which `next build` itself never calls out to): succeeded cleanly, producing all four expected routes (`/`, `/dashboard`, `/recipes`, `/recipes/[id]`, `/recipes/[id]/edit`, `/recipes/new`, `/settings`, plus the NextAuth route handler) with no build errors.
4. **Full web test suite, clean `main` checkout**: `pnpm test` in `web/` — **28 test files, 101 tests, all passed** (2.27s).
5. **Full API test suite, clean `main` checkout, Testcontainers/Docker**: `pnpm test` in `api/` — **138 test files, 1527 tests, all passed** (110s).
6. **Full infra test suite, clean `main` checkout**: `pnpm test` in `infra/` — **9 test files, 212 tests, all passed** (123s), including `test/api-stack.exportShoppingListImage.test.ts`'s own CDK-synth-based assertions (unrelated to W18, confirmed unregressed) and the new `frontend-stack.test.ts` assertions from S1.
7. **`pnpm run validate:recipes` in `api/`**: **17 tests, all passed**; direct `find`/`wc -l` confirms 30 north + 23 south = **53**, matching every prior week's confirmed count, unregressed.
8. **Direct AWS CLI existence/shape checks on every piece of new infrastructure**, values never printed: `aws secretsmanager describe-secret` confirms both `parimaan/web-client-credentials` (owned by `Parimaan-dev-Auth`, per D1) and `parimaan/nextauth-secret-dev` (owned by `Parimaan-dev-Frontend`, per the same-day NEXTAUTH_SECRET fix) exist with the expected descriptions and tags. `aws iam get-role-policy` on the Amplify app's service role (`Parimaan-dev-Frontend-AppRole...`) confirms a single inline policy granting `secretsmanager:DescribeSecret`/`GetSecretValue` scoped to **exactly those two secret ARNs** — not a wildcard, not any other secret in the account. (The separate auto-created compute role carries no inline policies of its own; the grant lands on the app's service role instead — `frontend-stack.ts`'s `grantRead(this.app)` calls resolve to `App.grantPrincipal`, and this is where CDK attached the resulting policy. Not a discrepancy from what D1/S1 intended — the secrets are reachable by the app exactly as designed — but worth naming for a future reader who goes looking for the policy on the compute role specifically and doesn't find it there.)
9. **Direct code reads of every D1–D7 claim**, not just PR descriptions — see the audit table below.

#### Decisions-versus-shipped audit (§24.4 D1–D7)

| # | Decision | Shipped as decided? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Web client secret delivered via a new Secrets Manager secret written by `auth-stack.ts`, fetched at NextAuth cold-start | **Yes.** | `infra/stacks/auth-stack.ts` read directly: `this.webClientCredentialsSecret = this.createWebClientCredentialsSecret()` builds a `Secret` holding `clientId`/`clientSecret` (the latter `this.webClient.userPoolClientSecret`, never a literal). `web/src/auth/secrets.ts` fetches it at cold start, memoized — confirmed live via `aws secretsmanager describe-secret` (check 8 above) and via the Amplify app's own `WEB_CLIENT_CREDENTIALS_SECRET_ARN` env var (an ARN, never the value). No `DescribeUserPoolClient` runtime call anywhere in the diff — the rejected alternative was genuinely not built. |
| D2 | NextAuth's `jwt`/`session` callbacks persist the raw Cognito ID token; no custom refresh logic | **Yes.** | `web/src/auth/callbacks.ts` read directly: `jwtCallback` persists `idToken`/`refreshToken`/`accessTokenExpiresAt` on first sign-in (`account` present), and on every later call either passes the token through while valid or calls `refreshAccessToken` — reusing NextAuth's own refresh-token OAuth shape, not a hand-rolled one. `sessionCallback` surfaces only `idToken`/`error` onto the session, deliberately omitting `refreshToken` from client-visible state. `web/src/graphql/authHeader.ts`'s `buildAuthorizationHeaders` confirms the bare-token-no-`Bearer`-prefix convention, explicitly cross-checked against `mobile/lib/shared/graphql/auth_link.dart`'s own doc comment rather than assumed. |
| D3 | Dashboard defaults to `me.households[0]`, no switcher | **Yes, but with a known, still-open minor duplication.** | Confirmed: no household-switcher UI exists anywhere under `web/app/`. But the "resolve the active household" logic exists as **three separate functions** — `web/src/dashboard/resolvePrimaryHousehold.ts`, `web/src/household/resolveHousehold.ts`, `web/src/graphql/resolveHouseholdId.ts` (22/20/33 lines respectively, confirmed present via direct `find`/`wc -l`) — a real, explicitly-flagged-during-the-S6-merge duplication that was deliberately left unconsolidated rather than risk a late, unreviewed refactor across three already-merged screens. Still present, unconsolidated, as of this pass — named here again rather than silently dropped from tracking. |
| D4 | New `Query.shoppingList(menuId: ID!): ShoppingList` read field, returns `null` before a list exists | **Yes.** | `api/src/resolvers/shoppingList.ts` read directly: reuses `findShoppingListByMenu` (the identical lookup `generateShoppingList.ts` already performs), the identical `requireHouseholdMember`/`ForbiddenError`/`DENIAL_MESSAGE` denial pattern every other list-scoped field uses, and returns `null` (not a thrown error) when no list exists. Live-reconfirmed end to end — see check 2 above — the single most important functional re-verification this pass, since D4 is this week's only new server surface. |
| D5 | Recipes and settings get real edit capability; pantry/meal-plan stay view-only | **Yes.** | `web/app/dashboard/` renders `Query.pantry`/`Query.menu`/`Query.shoppingList` with no mutation wired anywhere in that screen (confirmed via `grep` — zero mutation imports in `app/dashboard/`). `web/src/recipes/buildRecipePatch.ts` and `web/src/settings/buildSettingsPatch.ts` both read directly: genuine field-level diffing, not full-object overwrites — `buildSettingsPatch.test.ts`'s own assertion (`expect(Object.keys(patch)).toEqual(['mealsEnabled'])` for a single-field change) directly proves the partial-patch contract the plan itself required. |
| D6 | `@aws-cdk/aws-amplify-alpha`, version-matched, API shape re-verified against a real synth before trusting it | **Yes — and the three named findings hold up under direct inspection, not just the PR's own claim.** | `infra/stacks/frontend-stack.ts`'s class doc comment names three findings (no GitHub App connection option — only PAT-based `ISourceCodeProvider`; `Platform.WEB_COMPUTE` required explicitly, the default `Platform.WEB` being static-only; domain association as its own `Domain` construct, mapped onto the branch via `mapRoot`/`mapSubDomain`). All three independently confirmed against the real deployed resources this pass: `aws amplify get-app` shows `"platform": "WEB_COMPUTE"`; `aws amplify get-app --query 'app.repository'` returns `null` (finding #1, confirmed — and the direct cause of this week's biggest reachable-verification gap, see above); `aws amplify list-domain-associations` shows a real, separate `domainAssociationArn` for `dev.parimaan.app` distinct from the `App`/`Branch` resources. `pnpm install` in `infra/` resolves `@aws-cdk/aws-amplify-alpha@2.208.0-alpha.0` against `aws-cdk-lib@2.265.0` with no peer-dependency conflict, and `cdk diff` (check 1 above) synthesizes and diffs cleanly against the real stack. |
| D7 | No new mutations or resolver behavior changes beyond D4's one new query | **Yes.** | `git diff` of `shared/schema.graphql` between the plan-lock commit and `HEAD` shows exactly one new field added — `shoppingList(menuId: ID!): ShoppingList` — and zero other schema changes. No existing resolver's `.ts` file shows behavior changes outside what S1-S6's own "Delivers" lines already named. |

**All seven decisions shipped as locked.** D3 carries the one already-known, still-open minor gap (three duplicate household-resolution helpers, not consolidated this pass per this slice's own explicit scope — consolidating it belongs to its own TDD-pipeline slice, not bundled into a docs-and-verification pass). D6's three findings — the week's most "re-verify, don't trust" decision — all independently confirmed against live AWS state, not only read from the PR description, and directly explain why step 4 of this pass's own verification scope (a real browser sign-in) could not be exercised: finding #1 (no GitHub App connection option) is the concrete, load-bearing reason the Amplify app has zero build jobs and therefore nothing a browser could sign into yet.

**Rough total actual (plan lock through the NEXTAUTH_SECRET fix): ~2.51 hr against those seven items' planned ~13.0 hr** (3.0 + 1.0 + 2.5 + 2.0 + 3.0 + 1.5, the NEXTAUTH_SECRET fix itself having no separate planned-hours line since it wasn't in §24.3's own slice breakdown) — under, continuing the same direction as every week since W12, and proportionally one of the widest gaps yet measured, consistent with this week's own S1-S6 slices shipping in sequence with no blocking rework.

**W18 closes here, shipped mostly as planned, with one real and clearly-bounded gap.** All four screens (sign-in, dashboard, recipes, settings) exist in the merged code, build cleanly from a clean `main` checkout, and pass their own test suites; D4's new `Query.shoppingList` field is live-reconfirmed end to end, returning `null` before generation and the real list afterward; the NEXTAUTH_SECRET gap S3 itself flagged forward was found and fixed the same day (§24's own intro, and this section's D1/D6 audit). **What remains open, honestly:** the Amplify app has zero connected repository and zero build jobs — `dev.parimaan.app` and the default Amplify domain currently serve no running application at all, a direct and fully-expected consequence of the CDK Amplify construct's own documented GitHub-App-connection gap (`frontend-stack.ts` finding #1), not a new defect found this pass. A human still needs to create a GitHub PAT, store it in Secrets Manager, and supply it via CDK context before the app can build real code — and, separately, a real browser-based OAuth sign-in against the deployed app (this pass's own step-4 instruction) is not a claim this agent could make for itself even once that build exists, since this session has no interactive browser/OAuth-redirect capability; that specific check needs either a human or a browser-automation tool this session was not given. The three-way household-resolution-helper duplication (D3) remains a known, still-unconsolidated minor gap, named again rather than dropped from tracking. Every other piece of real-AWS verification reachable from this position — `cdk diff` against all four target stacks (zero unexpected drift), the shopping-list read/write round trip, the full web/API/infra test suites on clean `main`, and direct AWS CLI existence/shape checks on both new secrets and the IAM grant scoping them — was done and is recorded above.
